# Redkiln-RS — architecture design

**Date:** 2026-09-08 · **Status:** **The authoritative architecture design.** It supersedes `2026-09-05-architecture-proposal.md` at revision 4 in full; where the two disagree, this document governs and the proposal is history. **It is not "design final".** Three spikes gate that label — **S-BIN**, **S-H1c** and **P8's unrun arms** — and P8 has no result (F38): 3 draws of a pre-registered 27+3, its 65-minute and 24-hour arms and all three no-kill controls unrun, and its third criterion circular by construction. Phase 1 may start on the DECIDED rows of §0.1; nothing may be declared final. · **Supersedes:** `2026-09-05-architecture-proposal.md` (rev. 4, 2026-09-07) in full; it remains citable as the source of the retrofitted decisions ADR-0001–ADR-0073 and as the richest record of concrete types, tables and contracts, but no claim may be carried from it without a check against the evidence brief. · **Companions:** `references/adr/` (89 records, ADR-0001–ADR-0089; 86 accepted, 2 superseded, 1 proposed — **ADR-0089**, accepted 2026-09-09, partially supersedes ADR-0030, which stays `accepted` and now carries `superseded_by: ["0089"]`) · `references/2026-09-08-evidence-augmentation.md` (external research pass) · `references/2026-09-07-process-catalog-proposal.md` · `references/2026-09-07-activity-graph-schema.md` · the thirteen acceptance-criterion area files (AC-001–AC-519 **plus AC-520**, the derived-facet gate added 2026-09-09; SM-001–SM-156 **plus SM-157**, derived-facet precision, added the same day — both ids sit outside their area's exhausted range deliberately, and the registers record why). · **Inputs:** the condensed evidence brief of 2026-09-08 (F1–F38; no F9, F30 unassigned) over `spikes/FINDINGS.md`, `spikes/F29-DRAFT.md`–`F38-DRAFT.md`, plus **`spikes/F39-DRAFT.md`** (P1 arm D scored 2026-09-09, postdating the brief) and `references/2026-09-09-change-classification.md`, `spikes/DECISIONS-2026-09-09.md`, `spikes/METHOD*.md`, `spikes/HANDOFF.md`; the S-MIG census at corpus pin `47faec9f`; the external research pass of 2026-09-08 (48 confirmations, 13 contradictions, 44 new findings, 30 unresolved); Claude Code, Claude platform, gitoxide, crates.io and competitor documentation as fetched 2026-09-08. · **Evidence discipline:** every load-bearing claim carries a finding number or a source; where a claim rests on one draw, one machine or one operator, it says so. Claims are tagged **[measured]** / **[documented]** / **[inferred]**. A saturated metric is reported as saturated, a powerless criterion as powerless, and a ground truth known to be wrong is never used as a denominator. Every unknown is discharged as a named spike with a named consumer (ADR-0012), never as prose.

---

## 0. The design in one paragraph

Build Redkiln-RS as a **clean-break, single Rust binary that owns the loop**: a **deterministic kernel** — stage selection, gate evaluation, budgets, retry, replay — that holds no non-determinism of its own and dispatches **one harness process per activity**, every one of them a child with its own card, schema, tool set, MCP config and cost record; durable state written as **git trailers first and an append-only event log second**, split by the test *if the database were deleted, would `redkiln run` take a different next action?*; **human gates as durable marks** — a `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` record, a `Gate:` trailer and a process that exits 0, never a process that waits; and **every agent output a typed value** validated at the boundary, of which markdown is a rendering and never a source. The kernel is hexagonal along five named variation axes — event store, harness, tracker, docs site, toolchain — each a **sync** trait with one real adapter and one fake, with every `.await` confined to an adapter behind an explicit cancel (ADR-0076); git is deliberately not a port. What separates this revision from the 2026-09-05 proposal at revision 4 is that the P-series and the research pass moved the line between built and blocked in **both** directions: P6 collapsed the lattice's unsatisfiability from 23.5% to 0.3% and left zero rank ties and zero hard cycles (F35) while P9/P9b/P6 showed the activity-graph schema is authorable at a median 53–73 non-blank lines against a >150 kill (F32/F33/F34), so the catalog, the schema and the selection engine's *semantics* are now decided and buildable; and against that, P1's kill fired 4 of 4 on the selection engine's *input* (F36) and arm D's scoring then found the **derived** half of that input measurably wrong rather than merely unstable — no facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar, `auth` scores **0.00** [0.00, 0.26] over eleven positives every one of which is false, and the bar itself is unreachable at n=20 (F39; ADR-0089) — P7's primary criterion proved powerless at n=3 rather than negative (F37), P8 returned no result on its own question (F38), and the research pass withdrew two facts the proposal leaned on — gix's `GIT_COMMON_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` interplay and its trailer-write capability (ADR-0077) and the single-variable TTL fix (ADR-0078). The design is therefore decided in more places than the proposal was, and final in fewer.

---

## 0.1 What is decided, and what is blocked

Thirty-eight areas. **Twenty-five DECIDED, four PROVISIONAL, nine BLOCKED.** The ninth blocked row is new as of 2026-09-09: F39 scored the *derived* facet source, which AC-071's block never covered, and ADR-0089 records what follows. Blocked rows are listed first because they are the ones a reader must not miss; nothing later in this document resolves one of them in prose.

| Area | State | The evidence | What unblocks it | Gates Phase 1? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Selection engine — the context-vector source** | **BLOCKED** (ADR-0079, status `proposed` — the only non-accepted record in the set other than two full supersessions) | P1's kill fired **4 of 4**: the modal `R(stage, ctx)` was identical across ten draws on **5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20** items against an 80% line; the prescribed anchor-rewrite repair did not work; `external_dep` collapsed to a constant (**200/200 false**); three axes with **byte-identical** anchors drifted up to **−0.168**, larger than the effect the alpha<0.6 rule exists to detect, so that rule is **not a decidable predicate on this corpus**; `unknown` rates novelty 46.0%, size 39.5%, blast_radius 31.0%, criticality 27.0%. [measured, F36; 400 dispatches, 0 void, $21.37] **Arm D is now SCORED** and its answer is not a threshold miss: over **140 truth cells** at pin `47faec9f`, **no derived facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar** — `ui` **1.00** [0.65, 1.00] recall 0.78, `toolchain` 0.73 [0.48, 0.89], `change` 0.72 [0.54, 0.85], `binary_change` 0.60 [0.23, 0.88], `flag_exists` 0.00 [0.00, 0.66], **`auth` 0.00 [0.00, 0.26] over eleven positives, every one false**, and `data_migration` **NOT MEASURABLE** (0 positives across all 20 items and all three sources — the criterion has no denominator). The ≥ 0.95 clause is **not reachable at n = 20 for any facet however good**: `ui` is 7/7 and its lower bound is 0.65, and roughly **60** positives are needed for a lower bound above 0.95 — the same class of defect as P1's alpha rule (F36) and P7's criterion 1 (F37) [measured, F39; 140 cells, **$0** model cost, **panel-adjudicated** under METHOD-P amendment 9, never human-panel]. **F39 discharges the fourth of AC-071's five clauses and no other** — the arm-D panel is scored — and **the block still stands on the first**: F36 measured modal required-set identity at **0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10** against the 0.80 line. F39 measures a *derived* vector; ADR-0079 asks about an *LLM-emitted* one, so it narrows 0079's options without deciding among them and **ADR-0079 stays `proposed`**. Externally corroborated across five 2026 frontier models, with Claude-Sonnet-4 measured at background temperature 0 — the instability is schema-induced, not decoding noise [preprint, arXiv 2603.04417 / 2604.22411] | **AC-071's five clauses, in full and in one place**: identity ≥ 0.80, n ≥ 20 items, ≥ 10 draws per item, arm-D panel scored, corpus pinned. **The fourth is DISCHARGED** — the panel was adjudicated and arm D scored on 2026-09-09 over 140 truth cells at $0 model cost [measured, F39] — and clauses 2, 3 and 5 were met by arm L's own record (20 items × 10 draws at a pin). **What remains is clause 1 alone:** a P1 re-run whose modal `R(stage, ctx)` identity reaches **0.80**, against the **0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10** measured [F36] — §4.9 and §10.2 exit (6) state the same condition and no other. A temperature spike is explicitly not the answer | **YES** — Phase 1 may not author a `require` row keyed on `criticality`, `novelty`, `size`, `blast_radius`, `market_facing`, `external_dep` or `has_repro` sourced from an LLM classification dispatch. `kind` is out of scope of the block (human input, ADR-0030 rule 4). The engine's **semantics** are decided and buildable now |
| **Selection engine — the DERIVED-facet source** | **BLOCKED** (**ADR-0089**, accepted 2026-09-09; it partially supersedes ADR-0030, which stays `accepted` and now carries `superseded_by: ["0089"]`) | **AC-071's block reaches `source = "llm"` only, so the semantic facets — declared `source = "derived"` — ran outside it entirely, and they are the ones measurably wrong.** The deriver is an unanchored, case-insensitive regex over file paths **and diff lines**. Scored against **140 truth cells** at pin `47faec9f`: **`auth` 0.00 [0.00, 0.26] — eleven positives, eleven false, so the rule carries NO SIGNAL on this corpus**; `flag_exists` 0.00 [0.00, 0.66]; `binary_change` 0.60 [0.23, 0.88] recall 1.00; `change` 0.72 [0.54, 0.85]; `toolchain` 0.73 [0.48, 0.89]; `ui` 1.00 [0.65, 1.00]; `data_migration` **NOT MEASURABLE**. The collision is **correlated, not random** — it is with the vocabulary of the instrument under test: `auth` fired 43 times, **40 of them inside *authored* / *author* / *authors* / *authoring* / *report-md-unauthored* / *only-the-author-can-advance***; `token` 44 times, **36 inside a design token** (`brand-token-substrate`, `brand-tokens.test.ts`, `redkiln.tokens.json`, `tokens_out`); `oauth` 8 times, **all 8 inside *coauthor*** (the string `oauth` sits inside `c-oauth-or`); `session` 8, six of them a *work* session; `role` 2, both an ARIA attribute. And **4 of the 20 items changed no code whatsoever** — every changed file is `.bklg/` bookkeeping or telemetry — the panel returned `unknown` on **all 16** of their semantic-facet cells and the script emitted a confident boolean on every one. [measured, F39; **$0** model cost, reported **PANEL-ADJUDICATED** under METHOD-P amendment 9 and never as human-panel — 60 cells a deterministic oracle over git metadata, 39 unanimous panel, 9 unanimous with the adversarial flag mechanically moot, and **32 human adjudication, Ryan blind, of which 15 of 32 differ from the script**] | **S-FACET** (§10.5), pre-registered in `references/2026-09-09-change-classification.md` §6 with four arms — control, anchored-paths-only, `surfaces.toml` declared boundaries, declared + call-site — and a kill criterion whose either outcome is a legitimate result. Named consumer: `facets.toml`'s `source`/`deriver` fields. **Source asymmetry is a separate, untaken decision registered as an arm of it**: `commits` beats `docs` on every facet (`toolchain` **1.00** [0.70, 1.00] vs **0.50** [0.22, 0.78]; `change` **0.80** [0.61, 0.91] vs **0.50** [0.24, 0.76]; `binary_change` finds nothing at all from `docs` — recall 0.00, 3 missed) and **`union` is worse than `commits` alone** on `toolchain` and `change`, because unioning a precise source with an imprecise one imports the imprecision [measured, F39 §3]. ADR-0089 does not decide it and does not touch ADR-0079 | **YES** — no `require` row or `applicable_if` may be keyed on `auth`, `ui`, `flag_exists` or `data_migration` as computed by the lexical deriver. ADR-0089 withdraws the repo-wide `auth` boolean outright and replaces it with a **set-valued `surfaces` facet** over a checked-in `surfaces.toml` path-glob map (set-valued is precedented by `toolchain` and `change`), which is **unscored until S-FACET runs** and may therefore be authored and linted but may **not** key a `require` row on ADR-0089's strength alone. **No** for the engine, whose semantics are settled. Two of ADR-0089's three parts ship in Phase 1: `facets.toml`'s `source` gains a companion **`deriver`** field and no facet may declare `source = "derived"` without a scored record for that deriver; and **boolean facets become tri-valued — absence of evidence is `null`, never `false`** — routed to AC-079's existing `gate_opened { reason: unknown_facet }`, which ADR-0067's `recent_deploy` already does under `R-recent-deploy-feed-absent`, so the pattern is generalised rather than invented |
| **`ChangeSummary` type design** | **BLOCKED** (fixture pinned 2026-09-09; spike still unrun) | **S-BIN registered and UNRUN — but no longer unplanned.** The fixture is pinned and its five items and two seeds are registered (METHOD-S §7, 2026-09-09), and reading the real project already found three places the draft type does not fit what it emits: under LFS `--numstat` reports an asset change as a **pointer-line diff** — `1 1` or `2 2` — and never the binary marker, so `text_diff` cannot be decided by asking git; `tests` has no field for `succeededWithWarnings`, which is **180 of 1029** on the project's own last run; and `captures` does have a producer (UE's automation screenshot comparison), unexercised. Those are inputs to the spike and **fix nothing about the type** [measured, METHOD-S §7 amendment 2026-09-09]. **The seeds' greenness, which §3.2 requires verified before dispatch, has NOT been run and may retire either seed.** No review evidence of any kind exists for items whose change is not a text diff [documented; ADR-0008, ADR-0012] | S-BIN on the real Unreal project: 5 items whose change is asset manifest + automation report + captures, no diff; can the reviewer verify ACs and catch a seeded regression | **YES**, explicitly — the brief registers S-BIN as gating Phase 1 for the `ChangeSummary` type. The five toolchain-keyed kinds (ADR-0073) cannot be finalised without it |
| **Card-delivery append route** | **BLOCKED** | **S-H1c registered and UNRUN**; ADR-0016's delivery clause is conditional on it [documented; ADR-0016, ADR-0078] | S-H1c — measure the append route against the static `--system-prompt` baseline | **No** for the Phase-1 default: static `--system-prompt` ships (ADR-0016, untouched by ADR-0078's partial supersession). **The append route may not be enabled as anything but an unmeasured config option until S-H1c runs** |
| **The quality shorthand default** | **BLOCKED** (ADR-0080 leaves it explicitly unset) | P7's primary criterion is **powerless at n=3** — every Wilson interval overlaps by construction. The `adversary → policy` block cost **2.64×** ($2.69 vs $1.02/run) and its policy check was **false on 33 of 33**. The frequently-quoted "changed the root cause on 1 item in 10" is **the powerless criterion's own output, not a result** — one cell (`issue-81`, 0/3 vs 1/3) moved by one draw of three, with nothing excluding 0 and nothing excluding much more (F37 §2); deviation was offered 71 times and used 0. [measured, F37; 61 runs, 297 records, 0 void, $131.62] The evidence half works: 188 findings, **78% required running something**. **Instrument confound, carried with every P7 figure:** the CLI moved from 2.1.263 to **2.1.265 partway through P7**, catching exactly `recommended`'s r3 draw on all ten items while `optional` ran wholly on 2.1.263 — no effect is detectable in F37 §7's per-repeat split, and **the arms are still not instrument-matched** | A re-run at **n = 10 per cell** plus a settled severity vocabulary. The mandatory arm's cost ratio is additionally unreconciled — 3.66× in F37, 4.4× in the decisions log | **No** — Phase 1 authors each quality block explicitly per activity. **No default may be set from the P7 run**, and the forbidden predicate `findings.none(evidence in [Reproduced, MutationSurvived])` may not be used as a terminal condition (ADR-0080) |
| **"Design final" as a whole** | **BLOCKED** | **P8 has no result.** 3 draws of a pre-registered 27+3, $3.01 of a $40 cap. Its 65-minute and 24-hour arms — the ones that straddle F10's ~1 h TTL — and **all three no-kill controls are unrun**; its third criterion is **circular by construction** (the mark was minted by the driver that implements the semantics under test); and its §5.2 fixture design failed outright — "remove the repro steps so the gate fires" does not work against an agent holding `Read` and `Bash`, so every gate arrival was forced and the arrival rate is entirely unmeasured. [measured/PARTIAL, F38; ADR-0087] | P8's 65-minute and 24-hour arms and its three controls, run against a gate that fires on its own condition and a kernel that mints the mark independently of the driver; plus S-BIN and S-H1c | **No — it gates the label, not the build.** Phase 1 starts on the DECIDED rows. No document, section or commit message may describe this design as final |
| **Git write path — the common-dir interplay** | **BLOCKED**; hybrid adopted meanwhile (ADR-0077) | gitoxide's own `crate-status.md` states that "the delicate interplay between `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` isn't implemented" — **precisely the mechanism the store design leans on**, since one store per repository lives at `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`/redkiln/ and is shared by every worktree. A named gap in first-party docs, not a hypothesis. [documented, 1P GitoxideLabs/gitoxide, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 2, severity **high**] | **S-GIX**, run as a go/no-go against the exactly-pinned `gix = "=0.87.1"` | **No** — the hybrid ships: gix for `rev-parse`, status, shallow clone by SHA and trailer *parsing*; `git worktree add/remove` shells out. **gix may not own the write path until S-GIX passes** |
| **Git trailer *writing*** | **BLOCKED**; hybrid adopted meanwhile (ADR-0077) | `crate-status.md` documents trailer **parsing**; no mature first-class trailer-**writing** API comparable to `git interpret-trailers --in-place` was found, and the agent could not fetch a definitive docs.rs API read either way — **UNVERIFIED absence, not a proven one**. Git trailers are **tier-1 state**, so this is load-bearing. [documented, 1P gitoxide `crate-status.md`; research pass §1 row 8 and §5, named there as "the highest-value single check in the list"] | **S-GIX** — a docs.rs API read plus a five-line unit test against the pinned version | **No** — the write path shells out to `git interpret-trailers --in-place` / `git commit --trailer` and that route is decided and unblocked. What is blocked is any claim that gix can own it |
| **Cache-TTL verification** | **BLOCKED**; the rule itself is decided (ADR-0078) | The documented `ephemeral_1h_*` / `ephemeral_5m_*` usage fields — the only per-run evidence that would verify a TTL — were **MEASURED ABSENT**: `usage` uses plain cache naming and exposes no TTL split, which made charter E1's split metric untestable. [measured, F3] The platform docs describe a **two-bucket** TTL (main conversation vs subagents/workflows/forks), a layered precedence chain, and those exact fields as the verification surface, with subagent traffic defaulting to **5m regardless of billing type**. [documented, 1P code.claude.com/prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08] The measurement and the documentation are in direct contradiction | **S-TTL1** — reconcile the documented fields against a recorded real stream at the pinned CLI version (v2.1.242+ is required for the setting and env var at all), recording billing mode as a confound | **No** — but **no run may be reported as 1 h without per-run `ephemeral_*` field evidence** (ADR-0078), and both `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` and `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` are set in the runner env |
| Mid-graph gate placement and resume cost | **PROVISIONAL** (ADR-0087) | Mid-graph resume was lossless across a real `taskkill /T /F` — **0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical** — and cost **0.45× / 0.55× / 0.78×** of ~18.6k against a >1.5× kill. [measured, F38; n=3] But all three runs sat **inside** F10's ~1 h TTL (measured `gap_minutes` 0.9, 28.4, 29.1, notwithstanding the `0-delay` arm label), there is **no within-experiment baseline** because the controls are unrun, and the comparison is only to F28 | P8's unrun arms and controls | No — the decision is safe under either answer for placement, but **"cheaper than a stage gate" may not be stated without n=3 and the missing baseline named in the same sentence** |
| Review-gate calibration against **false approval** | **PROVISIONAL** (ADR-0088; the arm is registered and **unrun**) | Every false-positive figure this programme holds measures false *rejection*. The mirror risk is documented and large: framing a change as already reviewed and approved reduces vulnerability detection by **16–93%**, strongly asymmetric toward false negatives. [preprint, arXiv 2603.18740] CLEAN is an observational instance of the framing, never a controlled arm | The S-SUBTLE "presented as already approved" arm, paired per item, sized before it runs (at n=5 with detection at ceiling only a large effect is observable) | No — but **until it runs no section may claim the review gate is guarded against false approval**, and no S-VAR or S-TYPED false-positive rate may be presented as evidence of overall gate calibration |
| False-positive rates and MCCs from E3 | **DECIDED** (was PROVISIONAL; resolved 2026-09-09) | **RESOLVED 2026-09-09.** `spikes/results/e3-severity-annotation-ryan.md` is filled and was found during the Track-A walkthrough: **4 BLOCKING, 2 ADVISORY**, including a documented self-correction on finding 4 (originally annotated BLOCKING on a claim that was false). It settles to arm (a) FP **1/23**, MCC **+0.978**; arm (b) FP **0/23**, MCC **+1.000**. The direction is unchanged — headless is not worse — and the gap is one item, which McNemar cannot separate from chance, so **quality still cannot discriminate the two arms and the entire reliable difference remains the 1.72× cost**. This document's earlier rows called the sheet "returned unfilled"; that was stale. **The sheet is dated 2026-09-04 and is NOT BLIND by its own header** — "Filled by the model at Ryan's direction… NOT BLIND", with κ against an independent human annotation **forfeited**, because the same model produced `e3-severity-annotation-model.md` and this sheet and the two are not independent raters [documented, the sheet's own header]. A blind annotation of the preserved `e3-severity-annotation-BLANK.md` is what would buy κ back, which is why E3-SEV stays named as the open route. **CLEAN remains unusable as a denominator** — its ground truth is wrong for ≥13 of 23 — so these rates are still upper bounds, and that limit is what actually outlives the adjudication. [measured, F24 + the filled annotation sheet] Separately, **CLEAN's ground truth is wrong for ≥13 of 23 (57%)**, so every false-positive rate measured against it is an **UPPER BOUND** and CLEAN may never be used as a denominator | Ryan's severity adjudication, plus a settled severity vocabulary | No — the gate's design does not depend on these numbers; only claims about its calibration do |
| Distribution and the plugin surface | **PROVISIONAL** | Proposal §4.9 is three sentences and **no ADR carries it**. Every distribution finding is `[documented]` from *shipmates'* issue history — one project, one maintainer, one release series. Redkiln-RS has shipped no binary and has **no distribution baseline of its own**; AC-480..AC-519 are borrowed-failure criteria and every measure in the area starts with "no baseline" | A first release, or a decision recorded as an ADR of its own | No — Phase 1 ships no plugin; `rk-plugin-gen` is Phase 2 |
| Architecture shape — clean-break single binary owning the loop | **DECIDED** | **No difference was detectable, and the instrument could not have detected one**: detection **23/23 vs 23/23**, **McNemar p=0.625** (no pre-declared margin, no power — a tie at ceiling is neither equivalence nor "not worse"), in-session **1.72×** the cost ($97.39 vs $56.47) — the primary metric **saturated**, so the result is "no discriminating power", not "headless wins on quality" [measured, F23]; **20/20 in all four cells** across two harder tiers, blind and sighted — **saturated, so the harder tier separated nothing either; four cells at ceiling confirm no between-arm difference** [measured, F26; §1.2]. ADR-0001, ADR-0002 | — | It **is** Phase 1 |
| Kernel — workflow/activity split, replay, memo, budgets | **DECIDED** | Suspension is lossless **174/174** across a process boundary [measured, F19], and mid-graph state survived a real kill **3/3** [measured, F38]. **Stated limit: the FSM was never replayed**, so "same next action" is unproven and ships as a kernel replay test, and P8's state holds exactly one step. Content-addressed, loop-aware memo key. ADR-0021, ADR-0022, ADR-0086 | — | Yes — it is the Phase-1 core |
| No durable-execution framework | **DECIDED**, re-argued on facts that hold | The proposal's two supporting facts are **false as of 2026**: `temporalio-sdk` on `temporalio-sdk-core` v0.5.0 is a live Public Preview, not a 2021 alpha, and `graph-flow` makes postgres an optional feature with an in-memory session store. The exclusion survives on three surviving facts: Temporal's Rust SDK still mandates a server; graph-flow's in-memory mode does not survive a process restart and so is not durable; `iopsystems/durable` requires Postgres **and** WASM-component compilation. [documented, 1P crates.io / GitHub, fetched 2026-09-08] ADR-0086 | — | Yes — the memo table is built here |
| Five named ports, sync traits, async in adapters | **DECIDED** | Native async-fn-in-trait is **still not dyn-compatible**; `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` remains nightly-only. "No generics leak into the core" and "dyn-dispatched port" are in direct tension the moment a port method is async, so port traits are sync, every `.await` is in an adapter, and cancellation is an explicit port operation implemented as a real process-group kill. [documented, 1P async-fundamentals-initiative, fetched 2026-09-08] ADR-0003, ADR-0076 | — | Yes |
| Git is not a port | **DECIDED** | Trailers, worktrees, disposable clones and `reconcile()` are load-bearing and have measured behaviour (F12, F19); no alternative VCS is named. ADR-0003 | — | Yes |
| Tier-1 / tier-2 durable-state split, and the second trailer axis | **DECIDED** | `Story:`-trailer resume alone is **insufficient**: **20.3% of slices (15/74) passed through a rejected state** over 360 trailer commits, and sealed-more-than-once was 21.6%, so a `Slice-Verdict:` axis is required. [measured, F12; the registered primary was uncomputable at n=1 and that is disclosed] ADR-0018 | — | Yes |
| `reconcile()` at every entry, and no optimisation that skips it | **DECIDED** | Drift is **12/174 = 7%** overall, and **3.6× more likely** on gates over 1 h (9/79 = 11% vs 3/95 = 3%) [measured, F19], while **45–48% of gates exceed 1 h** [measured, F18; n=174/191, one operator, one machine]. Median in-gap commits is 0 — rare, predictable, and cheap to catch. ADR-0018 | — | Yes |
| Human gate is a durable mark, never a waiting process | **DECIDED** | Nothing may depend on a process surviving: **~12 kills against one 12,000 s survivor**, and `issue-81` was killed 3× as a background job and completed in **116 s** in the foreground [measured, operational, F22; cause unestablished]. A gate costs a bounded ~**18,603 tokens** whether it lasts **65.2 min or 49.7 h** — identical to the token, on **one measured draw per duration point (n=1 each)**, so this is two points and not a demonstrated law [measured, F28, with the published correction that the flatness was **shared-prefix subsidy, not durability**]. Gate duration is free; frequency is marginal; placement is a value decision. ADR-0009, ADR-0017, ADR-0087 | — | Yes — CLI is the only answer channel in Phase 1 |
| Gate record `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` with a terminal expiry path | **DECIDED** | Every surveyed durable-execution platform mints a single-use token and guarantees a terminal event; **only GitHub pins a SHA, and none binds a tree hash** — so redkiln's binding is stronger than any surveyed platform, and stale-answer handling everywhere else is workflow-side re-validation rather than signal-side invalidation, which is why an explicit re-validation at the read site is required. [documented, 1P Step Functions / Restate / Trigger.dev / Inngest / DBOS / GitHub, fetched 2026-09-08] Expiry in the corpus was **unobservable, not observed-absent** — the F18/F19 gates had no deadline mechanism, so no gate *could* expire. ADR-0017 | — | Yes |
| Review gate — reviewer proposes, machine confirms | **DECIDED** | The verdict is a weighted coin and the findings are not: **not one defect class is stable-reject** (class means Reproduced 0.70 > MutationSurvived 0.37 > NarrowedPin 0.10 > control 0.00) while **19/19 rejections were on-target** [measured, F29; 60 draws, 0 void, $176.96, 16.2 h]; with the verdict removed, schema conformance was **52/52 = 100%** — a **ceiling** result, and it measures *emitted payloads*, not the generated schema — adjudication **14/14 confirmed**, **verified false positives 0/23**, a **floor** result and an **upper bound against a ground truth wrong for ≥13 of 23**, at **+74%** cost [measured, F31; both saturated, §1.2]. The evidence class is therefore computed in Rust from the repro record and a finding blocks only after the deterministic gate re-executes it and it flips. ADR-0074 (full supersession of ADR-0004) | — | Yes |
| Neither gate is sufficient alone | **DECIDED** | A reproduced regression was **approved on 3 of 10 draws** [F29], and four adjudicated defects survived fully green suites of **1,890/1,890 and 1,907/1,907** [F31]. The review gate is high-precision, low-recall: it may **ADD** findings and may never **CLEAR** an AC; blocking is computed in code from re-executed evidence, never from the model's `pass`. Corroborated: **34% (221/644)** of functionally-passing agent repairs fail review constraints derived from real PR comments [preprint, arXiv 2609.04167] | — | Yes |
| The lenient policy default (`narrowed-pin` advises) and the Advisory sink | **DECIDED** | The relaxation is free: lenient gave **9/23 blocked** against strict's 12/23 — block **counts** over the pinned CLEAN set, never accuracy — at **0/23 verified false positives**, a **floor** result and an **upper bound against a ground truth wrong for ≥13 of 23**, with detection unchanged [measured, F31] — **but the severity call itself is one operator's judgement (Ryan's), stated as such**. Its consequence is that the 7 adjudicated-true `narrowed-pin` findings need a destination, so an advisory finding is written in full, surfaced at the next gate without blocking, and retired only by re-review or an explicit recorded human act. ADR-0074, ADR-0082 | — | Yes |
| Verification gate — per-AC discriminating test is the norm | **DECIDED** | Red→Green is evidenced at **test** granularity but an AC is a claim about **one assertion**: on the AC-015 probe, removing step 7 turned the suite RED while inverting the ordering left **36 of 36 GREEN** [measured, F25; n=1 AC, one operator]. The affected-area gate kills **9 of 10 test-reachable mutants** (headline 93.3%, test-reachable 90.0%, median wall 323 s) — **the CI on 1/10 is wide and is reported as wide** [measured, F11]. **Scope, carried with every F11 citation: 8 of 23 corpus items sit outside S0.1 entirely, because their fixes touch only `workflows/` and not `src/` — a third of the corpus is unmeasured by this instrument, by construction** (§1.2, **S0.1-SCOPE**). ADR-0019 | — | Yes |
| Structured output is a configuration-time check | **DECIDED** | An agent card's `tools` allowlist **silently disables `--json-schema`**: `structured_output` absent, **exit 0**, `subtype: success`; adding `StructuredOutput` fixes it. [measured, F20] Therefore `StructuredOutput` is required in every card whose role demands a schema, checked at configuration time, and nothing parses JSON out of prose | — | Yes |
| Isolation is a measurement precondition | **DECIDED** | A default `claude -p` inherits the operator's whole environment: 12 MCP servers, ~280 tools, cache_creation **8,243 → 3,611** under `--strict-mcp-config` — MCP was **4,631 tokens = 56%** of the write, tools 280 → 34. The flag must be verified from the child's own `system/init`, because an ignored flag looks identical to an honoured one. [measured, F8] | — | Yes |
| Effort and model pin per dispatch | **DECIDED** | `--agents` is **INERT without `--agent`** (canary: both → BANANA; `--agents` alone → 4) [measured, F7; retires RFC R2]; reasoning effort **is** applied per dispatch — thinking tokens low **0.0** / high **4.8** / xhigh **221.0**, complete separation — **but low vs high did NOT separate on output** [measured, F15] | — | Yes |
| Cache rule is a split rule, scoped to machine and directory | **DECIDED** (verification of it is the BLOCKED row above) | The two mitigations point in **opposite** directions and this may not be collapsed: `--system-prompt` holds share **1.000** across commits [F16, 50 dispatches, within-arm range ≤0.001] and — on a prompt attribution the record does not establish — across worktrees w2–w10 [S-WT: w1 0.807, w2–w10 all 1.000 — **carried without a finding number**, F30 was reserved and never written; **CONTESTED**: the brief does not say which prompt configuration S-WT ran, and F16's worktree-each arm read 0.685 the other way — §7.7, AC-387], but **forfeits the shared-prefix subsidy across time** — not an account-wide or org-wide one: **§7.2 records that there is no account-wide shared head**, and the brief's "account-wide" wording is retired here for the same reason "org-scoped" was — creating **29,680** tokens at share **0.000** at 66.8 min [F28, **n=1**] and **29,619** at share **0.000** at 66.1 min [S-H1b, **n=1**, and S-H1b carries **no finding number**] — **two single draws from two spikes, quoted separately and never as an interval** — where the default prompt costs a flat 18,603 at both 65.2 min and 49.7 h, one draw per point [F28 correction, S-H1b]. Scope is **workspace-scoped** on the API/AWS/Foundry, org-scoped only on Bedrock/GCP, and additionally **machine + directory + git-status** scoped inside Claude Code — the proposal's "org-scoped" is withdrawn. Two worktrees of one repo therefore **never** share a cache; every worktree pays a cold first turn, structurally. [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08] ADR-0078 | — | Yes |
| Cost and headroom telemetry — batch-delta with a `resolution_limited` flag | **DECIDED** | `utilization` is quantised to **0.01**, so **per-run cost is not measurable** and the E0 cache-cost gate was **STRUCK, not answered**; `five_hour` and `seven_day` are proven independent, not mirrors [measured, F6]. 40 dispatches / ~1.9M tokens stayed **below the instrument** (0.100 → 0.100) [F14]. Per CLEAN draw is **0.0098** five_hour — **F21's 0.077 is 7.8× too high and is superseded** [F29]. The binding constraint on a CLEAN set is **wall clock, not headroom** (16.2 h for 60 draws) | — | Yes |
| Throttle on `five_hour`; the binding sub-limit is invisible | **DECIDED** | Plan headroom is readable only via an undocumented top-level `rate_limit_event` (`result.rate_limit` is null), and there is **no Opus/Sonnet family sub-limit key** — the binding limit cannot be observed, so throttling is on `five_hour` with margins of ≥2 ticks and per-item token appetite as the real ceiling. [measured, F1] | — | Yes |
| Packs on two axes; rigor is an ordinal deontic enum | **DECIDED** | Process packs own structure; technology packs bind and contribute at slots, never stages or `require` rows. Rigor is `forbidden < discouraged < optional < recommended < mandatory` — ordinal, never summed, because **rank reversal occurs for virtually every weighted MCDM method**, TOPSIS and SAW included [peer, ScienceDirect S0895717711007850]. The ladder must be **monotone in internal quality**: escalating to `mandatory` with `has_repro` currently selects `bisect`, which has **no adversary and no policy step** [F37, brief C.10], and the lattice lint must enforce the fix. ADR-0014, ADR-0020, ADR-0022, ADR-0023, ADR-0029, ADR-0073, ADR-0081 | — | Yes |
| Catalog v0, the activity-graph schema, and the lattice | **DECIDED** | Lattice unsatisfiability fell **23.5% → 11.4% → 0.3%** and dead activities **15/101 → 6/102**, with **0 rank ties**, **0 hard cycles** and a 27 s lattice runtime after decisions 1–10; the pre-registered reading fires the kill twice and the amended lattice clears all three criteria, and **both readings are reported** [measured, F35]. Authoring is tractable: 90 activities at a median **53** non-blank lines, **0.11** first-run lint errors per file against P9's 4.7 — PC5 clears by **2.8×** [F34]; the schema authors at a median 56.5 and a second author at 73 (+29%), against a >150 kill, **but P9's "amendments will fall" was falsified at n=3** [F32, F33]. ADR-0027, ADR-0028, ADR-0030, ADR-0031–ADR-0073 | — | Yes |
| Typed artifacts, layout C, single write path | **DECIDED** | Markdown is a rendering: typed frontmatter + rendered read-only digest-stamped structured sections + opaque prose, with JSON sidecars for machine-only artifacts, and **nothing parses structure out of prose**. The AC type is permissive because the corpus forces it — **19 of 153** shipped specs (12%) write criteria with **no GWT (45 ACs) or no verification note (46 ACs)**, which is AC-137's own predicate and is disjunctive over specs; gate results are free-form in **39/156** reports and **19/23** reviews so they are emitted and rendered, never parsed; `plan.md` leaves the artifact list (**110/110** were the unfilled scaffold). [measured, S-MIG — **no F-number**; 1,312 files / 6,877 records at corpus pin `47faec9f`, zero model cost; the lifters were iterated four times against this corpus before the recorded run, so these are shares under a locator tuned to the corpus, not a blind parse] ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0075 | — | Yes |
| Clean break, `redkiln-migrate`, self-hosting as the switch | **DECIDED** | No code, file format or runtime is shared with the old redkiln. `reconcile.rs` is the one code import, rewritten in the spike and validated on 360 commits / 74 slices [F12]; the 101 CLI test files cross as black-box specifications and the recorded bugs as test cases; the corpus moves to its own pinned repo. ADR-0002, ADR-0013 | — | Yes — `redkiln-migrate` v0 is in Phase 1 |
| Rust workspace, toolchain pin, lint policy, dependency policy | **DECIDED** | Direct repository inspection found **no `.github/workflows` at all**, **no `rust-toolchain.toml`**, **no `[lints]` table** and no integration-test directory — measured absences, in that severity order. `gix` is at **0.87.1**, pre-1.0, with a dense RUSTSEC history concentrated in Windows checkout and path handling — exactly the disposable-worktree surface — **all fixed at or before 0.87.1, so the pin is CLEAN as of 2026-09-08**. `clippy::correctness` is the only deny-by-default group; pedantic and nursery are documented as false-positive-prone. SQLite serialises writers, so one writer connection, never a pool — **but that semantics was read via search only, not against docs.rs/rusqlite, and is [documented, UNVERIFIED]**, discharged as **R-SQLITE** (§9.2, §9.4, §10.4); the 1P tag on this row covers the repository inspection and the clippy/gix material, **not** the SQLite claim. [documented, 1P + direct inspection, fetched 2026-09-08; the SQLite clause UNVERIFIED] ADR-0083, ADR-0084 | — | Yes |
| Phase 1 scope — fix and story lanes, CLI-only gates | **DECIDED** | `bug-standard` + `story` only, so Phase 2's own stories are authored under Redkiln-RS; gate answers from the CLI only, tracker grammar and web view in Phase 3; the Claude adapter authenticates by subscription only and asserts no API key. ADR-0009, ADR-0010, ADR-0011 | — | Yes |

**How to read the ratio.** Twenty-five of thirty-eight areas are decided on measurement, and the spine of the build — kernel, ports, store, git substrate, the two gates that compute their blocks in code, the cost model — is among them. Four are provisional and say what would settle them. Nine are blocked, and three of those nine (the selection engine's LLM-emitted input, its derived-facet source, and `ChangeSummary`) stop specific Phase-1 work rather than the phase. **The method finding this table exists to honour:** five metrics saturated in this programme, and every discriminating result came from **cost, stability or mechanism — never from success rates** (brief C.12, F28). A row that reads "decided" here is decided on that kind of evidence or it is not decided at all.

---

## 1. What the evidence settled, and what it did not

Three tables. **1.1** is what the F-series closed and what each closure licenses a later section to assume. **1.2** is the register of everything that did not close — saturated, powerless, struck, not answerable, unrun or contested — with a Hedge column, because a reader who takes 1.1 without 1.2 will over-read this document. **1.3** is the short list of results that changed the thesis since `2026-09-05-architecture-proposal.md` rev. 4 was written; those five are the reason a fresh document exists rather than an errata sheet. §0.1 carries the decided/blocked ledger by area — this section carries the evidence behind it and does not repeat it.

### 1.1 Settled

| Question | Answer | Evidence | What it licenses |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Does moving review out of the interactive session into headless dispatch degrade it? | **No degradation is detectable — and the instrument could not have detected one.** Detection **23/23 vs 23/23**, **McNemar p=0.625**; cost **$97.39 vs $56.47 = 1.72×**; `parent_inspected` **0/46**. CLEAN false positives 4/23 vs 2/23 and MCC +0.839 vs +0.917 are **PROVISIONAL and do not belong in a settled column** (§0.1): severity is unadjudicated, and both are computed against a ground truth wrong for **≥13 of 23**, so the FP figures are **upper bounds** and an MCC against that key is not a settled figure at all. Re-run at two harder tiers: **20/20**, sighted and blind (5/5 ×4), $36.18, blind +18% median wall — **all four cells at ceiling, so this is a second saturated pair and confirms nothing about a between-arm difference** | [measured, F23; measured, F26] | ADR-0001/ADR-0002 — the clean-break single binary owning the loop, on the ground that **no difference was detectable and the instrument could not have detected one** — never "nothing was lost", which is a non-inferiority claim a tie at ceiling with no margin and no power cannot support, and never that headless wins on quality. **The primary metric saturated at ceiling in both arms, so it ranked nothing**; the only discriminating figure in E3 is cost. Any later section citing F23 must cite it as a non-inferiority result with a saturated primary |
| Are model and reasoning effort pinnable per dispatch? | **Yes, and the pin is a card property.** `--agents` is **INERT without `--agent`** (canary: both → BANANA; `--agents` alone → 4); the card's model + effort are accepted. Effort **is** applied per dispatch: thinking tokens low **0.0** / high **4.8** / xhigh **221.0**, complete separation; output 121/130/360 — **but low vs high did not separate on output** | [measured, F7, which retires RFC R2; measured, F15] | Per-activity model/effort declared on the card and refused at configuration time when incoherent (AC-203, AC-212, AC-236, ADR-0003). **Forbids** treating low→high as a quality dial: only xhigh is an observable step, and a pack that spends on `high` expecting more thinking is spending on nothing measured here |
| What does the prompt cache actually do under commits and under worktrees? | **Steady-state cache-read share: no-commit 1.000 · commit-each 0.689 · +`--exclude-dynamic` 0.846 · +`--system-prompt` 1.000 · worktree-each 0.685.** A commit costs ~31% of the prefix, half of it recoverable. The prefix has **two segments** — ~17,439 shared across directories + ~7,827 per-directory, 19/19 fresh repos identical — so a new worktree costs ~31%, not 100%. Across worktrees the S-WT record reads w1 0.807, **w2–w10 all 1.000** — **but the brief does not record which prompt configuration S-WT ran**, so attributing that hold to `--system-prompt` is **CONTESTED** (§7.7, AC-387) | [measured, F16; 50 dispatches, within-arm range ≤0.001] · [measured, F13] · [measured, **S-WT, carried without a finding number** — F30 was reserved for S-H1b/S-WT and never written, so these numbers exist only in the design-recommendation and the proposal] | `cache_profile` as a per-activity setting (AC-383, SM-113, ADR-0078). **Worktree cold-start is NOT priced by this row:** AC-387 requires the cost model to return `Unmeasured(cell)`, because F16's worktree-each 0.685 (~31%) and S-WT's 1.000 point opposite ways and the brief does not record which prompt configuration S-WT ran — §7.7, **CONTESTED EVIDENCE**, where the two readings differ by roughly 31× on a fan-out. **Forbids** the inference that "held across commits" implies "helps across time" — explicitly falsified by the F28 correction (brief C.1); see §7, which owns the two-mitigations-point-in-opposite-directions rule |
| Is suspension lossless, and at what granularity? | **Lossless at both granularities measured.** Activity granularity across a process boundary: **174/174**. Mid-graph step granularity across a real `taskkill /T /F`: **3/3 — 0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical** | [measured, F19] · [measured, F38; **n=3, PARTIAL**] | The workflow/activity split, the memo table and mid-graph gate resume (AC-013, AC-161, AC-185, AC-187, ADR-0021, ADR-0022, ADR-0086, ADR-0087). **Stated limit, carried in every citation:** the FSM was **never replayed**, so "same next action" is unproven and ships as a kernel replay test (AC-006, AC-188), and P8's state holds exactly one step (brief C.4) |
| Does a human gate get more expensive the longer it stays open? | **No — gate cost is flat in duration.** A gate costs a bounded ~**18,603** tokens at **65.2 min** and **18,603** at **49.7 h** — identical to the token, at **one measured point per duration (n=1 each)**, so duration-invariance is two draws and not an established law. At 0.9 min, share 1.000 / creation 0. Under `--system-prompt` at 66.8 min the same gate creates **29,680** at share **0.000** | [measured, F28, **with the published correction that the flatness was shared-prefix subsidy, not durability**] | Gate duration is free, frequency is marginally costly, and **placement is therefore a value decision, not a cost decision** (brief C.2; ADR-0009, ADR-0017, ADR-0087; SM-073). It does **not** license calling the gate durable — the mechanism is a subsidy that `--system-prompt` forfeits |
| Is a structured digest sufficient to carry a gate across a suspension? | **Measured sufficient, with no variance to detect insufficiency in.** H4 Jaccard **FULL 1.000 = CONTROL 1.000, effect +0.000** — the digest carries what the next step uses | [measured, F28] · **zero-variance caveat:** both arms sat at 1.000, so this is a saturated pair, not a demonstrated equivalence. The research pass declines the strong form outright: no first-party or peer-reviewed source affirms "prose adds nothing", and the same vendor's compaction guidance argues the opposite for judgment-bearing content [1P code.claude.com/docs/en/context-window, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 6, severity medium] | A `files[]` + evidence digest as the resume payload, **with rationale and caveats carried as explicit structured fields (`notes` / `caveats`), never as discardable prose** (AC-037, research pass §4 rec 18). Forbids stating digest sufficiency for judgment-bearing content |
| Does the affected-area verification gate catch a real regression? | **It kills 9 of 10 test-reachable mutants.** 15 mutants; 9 killed at test, **1 survived**; headline **93.3%**, test-reachable **90.0%**; median wall **323 s** | [measured, F11] | The affected-area gate as the norm and mutation as per-AC evidence (AC-289, AC-340, SM-101, ADR-0019). **The CI on 1 of 10 is wide and is reported as wide** — this is a floor for "the gate does something", not a precision figure. **Scope, carried with every F11 citation: 8 of 23 corpus items sit outside S0.1 entirely, because their fixes touch only `workflows/` and not `src/` — a third of the corpus is unmeasured by this instrument, by construction** (§1.2, **S0.1-SCOPE**). |
| Is `Story:`-trailer resume alone enough to reconstruct a slice's history? | **No — 1 slice in 5 passed through a rejected state.** 360 trailer commits over 74 slices; **ever-rejected 15/74 = 20.3%**; sealed-more-than-once **21.6%** | [measured, F12; **the registered primary was uncomputable at n=1** — 1 of 101 runs recorded the field — so the finding is re-scoped and disclosed as such] | The second trailer axis (`Slice-Verdict:`) and the tier-1/tier-2 split (ADR-0018, AC-162, AC-165, AC-371; §5 owns the mechanics). A single-axis trailer design would lose a fifth of the state it exists to hold |
| Is the reviewer's *verdict* usable as a gate signal? | **No — the verdict is a weighted coin and the findings are not.** Detection **0/7 items flaky over 21 draws**; **verdict 2/4 items flaky**. Across 60 draws: **not one defect class is stable-reject** (class means Reproduced **0.70** > MutationSurvived 0.37 > NarrowedPin 0.10 > control **0.00**) while **19/19 rejections were on-target** | [measured, F27; n=2–3, and a "stable" label at this n tolerates a true flip rate up to ~30%] · [measured, F29; 60 draws, 0 void, $176.96, 16.2 h] | Reviewer-proposes / machine-confirms: the evidence class is computed in Rust and a finding blocks only after the deterministic gate re-executes it and it flips (ADR-0074, full supersession of ADR-0004; AC-285, AC-286, SM-087, SM-091). Externally consonant: coding items sit at 23.6% instability in the high-flip stratum and a single trial matches a 50-trial majority only 86.6% of the time [preprint, arXiv 2606.13685] |
| Does forcing the reviewer to emit a typed value cost detection? | **No, and conformance was perfect.** 51 valid records, **schema conformance 52/52 = 100%**, `repro.command` fill **147/147**, detection unchanged (S-SILENT 23/23, S-SUBTLE 5/5 — both saturated), **verified false positives 0/23** — a **floor** result and an **upper bound against a ground truth wrong for ≥13 of 23** — adjudication **14/14 confirmed**, at **+74%** cost ($112.25 vs prose $64.61) and **1.6×** median wall (1,071 s vs 682 s) | [measured, F31] | Typed artifacts with markdown as a rendering, and nothing parsing structure out of prose (ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0075; AC-280, AC-281, SM-039, SM-040). Conformance is a **ceiling result** — it proves the contract is satisfiable, not that it is hard to satisfy |
| Can the catalog and its lattice actually be authored and closed? | **Yes, on both counts.** Authoring: 90 activities at a median **53** non-blank lines, sum 5,059, **0.11** first-run lint errors per file against P9b's 1.3 and P9's 4.7 — PC5 clears by **2.8×**; the schema itself authors at a median **56.5**, and a second author at **73 (+29%)**, against a **>150** kill. Lattice: unsatisfiability **23.5% → 11.4% → 0.3%**, dead activities **15/101 → 6/102**, **0 rank ties**, **0 hard cycles**, 27 s runtime | [measured, F34; F32; F33 — all zero model dispatches] · [measured, F35] | The v0 catalog, the activity-graph schema and the lattice CI (ADR-0027, ADR-0028, ADR-0030, ADR-0031–ADR-0073; AC-100–AC-103, AC-106, AC-112, SM-025–SM-027, SM-030, SM-031). **Two disclosures travel with it:** F35's pre-registered reading **fires the kill twice** (23.5%, 11.4%) and only the amended post-decision lattice clears all three criteria — both readings are reported; and F33 **falsified P9's "amendments will fall" at n=3** (27 ambiguities, 20 of them new) |
| Can a tool-restricted card be trusted to return a schema? | **No — it fails open, silently.** An agent card's `tools` allowlist disables `--json-schema`: `structured_output` absent, **exit 0**, `subtype: success`; adding `StructuredOutput` fixes it | [measured, F20] | `StructuredOutput` required in every card whose role demands a schema, checked at **configuration** time (brief C.5, AC-234) |
| Is a default `claude -p` a controlled measurement environment? | **No.** A default child inherits the operator's whole environment: 12 MCP servers, ~280 tools; cache write **8,243 → 3,611** isolated; MCP alone was **4,631 tokens = 56%** of the write; tools 280 → 34 | [measured, F8] | Isolation as a measurement precondition, **decided from the child's own `system/init` and never from argv, because an ignored flag looks identical to an honoured one** (brief C.11, AC-202, SM-114) |
| May anything depend on a process surviving? | **No.** ~12 kills against one **12,000 s** survivor under `Start-Process`; `issue-81` was killed 3× as a background job and completed in **116 s** in the foreground | [measured, operational, F22; **cause still unestablished**] | Every wait is a durable mark plus a later read; no waiting process anywhere in the design (brief C.6, ADR-0017) |
| How often does the world move under an open gate? | **Rarely, and predictably.** Drift **12/174 = 7%** overall, and **3.6× more likely** past 1 h (9/79 = 11% vs 3/95 = 3%), while **45–48% of gates exceed 1 h** (gate→verdict n=174 median 10.5 min; stage→verdict n=191 median 51.6 min, p90 ~9 h, **max 226 h = 9.4 days**). Median in-gap commits is 0 | [measured, F19; F18 — zero model cost, **one operator, one machine**] | `reconcile()` at every entry, and **no optimisation that skips it** (brief C.3; ADR-0018; AC-126, AC-172, AC-173, AC-176, SM-049, SM-058) |

### 1.2 Not settled, saturated, struck, or known wrong

Every row here is a place where a number exists but does not mean what it appears to mean, or where no number exists at all. The Hedge column names the branch taken meanwhile; the discharge is §10's spike register (ADR-0012). Rows marked **†** are among the **nine broken pre-registrations** the brief records in §E. **The ninth postdates the brief's 2026-09-08 condensation and was added to its §E list on 2026-09-09, taking that list from eight to nine:** METHOD-P §1.4's precision ≥ 0.95 rule is **not reachable at n = 20** for any facet however good, which F39 reports in its own Limits as a result *about* the pre-registration and never as an amendment to it — the same class as P1's alpha rule and P7's criterion 1, both already on this list.

| Item | State | Why it cannot be read as a result | Evidence | Hedge |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| E3's detection metric | **SATURATED** | 23/23 in both arms. A metric at ceiling in both arms ranks nothing; p=0.625 is the absence of a signal, not evidence of equivalence | [measured, F23] | Cited only as non-inferiority. The discriminating figure is the 1.72× cost ratio |
| E3-H's harder-tier detection | **SATURATED** | 20/20 sighted and blind. **The prediction that a harder tier would separate the arms could not be tested — both arms sat at ceiling in both tiers.** It failed to be detectable; it was not falsified, and principle 1 forbids reading it as one. The line is closed on that ground | [measured, F26] | No further tier is registered; the harder-fixture route is abandoned rather than re-run |
| S-TYPED schema conformance and verified false positives | **SATURATED** | 52/52 = 100% and 0/23. Both are ceiling/floor results; neither can move in the favourable direction | [measured, F31] | Conformance is a build-time invariant (AC-280), not a metric to track |
