### 1.3 The five results that changed the thesis

These are the results that postdate `2026-09-05-architecture-proposal.md` rev. 4 and move a claim it made. Every other correction in this document is a refinement; these five are reversals.

| Result | What rev. 4 held | What now holds | Evidence | Consequence for this document |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **F36** — P1's kill fires 4 of 4 on the selection engine's *input* | The selection engine could be driven by an LLM-emitted context vector, with a per-facet alpha≥0.6 check as its acceptance gate | The modal `R(stage, ctx)` was identical across ten draws on only **5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20** items against an **80%** line; the prescribed anchor-rewrite repair **did not work**; `external_dep` collapsed to a **constant (200/200 false)**; three axes with **byte-identical anchors** drifted up to **−0.168**, so the alpha rule is not a decidable predicate here; `unknown` rates novelty 46.0%, size 39.5%, blast_radius 31.0%, criticality 27.0%. Arm D was left uncomputed by F36 and has since been **scored by F39**: no derived facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar, `auth` scores **0.00** [0.00, 0.26] over eleven positives, and the ≥ 0.95 clause is unreachable at n=20 for any facet — which discharges **AC-071's fourth clause only**, leaves the block standing on the first (identity 0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10 against 0.80), and opens a **separate** block on the *derived* source under ADR-0089 | [measured, F36; 400 dispatches, 0 void, $21.37; measured, F39, for arm D — 140 cells, $0, panel-adjudicated]. Externally corroborated across five 2026 frontier models, with Claude-Sonnet-4 measured at **background temperature 0** — the instability is **schema-induced, not decoding noise** [preprint, arXiv 2603.04417; arXiv 2604.22411] | **BLOCKED (ADR-0079, `proposed` — the only non-accepted record other than two full supersessions).** The engine's **semantics** are decided and buildable; the **source** of its facets is not. §4 states the block at full strength in its own subsection and may not repair it. Phase 1 may not author a `require` row keyed on an LLM-classified facet; `kind` is out of scope of the block (human input, ADR-0030 rule 4). Do not spend a spike on temperature — apply the boundary-distance diagnostic to the existing draws instead [preprint, arXiv 2511.01176; research pass §4 rec 21] |
| **F37** — P7's primary criterion is powerless, not negative | The `adversary → policy` quality block was a recommendable default and the rigor ladder was monotone | The block costs **2.64×** ($2.69 vs $1.02/run) and its policy check was **false on 33 of 33**; the "**1 item in 10**" root-cause change is one cell moved by one draw of three — the powerless criterion's output, named here only to record that it may not be used as an effect; deviation was offered **71 times and used 0**. The criterion is **powerless at n=3** — every Wilson interval overlaps by construction. The evidence half of the block does work: **188 findings, 78% required running something**. And the ladder is **not monotone**: `mandatory` + `has_repro` selects `bisect`, which has no adversary and no policy step | [measured, F37; 61 runs, 297 records, 0 void, $131.62] | **BLOCKED (ADR-0080).** No default may be set from this run, and the predicate `findings.none(evidence in [Reproduced, MutationSurvived])` may not be used as a terminal condition. **ADR-0081** makes lattice monotonicity a lint (AC-105, SM-035). §4 and §6 author quality blocks explicitly and set nothing by default |
| **F38** — P8 returned no result on its own question | "Design final" was reachable this pass, and mid-graph gate placement was unpriced | **P8 has no result.** 3 draws of a pre-registered 27+3, **$3.01 of a $40 cap**; its **65-minute and 24-hour arms — the ones that straddle F10's ~1 h TTL — and all three no-kill controls are unrun**; its third criterion is **circular by construction**; its §5.2 fixture design **failed outright**, so every gate arrival was forced and the arrival rate is unmeasured. What it did return: mid-graph resume is lossless across a real kill (**0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical**) at **0.45× / 0.55× / 0.78×** of ~18.6k against a **>1.5×** kill — but all three runs sat inside the TTL and there is **no within-experiment baseline** | [measured/PARTIAL, F38; n=3; ADR-0087] | **"Design final" is BLOCKED as a label** (§0.1). Phase 1 starts on the DECIDED rows; **no document, section or commit message may describe this design as final.** ADR-0087 is PROVISIONAL and its cost claim carries n=3 and the missing baseline in the same sentence |
| **Research contradiction #2 — gix does not implement the `GIT_COMMON_DIR` / `GIT_WORK_TREE` interplay** (severity **high**) | Phase 1's `rk-git` would use gix to manage worktrees for a store living under `git-common-dir` and shared across worktrees | gitoxide's own `crate-status.md` states that "the delicate interplay between `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` isn't implemented" — **precisely the mechanism the store design leans on**. This is a named gap in first-party documentation, not a hypothesis. Separately (contradiction #8, severity medium): `crate-status.md` documents trailer **parsing**, and no mature first-class trailer-**writing** API comparable to `git interpret-trailers --in-place` was found — an **UNVERIFIED absence, not a proven one**, and named in §5 as "the highest-value single check in the list" because trailers are tier-1 state | [documented, 1P GitoxideLabs/gitoxide `crate-status.md`, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 rows 2 and 8, and §5] | **BLOCKED; the hybrid ships meanwhile (ADR-0077).** gix owns `rev-parse`, status, shallow clone by SHA and trailer *parsing*; `git worktree add/remove` and `git interpret-trailers --in-place` / `git commit --trailer` shell out. **gix may not own the write path, and §5 may not claim gix can write a trailer**, until **S-GIX** passes as a go/no-go against the exactly-pinned `gix = "=0.87.1"` |
| **Research contradiction #3 — native async fn in trait is still not dyn-compatible** (severity **high**) | A dyn-dispatched async port needed no `async-trait` under current Rust | Native async-fn-in-trait (stable since 1.75) is **still not dyn-compatible**; the vtable-signature problem is unchanged and `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` **remains nightly-only** (seen in nightly symbol tables through ~1.93–1.96, with no stabilisation announcement found). "No generics leak into the core" and "dyn-dispatched port" are in **direct tension** the moment a port method is async | [documented, 1P rust-lang.github.io/async-fundamentals-initiative; docs.rs/async-trait; fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 3] | **ADR-0076, taken explicitly rather than left implied:** port traits are **sync**, every `.await` is confined to an adapter holding its own runtime handle, and cancellation is an explicit port operation implemented as a real process-group kill. §3 owns the contract. Whether Rust 1.97 ships any stabilisation is one of the research pass's own unresolved items — until a direct release-notes read settles it, "still requires `async-trait` for dyn ports" is the safe working assumption [research pass §5] |

Three of the research pass's thirteen contradictions carry severity **high**, not two: the third is contradiction #1, the TTL bucketing correction, whose consequence is a blocked verification rather than a changed thesis — it appears in §1.2 as charter E1's struck metric and is owned by §7 (ADR-0078, spike **S-TTL1**).

### 1.4 How this ledger is discharged

Nothing in §1.2 is left as a prose hedge. Every row resolves to a named spike with a named consumer in §10's register, which is the mechanism ADR-0012 requires; where the discharge is a human judgement rather than a spike — the P1 arm-D panel (**adjudicated 2026-09-09**; 32 of its 140 cells are Ryan's own blind verdicts, F39), the E3 severity adjudication (**closed 2026-09-09**), the `bisect` ceiling question — the row names the person and the artifact, not an intention. **Two conventions this document uses are original process design, and are stated as such rather than presented as conformance to an external norm.** The research pass asked directly and could not find one: there is **no documented norm that an acceptance criterion should name its own verifying instrument**, so the AC-### + `depends_on` / `tracesTo` convention is **ahead of any external norm rather than confirming one**; and there is **no convention for phrasing an AC for a deliberately undecided or blocked item**, which is a genuine gap in the external literature and is why this document's PROVISIONAL / BLOCKED AC phrasing is invented here [documented, research pass §5 unresolved]. Both are load-bearing for the whole apparatus and neither is borrowed. Identifier conventions across the four families of design text (ADR-NNNN, AC-###, SM-###, and the F-series) are fixed by ADR-0085, which also records that ADR immutability is this repository's chosen convention rather than an inherited industry norm [1P github.com/adr/madr, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 11].

---

## 2. Principles the data imposes

Nineteen principles. The proposal's thirteen are all here, restated against the evidence as it now stands; six are new, forced by the P-series and the research pass. **Two changes of order and content matter enough to state before the table.** First, the method finding is no longer eleventh — it is principle 1, because it is the rule that decides whether any of the others may be called evidenced (brief §C.12). Second, three of the original thirteen rest on different evidence than they did: "nothing waits" now also rests on F38's mid-graph result, "absence of evidence is a block" now rests on F20's measured silent-schema-void mechanism, and "typed in, typed out" has had its strongest clause withdrawn by the research pass. §2.2 carries the full old→new map, so a `principle N` citation written against proposal §3 stays resolvable.

Each principle names the finding that forces it and states what it forbids. A principle that cannot be traced to a finding is not here. Where a principle touches a BLOCKED area of §0.1, the block is stated in the row and not resolved.

### 2.1 The nineteen

| # | Principle — and what it forbids | Forced by | Pinned by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | **Measure cost, stability and mechanism — not success rates.** *Forbids:* presenting a saturated metric as a win; reporting a powerless criterion as a negative result; reporting a criterion with **no denominator** as either a pass or a failure; reading a pre-registered bar as decided when the registered sample size cannot decide it; using a ground truth known to be wrong as a denominator; quoting a per-run figure the instrument cannot resolve. | Five metrics saturated in this programme and every discriminating result came from cost, stability or mechanism [brief §C.12]. E3's detection was **23/23 vs 23/23** at **McNemar p=0.625** while the cost separated at **1.72×** ($97.39 vs $56.47) [measured, F23]; 40 dispatches / ~1.9 M tokens moved `seven_day` **0.100 → 0.100** [measured, F14]; `utilization` is quantised to **0.01**, so E0's cache-cost gate was **STRUCK, not answered** [measured, F6]; P7's primary criterion is **powerless at n=3**, every Wilson interval overlapping by construction [measured, F37]; CLEAN's ground truth is wrong for **≥13 of 23 = 57%**, so every false-positive rate against it is an **upper bound** [brief §E, contested]. **F39 adds both of the new clauses:** `data_migration` emits **0 positives on 20 items across all three sources**, so precision is undefined and a perfect method scores identically — it is reported **not measurable**, never as a pass or a failure; and METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar is **not reachable at n = 20 for any facet however good**, `ui` at 7/7 buying a 95% lower bound of **0.65** against the roughly **60** positives a lower bound above 0.95 would need [measured, F39]. | AC-019, AC-381 (the question the resolution cannot answer is refused, not estimated), AC-382 (`low_sample` refused as an automatic input), AC-394, AC-396; ADR-0012 |
| **2** | **Rust holds the loop; the model holds no control flow.** Every `advance`, gate, retry and budget is code; the agent *classifies* and may *request* a typed deviation. *Forbids:* any model-produced value as an input to selection, review tiering, a budget decision or a terminal condition. | F36 converts this from a preference into a requirement: **400 dispatches, 0 void, $21.37**, and the modal `R(stage, ctx)` was identical across ten draws on only **5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20** items against an 80% line [measured, F36]. The typed deviation channel was **offered 71 times and used 0** [measured, F37] — the channel is right, its traffic is unmeasured. | AC-001, AC-003 (no `sleep`, no direct clock), AC-302 (`resolve_review_tier` takes no model-produced value), AC-113; ADR-0022, ADR-0030 |
| **3** | **Determinism is a property of the input as well as the engine — and this input is BLOCKED.** A deterministic selector over an unstable vector is not deterministic selection. *Forbids:* authoring a `require` row keyed on `criticality`, `novelty`, `size`, `blast_radius`, `market_facing`, `external_dep` or `has_repro` sourced from an LLM classification dispatch, until a P1 record clears AC-071's **identity ≥ 0.80** line; **and, separately, authoring one keyed on `auth`, `ui`, `flag_exists` or `data_migration` as computed by the lexical deriver, until S-FACET reports** (ADR-0089). `kind` is out of scope (human input, ADR-0030 rule 4). | P1's kill fired **4 of 4**; the prescribed anchor-rewrite repair did not work; `external_dep` collapsed to a constant (**200/200 false**); three axes with **byte-identical** anchors drifted up to **−0.168**, larger than the effect the alpha<0.6 rule exists to detect, so **that rule is not a decidable predicate on this corpus**; `unknown` rates novelty 46.0%, size 39.5%, blast_radius 31.0%, criticality 27.0%, and `unknown` is treated as `high` by position, so an axis oscillating unknown↔low moves `R` the full band width [measured, F36; brief §C.9]. Externally corroborated across five 2026 frontier models, with Claude-Sonnet-4 measured at background temperature 0 — the instability is schema-induced, not decoding noise [preprint, arXiv 2603.04417 / 2604.22411]. **The principle is now half-discharged and doubled.** Arm D is **scored**: no derived facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar, `auth` scores **0.00** [0.00, 0.26] over eleven positives every one of which is false, and the ≥ 0.95 clause is unreachable at n=20 [measured, F39; 140 cells at pin `47faec9f`, **$0**, panel-adjudicated]. That discharges **AC-071's fourth clause only** — the block on the LLM-emitted vector stands on the first, identity **0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10** against 0.80 — and it opens a second, distinct block on the **derived** vector, which AC-071 never covered (**ADR-0089**). | AC-009, AC-113 (**PROVISIONAL/BLOCKED**), AC-302; **ADR-0079** (status `proposed`, and it forbids being amended into a decision); **ADR-0089** (accepted, for the derived half). The LLM half is unblocked by a P1 record clearing identity ≥ 0.80 and nothing else; the derived half by **S-FACET** — see §0.1 and the register in §10 |
| **4** | **Git is truth; the record is a cache; `reconcile()` runs at every entry.** *Forbids:* any optimisation that skips reconciliation, and any write from the store back to git. | Drift is **12/174 = 7%** overall and **3.6× more likely** on gates over an hour (**9/79 = 11%** vs **3/95 = 3%**) [measured, F19], while **45–48%** of gates exceed an hour [measured, F18; n=174/191, one operator, one machine]; median in-gap commits is **0** — rare, predictable, cheap to catch. The substrate under this principle moved: gitoxide's own `crate-status.md` states the `GIT_COMMON_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` interplay "isn't implemented", which is precisely the mechanism the store leans on [documented, 1P GitoxideLabs/gitoxide, fetched 2026-09-08]. | AC-172, AC-173, AC-174, AC-176, AC-183; ADR-0018, **ADR-0077** (hybrid adopted, spike **S-GIX** named) |
| **5** | **Nothing waits.** Human gates, long gaps, rate-limit resets and fan-out joins are a durable mark plus a later read. *Forbids:* depending on a process surviving; a `sleep` in the workflow; treating a live process as a gate. | **~12 kills against one 12,000 s detached survivor**, and `issue-81` killed three times as a background job completed in **116 s** in the foreground, cause unestablished [measured/operational, F22]; a gate costs a bounded **~18,603 tokens** whether it lasts **65.2 min or 49.7 h** — identical to the token, on **one draw per duration point** — with the published correction that the flatness was **shared-prefix subsidy, not durability** [measured, F28]. **New evidence (F38):** the mark/read split now also holds *mid-activity* — mid-graph state survived a real `taskkill /T /F` with **0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical**, resuming at **0.45× / 0.55× / 0.78×** of ~18.6k against a >1.5× kill [measured, F38; **n=3, PARTIAL** — see the caution below]. | AC-024, AC-035, AC-185, AC-186, AC-187; ADR-0009, ADR-0017, **ADR-0087** |
| **6** | **Absence of evidence is a block, and the absence is usually silent.** `Result<Findings, StepError>`. *Forbids:* an absent digest read as `pass`; an exhausted budget read as `pass`; an absent usage field read as zero; a green suite read as a cleared claim. | F20 is the measured mechanism: an agent card's `tools` allowlist **silently disables `--json-schema`** — `structured_output` absent, **exit 0**, `subtype: success`; adding `StructuredOutput` fixes it [measured, F20]. The failure looks exactly like success, which is why the check is configuration-time. Reinforced twice: four adjudicated defects survived fully green suites of **1,890/1,890** and **1,907/1,907** [measured, F31]; and Red→Green is evidenced at *test* granularity while an AC is a claim about *one assertion* — on the AC-015 probe, removing step 7 turned the suite RED while inverting the ordering left **36 of 36 GREEN** [measured, F25; n=1 AC, one operator]. The `ephemeral_1h_*`/`ephemeral_5m_*` fields were **measured absent** [measured, F3]. | AC-007, AC-025, AC-031, AC-282, AC-320, AC-346, AC-362 (`Option` with no `Default`, no `unwrap_or(0)`); ADR-0019 |
| **7** | **Explicit over discovered; adoption and isolation are verified from the child's own report.** Every dispatch passes card, system prompt, tools, schema, MCP config and permission mode. *Forbids:* inferring from the parent side that a flag took effect — an ignored flag looks identical to an honoured one. | `--agents` is **INERT without `--agent`** (canary: both → BANANA; `--agents` alone → 4) [measured, F7]; a default `claude -p` inherits the operator's whole environment — 12 MCP servers, ~280 tools — and `--strict-mcp-config` moved `cache_creation` **8,243 → 3,611**, of which MCP alone was **4,631 tokens = 56%** of the write, tools **280 → 34** [measured, F8]. Isolation is a **measurement precondition**, not hygiene [brief §C.11]. | AC-195, AC-214, AC-215, AC-386 (a run whose isolation flags were not honoured has its rows marked non-comparable and excluded from every cost aggregate), AC-486, AC-511; ADR-0003, ADR-0010 |
| **8** | **Typed in, typed out; markdown is a rendering — but a digest carries files, not judgment.** *Forbids:* parsing structure out of prose; a locator treated as a grammar; and (**changed**) asserting that a structured digest loses nothing — a judgment call that matters downstream is carried as an explicit typed field or it is not carried. | Under the typed contract, schema conformance was **52/52 = 100%** and `repro.command` fill **147/147**, at **+74%** cost [measured, F31]. The type must be *permissive* because the corpus forces it: **19 of 153** shipped specs (12%) write criteria with **no GWT (45 ACs) or no verification note (46 ACs)** — AC-137's predicate, disjunctive over specs, not a "neither…nor" count — gate results are free-form in **39/156** reports and **19/23** reviews, and `plan.md`'s artifact list was the unfilled scaffold in **110/110** [measured, S-MIG — **no F-number**; 1,312 files / 6,877 records at corpus pin `47faec9f`, zero model cost; the lifters were iterated four times against this corpus, so these are shares under a locator tuned to the corpus, not a blind parse]. **Withdrawn:** "the reviewer's prose adds nothing" has no first-party or peer support, and the vendor's own compaction guidance argues the opposite for judgment-bearing content [documented, 1P code.claude.com context-window / prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 6, severity **medium**]. F28's H4 measured Jaccard **FULL 1.000 = CONTROL 1.000, effect +0.000** — for *file selection*, which is the only thing it measured. | AC-031, AC-329; ADR-0005 and ADR-0006 (both partially superseded by **ADR-0075**) |
| **9** | **One dispatch, one schema, per activity.** Sequential work gets a single agent; orchestration is reserved for the parallel project DAG and encapsulated review panels. *Forbids:* decomposing sequential work across agents to buy quality. | Sequential multi-agent decomposition **−39…−70%**, parallel **+80.9%**, error amplification **17.2×** independent vs **4.4×** centralised [preprint, arXiv 2512.08296, re-verified 2026-09-08 — a Google Research/DeepMind study, not "Google/MIT"; the cited 180-config / 4-benchmark figures are the original version, not the Apr 2026 v3's 260 configs / 6 benchmarks]; automatic multi-agent systems "consistently underperform CoT-SC despite being up to **10×** more expensive" across six frameworks [preprint, arXiv 2606.13003]. | ADR-0022, ADR-0026. AC-228 (the ~5 s fan-out stagger) is **PROVISIONAL** — no fan-out spike ran and the interval is documented, not measured here |
| **10** | **Gate duration is free, frequency is marginally costly, placement is a value decision.** Skipping is `0` — explicit and logged. *Forbids:* justifying or refusing a gate on its duration; pricing a gate without naming its arrival frequency. | **18,603 tokens** at both **65.2 min** and **49.7 h**, **one draw per duration point** [measured, F28 as corrected]; gate→verdict median **10.5 min** with **45%** over an hour, stage→verdict median **51.6 min**, p90 **~9 h**, max **226 h (9.4 days)** [measured, F18; one operator, one machine]; mid-graph resume **0.45–0.78×** [measured, F38, n=3, PARTIAL]. | AC-038, AC-039, SM-008; ADR-0087 |
| **11** | **No mitigation generalises past the axis it was measured on.** *Forbids:* inferring that "held across commits" implies "helps across time" — explicitly falsified; collapsing the two cache mitigations into one recommendation; interpolating a cost cell from two arms measured separately. | `--system-prompt` holds share **1.000** across commits [measured, F16; 50 dispatches, within-arm range ≤0.001] and — on a prompt attribution the record does not establish — across worktrees **w2–w10** [measured, S-WT: w1 0.807, w2–w10 all 1.000 — **carried without a finding number**; F30 was reserved and never written; **CONTESTED**, §7.7 / AC-387], but **forfeits the shared-prefix subsidy across time** — the brief's own wording is "account-wide", which §7.2 retires: **there is no account-wide shared head**, and isolation is workspace-scoped plus machine + directory + git-status inside Claude Code. It creates **29,680** tokens at share **0.000** at 66.8 min [F28, **n=1**] and **29,619** at 66.1 min [S-H1b, **n=1**, no finding number] — **two single draws, never a range** — where the default prompt costs a flat **18,603**, one draw per duration point [measured, F28 correction / S-H1b]. The two mitigations point in **opposite** directions [brief §C.1]. | AC-383 (every card declares the axis its `cache_profile` is optimised against), AC-387 (`Unmeasured(cell)` rather than a number), AC-382; ADR-0016, **ADR-0078** |
| **12** | **Neither gate is sufficient alone: a gate may ADD a finding and may never CLEAR a claim.** Blocking is computed in Rust from re-executed evidence. *Forbids:* the model's `pass` as an input to a block; a review verdict clearing an AC; a green suite standing in for per-AC evidence. | A reproduced regression was **approved on 3 of 10 draws**, and **not one defect class is stable-reject** — class means Reproduced **0.70** > MutationSurvived 0.37 > NarrowedPin 0.10 > control **0.00** — while **19/19 rejections were on-target** [measured, F29; 60 draws, 0 void, $176.96, 16.2 h]. With the verdict removed: conformance **52/52** (a **ceiling** result), adjudication **14/14 confirmed**, **verified false positives 0/23** — a **floor** result and an **upper bound against a ground truth wrong for ≥13 of 23** [measured, F31]. Four adjudicated defects survived fully green suites [F31], and the affected-area gate kills **9 of 10** test-reachable mutants (headline 93.3%, test-reachable 90.0%) with the **CI on 1/10 reported as wide** and **8 of 23 corpus items outside the instrument by construction** (§1.2) [measured, F11]. Corroborated: **34% (221/644)** of functionally-passing agent repairs fail review constraints derived from real PR comments [preprint, arXiv 2609.04167]. | AC-283, AC-284, AC-289, AC-320, AC-346; **ADR-0074** (full supersession of ADR-0004), ADR-0019 |
| **13** | **A finding that does not block still has a destination.** *Forbids:* dropping a non-blocking finding at source; leaving it implicit in a verdict; retiring one without a re-review or an explicit recorded human act. | The relaxation is free — lenient blocked **9/23** against strict's **12/23** — block **counts** over CLEAN, never accuracy — with **0/23 verified false positives**, a **floor** result and an **upper bound against a ground truth wrong for ≥13 of 23**, and detection unchanged [measured, F31] — but that leaves **7 adjudicated-true `narrowed-pin` findings with no destination, and no Advisory sink existed** [brief §C.8]. **The severity call itself is one operator's judgement (Ryan's), stated as such** — and that stays true after the E3 severity adjudication **closed on 2026-09-09** (4 BLOCKING, 2 ADVISORY; arm (a) FP 1/23 MCC +0.978, arm (b) FP 0/23 MCC +1.000). The two rates are no longer provisional and are still **upper bounds**, because CLEAN's ground truth is wrong for **≥13 of 23** and CLEAN may never be a denominator [F24; §10.4]. | AC-298, AC-310 (`findings_in == blocks + sink_records`), AC-311 (**human gate**), SM-089; **ADR-0082**, ADR-0074, ADR-0025 |
| **14** | **An ordinal ladder must be monotone in the quality it promises.** *Forbids:* an escalation in rigor that removes a quality-apparatus step (adversary, policy, or a declared quality block); summing rigor into a numeric score; escalating into a rigor at which a family has a hole. | Escalating to `mandatory` with `has_repro` currently selects `bisect`, which has **no adversary step and no policy step** [measured **mechanism**, F37 / brief §C.10 — cited as a mechanism and never as a rate, because P7's primary criterion is powerless at n=3]. The ordinal form is not a preference: **rank reversal occurs for virtually every weighted MCDM method**, TOPSIS and SAW included [peer, ScienceDirect S0895717711007850]. | AC-105 (lattice CI monotonicity check over the facet × rigor cross-product), AC-116 (escalation capped at the last total rigor), AC-303, AC-104, SM-035; **ADR-0081**, ADR-0020, ADR-0023, ADR-0056 |
| **15** | **A policy predicate that is a constant function of its arm carries no information and may not terminate anything.** *Forbids:* `findings.none(evidence in [Reproduced, MutationSurvived])` as a terminal condition; a facet whose measured distribution is degenerate feeding a `require` row; setting a default from a criterion with an empty denominator. | P7's policy check was **false on 33 of 33** — satisfiable only by a reviewer that finds nothing, which F23 (23/23), F26 (20/20) and F31 (52/52 conforming and 0/23 verified FP — both ceiling/floor results, the second an upper bound against CLEAN) prove this reviewer is not [measured, F37]. Deviation was **offered 71 times and used 0**, so criterion 3 has an **empty denominator** [measured, F37]. The same failure appears on the input side: `external_dep` collapsed to a **constant, 200/200 false** [measured, F36]. **The quality shorthand's default therefore stays unset** — see §0.1; it is BLOCKED, and the unreconciled mandatory cost ratio (**3.66×** in F37, **4.4×** in the decisions log) is not a tiebreaker. | AC-303, AC-113; **ADR-0080** (leaves the default explicitly unset and forbids the predicate), ADR-0030 |
| **16** | **Ports exist at named variation axes only; the port trait is sync, and git is not a port.** *Forbids:* a port without a named substitute or a fake that pays for it; a generic parameter leaking into the core; an `.await` outside an adapter; a cancel that is not a real process-group kill. | Five axes with real named substitutes (Codex, Linear, Astro, .NET/UE) plus EventStore's fake [documented, ADR-0003]; git's trailers, worktrees, disposable clones and `reconcile()` are load-bearing with measured behaviour and no alternative VCS is named [measured, F12, F19]. **Changed:** native async-fn-in-trait is **still not dyn-compatible** and `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` remains nightly-only, so "no generics leak into the core" and "dyn-dispatched port" are in direct tension the moment a port method is async [documented, 1P rust-lang.github.io/async-fundamentals-initiative, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 3, severity **high**]. | AC-480, AC-482; ADR-0003, **ADR-0076** |
| **17** | **The kernel knows no language and no tracker.** Ecosystem behaviour lives in technology packs behind the `Toolchain` port; collaboration surfaces are projections behind the tracker port and are never authoritative. *Forbids:* a technology pack contributing a stage or a `require` row; a projection being read back as truth. | A mixed Node+UE item **silently received the UE change summary only** under the old specificity tie-break, which is why a required toolchain-keyed kind now expands to one instance per element of the item's `toolchain` set [measured, F35 §1.3; ADR-0073 partially supersedes ADR-0029]. **Blocked at the edge:** the `ChangeSummary` type itself cannot be finalised — **S-BIN is registered and UNRUN and gates Phase 1** for it, and no review evidence of any kind exists for items whose change is not a text diff (§0.1; ADR-0008, ADR-0012). **Also blocked at the edge, and tested here (ADR-0089):** withdrawing the repo-wide `auth` boolean introduces a **set-valued `surfaces` facet** whose *values* come from a repo-authored `surfaces.toml` and are extensible by a technology pack — which ADR-0030 rule 1 already permits. The line holds unchanged: a technology pack may extend a facet's **value set** and may still not add a facet, a stage or a `require` row, and `surfaces` is unscored until **S-FACET** runs. | ADR-0014, ADR-0029, **ADR-0073**, **ADR-0089**; the five toolchain-keyed kinds |
| **18** | **Lessons, not code, from the old redkiln.** Its 101 CLI test files cross as black-box specifications and its recorded bugs as test cases. *Forbids:* sharing code, file format or runtime; and a single-axis trailer resume. | One exception — `reconcile.rs`, rewritten in the spike and validated on **360 trailer commits across 74 slices** [measured, F12]. The same run showed a `Story:` trailer alone is **insufficient**: **ever-rejected 15/74 = 20.3%**, sealed more than once **21.6%**, forcing the second `Slice-Verdict:` axis. F12's registered primary was **uncomputable at n=1** and that is disclosed. | AC-162, AC-165, AC-184; ADR-0002, ADR-0013, ADR-0018 |
| **19** | **The plugin surface stays and is generated from one source — on borrowed evidence, labelled as borrowed.** *Forbids:* presenting any distribution finding as redkiln-rs's own baseline; shipping a plugin in Phase 1. | The two standalone binaries that got adopted (Backlog.md, Archon) own a durable artifact **and** ship a skill/MCP layer [documented, proposal §3 principle 13 citing RFC §7 — **no ADR carries this area**]. Every distribution finding in this programme is `[documented]` from *shipmates'* issue history — one project, one maintainer, one release series — and redkiln-rs has shipped no binary, so AC-480..AC-519's distribution half are borrowed-failure criteria and every measure there starts with "no baseline". **PROVISIONAL** per §0.1. | AC-480..AC-519 (distribution half), SM-145..SM-154; ADR-0011 (`rk-plugin-gen` is Phase 2) |

**The caution that principle 5 carries.** F38's mid-graph result is **n=3**, all three runs sat **inside** F10's ~1 h TTL (measured `gap_minutes` 0.9, 28.4, 29.1, notwithstanding the `0-delay` arm label), and there is **no within-experiment baseline** because all three no-kill controls are unrun — the comparison is only to F28. "Mid-graph resume is cheaper than a stage gate" may not be written without that named in the same sentence (ADR-0087, PROVISIONAL). The label "design final" is blocked on the same unrun arms; see §0.1.

**The caution that principle 12 carries.** Every false-positive figure behind it measures false *rejection*. The mirror risk is unmeasured and documented as large — framing a change as already reviewed and approved reduces vulnerability detection by **16–93%**, strongly asymmetric toward false negatives [preprint, arXiv 2603.18740]. Until the S-SUBTLE confirmation-bias arm runs, **no section may claim the review gate is guarded against false approval** (ADR-0088), and CLEAN is never a denominator.

### 2.2 Old→new map, and what changed

Every proposal §3 principle appears exactly once. A `principle N` citation in an area file or in the proposal resolves through the middle column.

| New # | Proposal §3 # | Brief §C | Status | What changed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 11 | C.12 | **Promoted to first, unchanged in content** | It is the programme's own method finding and it governs how every other row may be argued. Eleventh understated it. |

Both are withdrawn: the threshold rests on **one vendor post with no benchmark, no model and no window size**, and the consensus claim overstates what the sources support — the honest form is "a growing and increasingly recommended pattern, not yet uniform" [research pass §4 recs 16 and 17]. Neither phrase appears anywhere in this document; the withdrawal is stated here because this document's evidence discipline requires a withdrawal to be *recorded*, as it is for "org-scoped" and "prose adds nothing". §7.7's parallelism table states the rules with no worker-count threshold and no consensus claim. |

**Coverage check.** Brief §C.1–C.13 map onto principles 11, 10, 4, 5, 6/7, 5, 12, 13, 2/3, 14/15, 7, 1 and 3 respectively — one-to-one, with C.5, C.9 and C.10 each splitting across two principles because each states two independent obligations. **C.13 is F39's** and was appended rather than inserted: the brief's §C list is cited by item number from four documents (AC-019, AC-303, AC-466, AC-509, SM-035 and this document's own §7 rows all name a §C.10/C.11/C.12), so a new implication takes the next free number and never a position inside the sequence [documented, the AC register's own §0.4 rule that renumbering breaks every citation already written]. Principles 8, 9, 16, 17, 18 and 19 are carried by the proposal, the research pass and the ADR set rather than by a §C implication, and their sources are named in the row.

---

## 3. Target architecture — kernel, store, git, and the five ports

### 3.1 The binary

One Rust binary owns the loop. A deterministic kernel holds stage selection, gate evaluation, budgets, retry, memoisation and replay and holds no non-determinism of its own; a store owns the typed artifacts behind a single write path; a git layer owns trailers, worktrees, disposable clones and `reconcile()` and is deliberately not abstracted. Five ports mark the named variation axes. Every `.await` in the process lives below the port line (ADR-0076).

```
        ┌───────────────────────── redkiln (Rust, one binary) ─────────────────────────┐
        │ kernel   packs → stages → activities · gates · budgets · memo · replay       │
        │          deterministic; no clock, no rand, no I/O, no .await   (ADR-0022/76)  │
        │ store    .bklg / .kb typed artifacts · one write path · canonical serialiser  │
        │ git      trailers · worktrees · reconcile() · merge queue      (NOT a port)   │
        └──┬────────────┬────────────┬────────────┬────────────┬───────────────────────┘
           │            │            │            │            │      ← sync trait objects;
           ▼            ▼            ▼            ▼            ▼        cancel(handle) is a
      EventStore     Harness      Tracker      DocsSite     Toolchain   port call, never a drop
  P1    sqlite      claude -p       —            —          node        ← adapters own every
  alt     —      codex*, pi*   github, linear*  mdbook*,  dotnet*,        .await and their own
                                                astro*    python*,       runtime handle
  fake  in-mem Vec  scripted    in-mem board   no-op rec.  fixture-      (ADR-0076)
                    responder                              driven
        (* named, not built in Phase 1 · no Postgres adapter is planned — ADR-0003)
```

Phase 1 ships exactly one adapter behind each port it uses — SQLite, Claude Code and Node — and **no tracker and no docs-site adapter at all**; GitHub and mdBook are Phase 3 (SM-148, corrected against proposal §7). The consequence is recorded in SM-148: `StepError::Unsupported` is knowingly saturated at zero until a second adapter of any port exists, so its first non-zero value is the signal, not its level.

### 3.2 Authority — who writes what, most to least trusted

| Rank | Layer | Owner and write path | What it carries | Evidence / decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Git** — commits and trailers `Story:` / `Slice-Verdict:` / `Gate:` | kernel, through `rk-git`; the write path shells out to `git interpret-trailers --in-place` / `git commit --trailer` | checkpoint commits, slice verdicts, gate marks | `reconcile.rs` validated on 360 commits / 74 slices [measured, F12]; the second trailer axis is forced — 20.3% of slices (15/74) passed through a rejected state, so `Story:` alone is insufficient [measured, F12]. ADR-0018, ADR-0077 |
| 2 | **Item store** — `.bklg`, `.kb` typed artifacts | kernel via the store's single write path; agents return values, the store renders files | specs, plans, reports, KB atoms | Nothing parses structure out of prose [measured, S-MIG census, 1,312 files / 6,877 records at pin `47faec9f`; the lifters were iterated four times against this corpus, so these are shares under a tuned locator, not a blind parse]. ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0075 |
| 3 | **Event log** — behind the `EventStore` port | kernel | every stage / gate / dispatch / cost / drift / memo event; the telemetry tier is disposable | Journalled results are what make replay possible at all; the replay instrument itself has never been run — F19's 174/174 is about the state *carrier*, and the FSM was never replayed [measured, F19, with its own stated limit]. ADR-0021, ADR-0022 |
| 4 | **Projections** — Tracker, DocsSite, web | kernel, derived | labels, sub-issues, docs pages, views | Derived, never authoritative: a human edit to an orchestrator-only label produces a typed `Drift` record and **no** state transition (AC-507); drift is 12/174 = 7% overall and 3.6× more likely on gates over 1 h [measured, F19]. ADR-0003 |

Two rules follow from the ordering and are cited, not restated, here. The tier-1/tier-2 split test — *if the database were deleted, would `redkiln run` take a different next action?* — and the store's file layout are §5's. `reconcile()` at every entry, with no optimisation permitted to skip it, is also §5's (ADR-0018). What §3 fixes is only the direction of trust: **a projection is never read back as authority, and an inbound tracker event is a typed input, never a transition** (AC-507, AC-508).

Two authority-adjacent guards belong here because they are port and kernel obligations. The orchestrator never writes to the user's checkout — every mutating call resolves through a run-owned worktree or the store under the resolved common dir, asserted by `git status --porcelain` empty plus `git diff-index --quiet HEAD` plus HEAD unmoved across a full lane run (AC-518) [documented, shipmates L5.3: 0.1.18 committed into the user's checkout, 0.1.19 pinned the write to a dedicated worktree — one project, one maintainer]. And agents hold no tracker credential: the assertion is made from the child's own reported environment, not from the parent's spawn arguments (AC-509), because an ignored flag looks identical to an honoured one [measured, F8: 12 MCP servers, ~280 tools inherited by a default `claude -p`; MCP was 4,631 tokens = 56% of the cache write].

### 3.3 The five ports

| Port | Responsibility (sync trait) | Phase-1 real adapter | Named alternatives | Required fake | Why this axis exists |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `EventStore` | append; query by item / SHA / run; snapshot; rebuild | SQLite (WAL, `busy_timeout`), one writer connection, never a pool | **none planned** — the port exists for the fake and future safety, not for a Postgres adapter | in-memory `Vec` | SQLite serialises writers, so one writer connection and never a pool — **[documented, UNVERIFIED]**: the WAL and `busy_timeout` semantics were read via search, not against docs.rs/rusqlite, and §9.2/§9.4 register **R-SQLITE** to close it. The rule ships either way, since a re-fetch can only narrow it [ADR-0084]. ADR-0003 |
| `Harness` | `dispatch(ActivitySpec) -> Result<Typed, StepError>`; capability registry with dated probes; cost/usage; **`cancel(handle)`** | Claude Code (`claude -p`), subscription auth only, never `--bare`, asserts no API key | Codex (`codex exec --json --output-schema`), Pi | scripted responder | Second-harness quality is unmeasured — E3 is Claude-only; a Codex-sourced result may not close a gate until an E3-shaped run exists (AC-239, AC-496, both PROVISIONAL; S-CODEX unrun). ADR-0003, ADR-0007, ADR-0010 |
| `Tracker` | `project(item) -> ExternalRef`; `inbox() -> Vec<TypedInput>`; idempotent writes | **none in Phase 1** (GitHub is Phase 3) | Linear (GraphQL, signed webhooks), Jira | in-memory board | Every external side effect needs an idempotency key [documented, shipmates L5.4: one thread holds the same report posted three times by their own orchestrator]. AC-508 |
| `DocsSite` | `build(DocSet)`, `check_links`, `drift(changed_sources)` | **none in Phase 1** (mdBook is Phase 3) | Astro/Starlight, rustdoc, DocFX | no-op recorder | Generator-agnostic invariants live in the kernel, generator checks in the adapter; the three invariants are exercised against the fake with a negative case each, or the suite is vacuous (AC-510) |
| `Toolchain` | detect; `build`; `test(selection)`; `lint`; `classify(path) -> Text/Binary/Generated`; `change_summary(range) -> ChangeSummary` | Node (vitest/pnpm) | .NET, Python, Unreal, Rust | fixture-driven | The kernel knows no language. **`ChangeSummary`'s field set is BLOCKED** — see below |

**`ChangeSummary` is blocked, and this section does not route around it.** S-BIN is registered and **unrun**, and no review evidence of any kind exists for items whose change is not a text diff [documented; ADR-0008, ADR-0012]. The port *shape* is asserted — a `Binary` classification means the reviewer receives a typed `ChangeSummary` and no textual diff of that path is assembled or dispatched (AC-497) — and the type's **contents are out of scope until S-BIN reports**. The five toolchain-keyed kinds of ADR-0073 cannot be finalised without it. Consumer: the Phase-1 type-design work for `ChangeSummary`; spike register in §10.

**The card-delivery route is blocked in the same disciplined way.** The Harness adapter's Phase-1 default is the static `--system-prompt` carrying the card body, with `--append-system-prompt` / `--exclude-dynamic-system-prompt-sections` absent from argv (AC-211, PROVISIONAL; ADR-0016, untouched by ADR-0078's partial supersession). The append route **may not be enabled as anything but an unmeasured config option until S-H1c runs**. Its cost consequences are §7's.

#### Port rules

| # | Rule | Instrument | Criterion | Evidence / decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P1 | One trait per port; **no generic parameter bound by a port trait appears anywhere in the kernel**, and every port is held as one object-safe trait object | API-surface lint + a compile-fail fixture | AC-480 | ADR-0003. The dyn boundary is load-bearing for the fakes; making the kernel generic to recover native async is explicitly forbidden (ADR-0076) |
| P2 | A fake per port is a hard requirement of adding the port, and the equality is asserted **as a number** | `traits.len() == fakes.len()` inventory test; a sixth trait with no fake must fail | AC-481 | [documented, shipmates L5.8: make the count the assertion] |
| P3 | Adapters are resolved **once at startup** from `.redkiln/config.toml`; the adapter set is a run-scoped constant recorded on the run-start event | mid-run config mutation test + replay assertion | AC-485 | ADR-0003 |
| P4 | A configured adapter that is not compiled in, or lacks required configuration, refuses the run **before any process is spawned and before any store write**; `doctor` reports the same defect at zero model cost | startup-resolution test; `doctor` rule row | AC-486 | Principle "explicit over discovered"; motivated by F7/F8 [measured] — the generalisation that an unhonoured setting looks identical to an honoured one is [inferred] from them, not measured |
| P5 | Capability differences are **data**: `{capability, verified_on, probe}`, with no capability readable except through a row carrying all three, and an unknown token failing the build rather than defaulting | registry schema test; vocabulary test; catch-all-arm lint | AC-489, AC-492 | [documented, shipmates L4.3/L5.7: capability research has a shelf life of weeks — Gemini→Antigravity, Windsurf→Devin Desktop invalidated a table by its own follow-up comment] |
| P6 | The kernel contains **no adapter-identity branch** — no adapter name string, no `Any::downcast`, no adapter-discriminant enum | kernel denylist lint + compile-fail fixture | AC-487 | ADR-0003. The lint proves absence of identity checks only; that a capability lookup exists is carried by AC-494/AC-495 |
| P7 | A missing capability is `StepError::Unsupported(capability)` carrying the token — never a generic error, never a retry, never a `pass`; a pack whose only route through a stage needs it fails validation with a span at the requiring row | resolver unit test; pack-validation test over a routed and an unroutable fixture | AC-494, AC-495 | ADR-0003, ADR-0014 |
| P8 | A **sixth port** is admitted only if a concrete substitute adapter is named, or the fake alone is judged to pay for the abstraction | routed to a human gate — **not machine-decidable**; the machine-checkable half is P2 | AC-482 | ADR-0003, ADR-0017. The failure mode guarded against is a plausible-sounding port with no substitute |
| P9 | The same shared conformance suite runs against `{fake, real}` for each port, and an assertion that passes on the fake and fails on the adapter is reported as a divergence | parameterised conformance suite, one CI matrix row per instantiation | AC-499, SM-151 | SM-151 is **known to saturate**: a green matrix reads zero by construction, so the tracked quantity is assertion-count growth and the divergence count is its alarm (brief C.12) |

The kernel's side of P1 is enforced twice: the workflow module may not name an adapter crate (AC-005, `cargo-deny` plus a `cargo tree -i` assertion returning empty) and the workspace graph may carry no edge, direct or transitive, from `rk-kernel` to any `rk-adapter-*` (AC-484). Kernel and pack-validation suites run with network egress denied, no adapter crates linked and a dispatch counter asserted at 0 (AC-498) — every replay and lattice test therefore costs $0.

#### Git is deliberately not a port

Trailers, worktrees, disposable clones and `reconcile()` are load-bearing and have measured behaviour [measured, F12, F19], and **no alternative VCS is named** — so the axis fails P8 on its own terms. The port inventory lint carries a denylist over the git surface, and a declared `trait GitPort` is a compile-fail fixture (AC-483). `rk-kernel` depends on `rk-git` as a concrete crate.

Two claims about the *implementation* of that layer are **BLOCKED**, and are named here rather than smoothed over. gitoxide's own `crate-status.md` states that "the delicate interplay between `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` isn't implemented" — precisely the mechanism the one-store-per-repository design leans on [documented, 1P GitoxideLabs/gitoxide, fetched 2026-09-08]. And trailer **parsing** is documented while no mature first-class trailer-**writing** API was found — an *unverified* absence, not a proven one, over what is tier-1 state. The hybrid ships meanwhile (ADR-0077): gix for `rev-parse`, status, shallow clone by SHA and trailer parsing; `git worktree add/remove` and the trailer write shell out. **gix may not own the write path, and no section may claim gix can write a trailer, until S-GIX passes.** Spike S-GIX, consumer: the `rk-git` write path; register in §10. The store's file layout and the read/write split in detail are §5's.

### 3.4 The workflow/activity split, and the determinism ban list

The split is transplanted deliberately: the **workflow** — stage selection, gate evaluation, retry policy, DAG order — is deterministic Rust replayable from the event log; **activities** hold all non-determinism and each carries a retry policy, a timeout with a number, and an idempotency key (ADR-0022).

**The ban list is CONVERGENT, not a Temporal-ism.** AWS's first-party Durable Execution SDK (2025/2026, Lambda-backed, distinct from Step Functions, supporting TypeScript/Python/Java/C# and **explicitly not Rust**) independently re-derived the same list for its `step` primitive: ban `Date.now`/`time.time`/`Instant.now`, UUID generation, HTTP/DB/SDK calls, `Math.random`, and any branching on these [documented, 1P AWS Durable Execution SDK determinism guide + 1P docs.temporal.io/workflow-definition, fetched 2026-09-08; evidence-augmentation §2, §3]. ADR-0086 forbids presenting the list as vendor-specific for exactly this reason. It is two vendors' documented designs, not a measurement that the split is optimal.

| Banned inside workflow code | Enforced by | Criterion | Where it may live instead |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Wall clock, monotonic instant, timezone-dependent value (`SystemTime::now`, `Instant::now`, `chrono::Utc::now`) | clippy `disallowed_methods` over `rk-kernel::workflow` with `-D warnings`, plus a `trybuild` must-fail fixture | AC-001 | Time reaches the workflow only as a journalled activity result — including the circuit breaker's expiry comparison (AC-035) |
| Randomness and non-deterministic id minting (`rand`, `uuid::new_v4`, address hashing) | same list, same fixture | AC-002 | Ids derive from `(run_id, activity_id, attempt)` or from a content hash |
| I/O — filesystem, network, process spawn, blocking sleep (`std::fs`, `std::process`, `std::net`, `tokio::time::sleep`, `thread::sleep`) | `disallowed_methods`/`disallowed_types` + fixture | AC-003 | Activities, which carry retry, a numeric timeout and the idempotency key |
| Iteration over an unordered map or set in a decision | `disallowed_types` on `HashMap`/`HashSet` in workflow decision signatures and locals + fixture | AC-004 | Ordered containers, or an explicit sort with a total key. Declared order is load-bearing — seed-order tie-breaking — so "invariant under input permutation" is a property the kernel **must not** have and was cut as an instrument |
| A dependency edge on any adapter crate | `cargo-deny` ban + empty `cargo tree -i` | AC-005 | The workflow reaches the world only through `rk-ports` traits |
| `async fn`, `#[async_trait]`, a returned future, or any dependency on `tokio`/`async-std`/`futures`/`async-trait` | see §3.8 | ADR-0076's forbids-block | Adapters, which own their runtime handles |

Replay is the instrument that would prove the split works, and it **has never been run**. A replay binds every port to its fake and asserts the decision sequence and next action are byte-identical to the record (AC-006); a divergence halts with a typed `ReplayDivergence { activity_id, recorded, recomputed }` and no continuation on the recomputed value is reachable (AC-007); the replay must reach the recorded terminal state with all N results served from the journal and **zero** harness dispatches, so that a regression which re-dispatches is a red test rather than a bill, and a kernel that does nothing fails the terminal-state half (AC-008). SM-009 has **no baseline**: F19's 174/174 losslessness is about the state carrier, and the brief records that the FSM was never replayed, so "same next action" is unproven [measured, F19, with its stated limit]. ADR-0086 names the residual honestly — Temporal and AWS detect ban-list violations at replay; here, without a runtime enforcing it, a violation is a latent and possibly silent memoisation bug, which is why the ban is a build failure rather than a convention.

One kernel rule exists because of a **BLOCKED** area and must not be misread as relieving it. Where a stage's selection consumes a model-emitted classification or context vector, that vector is recorded verbatim in the activity result that produced it, and every later read — replay, resume, or a second stage — resolves from the journal rather than a new elicitation (AC-009). This is a journalling rule and nothing more. It does **not** claim the vector is stable and does not license selection over one: P1's kill fired 4 of 4, with the modal `R(stage, ctx)` identical across ten draws on only 5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20 items against an 80% line, the prescribed anchor-rewrite repair failing, `external_dep` collapsing to a constant (200/200 false), and three axes with byte-identical anchors drifting up to −0.168 [measured, F36; 400 dispatches, 0 void, $21.37]. Arm D has since been **scored** — no derived facet clears 0.95 and `auth` scores 0.00 [0.00, 0.26] over eleven positives [measured, F39] — which discharges AC-071's fourth clause and leaves the block standing on its first, identity 0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10 against 0.80 (ADR-0079, status `proposed`). The same journalling rule now also carries a **`null`**: under ADR-0089 a derived boolean that cannot be computed is recorded as `null`, never coerced to `false`, and the journal carries the abstention rather than a manufactured answer. The selection engine's semantics are §4's; both blocks are §4's to state at full strength.

### 3.5 The idempotency key and the memo key

| Key | Shape | Rules it enforces | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Idempotency** | `(run_id, activity_id, attempt)` | A dispatch cannot be constructed without it — the port takes it by value, not `Option` — and a malformed key is refused before any process is spawned. A retry carries `attempt + 1` and never reuses an attempt number; the triple is unique across the whole log. A late second attempt whose sibling already committed is discarded as a duplicate, and at most one `activity_committed` event exists per `(run_id, activity_id)` | AC-010, AC-011, AC-012 |
| | | The key is **not** required to be globally fresh: re-entry after a kill deliberately reuses `run_id`, and uniqueness is asserted over the triple only. `redkiln run <item>` is the re-entry — there is no `--resume` flag — and no committed step re-executes | AC-013 (**PROVISIONAL**) |
| **Memo** | `hash(activity id, resolved inputs, card digest, **resolved** injected KB atoms, toolchain version)` — the *resolved atom set*, never the inclusion rule that produced it, because a workspace-scoped conditional-inclusion mechanism resolves differently by workspace (§8.6; research pass contradiction #12) and would make the key non-reproducible | Changing any one of the five components while the others are held fixed misses the memo; all five identical hits it. A stale `.kb` atom therefore invalidates stale work — editing or superseding an injected atom produces a `memo_miss` and a re-execution | AC-014, AC-015; ADR-0021 |
| | per **step**, not per activity | An activity realised as an encapsulated graph has a key per step, and the activity-level key the stage sees hashes **every card reachable in the subgraph**; a key computed from a single card reference is a lint error | AC-016; ADR-0021, ADR-0022 |
| | **loop-iteration rule** | A step inside a `repeat` / `on_false` cycle whose resolved inputs are byte-identical across iterations **does not** hit the previous iteration's entry, because the loop iteration counter is part of the step key. Two iterations, two keys, two executions — the direct regression test for "loops cannot learn" | AC-017; ADR-0021, ADR-0043 (its first sentence and lint stand; its second sentence — a step declaring `memo = false` to assert liveness — is withdrawn by ADR-0059, which moves liveness to a `live = true` flag on the verb's registry entry that implies `memo = false` on every step using it) |
| | live state opts out | A `tool` step whose verb reads live state (telemetry, flags, clocks) and does not declare `memo = false` fails pack validation at the step's span | AC-018; ADR-0021, ADR-0064 |
| | never silent | A memo hit appends a `memo_hit` event carrying the key and its five components; a skip is never invisible | AC-019; ADR-0036 |

AC-013 is **PROVISIONAL** and says why: P8 is PARTIAL — 3 of a pre-registered 27+3 runs, and its recorded state held **exactly one step**; the 65-minute arm, the 24-hour arm and all three no-kill controls are unrun [measured/PARTIAL, F38]. What was measured is real and narrow: **0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical** across a real `taskkill /T /F`, and mid-graph resume at 0.45× / 0.55× / 0.78× of ~18.6k against a >1.5× kill [measured, F38, n=3, one machine, one operator]. The kernel test generalises to N what the spike measured at 1.

Two measurement honesties belong with the memo table. SM-001 forbids an undifferentiated hit rate: the rate must be split into steps outside loops, steps inside `repeat`/`on_false` cycles — a cache miss **by construction** under ADR-0021 — and `memo = false` tool steps, since only the first bucket is a meaningful rate. And the cost of memoisation is untested: whether a guaranteed in-loop miss is materially more expensive than the useless memoised loop it replaces is unmeasured, and bucket (b) is where it would show. The A18 evidence behind AC-016..AC-018 is [documented, no n] — ADR-0021 records it as an observed, named failure mode from one author's one loop, not as a measurement, and it may not be re-tagged.

### 3.6 Budgets, retry and degradation — the layering

| Layer | Who | Policy | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Budgets | three counters, **never shared** | attempts per activity · tokens per item (appetite, fixed at triage) · wall clock per run. Distinct newtypes with no `From`/`Add` crossing them, so a shared counter does not compile. `attempts_total` counts the agent/tool steps of a **pass**, not the sum of retried steps across passes; a `for_each` over N items charges **once**, independent of N | AC-020, AC-021, AC-022, AC-023; ADR-0037, ADR-0053 |
| Inner retry | the harness itself (`system/api_retry`) | observe, record, do not duplicate; inner attempts count against the activity's budget | AC-026 (**PROVISIONAL**), AC-232 |
| Outer retry | kernel policy per error class | `Transport` / `Platform` 5xx / `Timeout`: exponential backoff with full jitter, base 2 s, cap 5 min, ≤ the pack's `N`. `RateLimited { resets_at }`: **no sleep** — write a `retry_at(resets_at)` mark and exit 0. `Schema`: re-prompt exactly once carrying the validation error, then `Schema { attempts: 2 }` | AC-029, AC-030, AC-031, AC-233, AC-235 |
| Never retried | — | `NotAdopted`, `NotIsolated`, `Unsupported(_)`, `Overage` — configuration, not weather. Zero re-dispatches, the typed error surfaced | AC-027, AC-234; SM-003 (a **tripwire that saturates at zero**, not a discriminator — one retry against any of the four blocks the release that introduced it) |
| Not an error at all | — | An activity that completed and returned blocking findings is **not** retried: the success and failure channels are disjoint (`Result<Findings, StepError>`), so "the reviewer said no" cannot be constructed as a `StepError` | AC-032; ADR-0004 (superseded by ADR-0074; cited here as the origin of the type split) |
| Circuit breaker | per model family | K consecutive `Platform` failures refuses the next dispatch for that family behind a **durable mark**; other families continue; the refusal survives process death, and breaker state is never held only in memory | AC-033, AC-034, AC-035, AC-237 |
| Degradation | policy, never silent | a model substitution is refused unless the pack permits it, and a permitted one records `Degraded { from, to, why }` in both the event log and the digest; a review produced under a substitution below a gate's demanded tier does not close that gate | AC-036, AC-037, AC-038, AC-236 |
| Budget exhaustion | — | An exhausted budget yields `Err(BudgetExhausted)` and the gate does not close; no code path maps an exhausted budget to `pass`. The run writes a durable mark and exits 0 — nothing sleeps waiting to be extended | AC-024, AC-025 |

**`K`, `N`, the backoff base and cap, the two-tick headroom margin and the per-item token appetite have no measured values.** No spike sized a `Platform` failure rate at all, and the AWS backoff post uses generic base/cap variables — "base 2 s, cap 5 min" does not appear in it, and it nudges toward Equal Jitter specifically for throttling errors [documented, 1P aws.amazon.com/blogs/architecture/exponential-backoff-and-jitter, currency-noted May 2023, fetched 2026-09-08]. Every one of these numbers is declared in the pack and refused as a code default (AC-029, AC-033, AC-023); the criteria verify conformance to the declared envelope, never that the envelope is right. Whether a halted run's budget is extended or the item abandoned is routed to a human gate and is **not machine-decidable** — no measured signal in this corpus discriminates it — though the mechanism around it is machine-checked (AC-039).

#### 3.6.1 The harness contract — AC-200..AC-239, named by subject

The harness area (AC-200..AC-239, SM-061..SM-070) is the largest area in the thirteen and had, until this revision, no design home at all; it was discharged in one sentence and two citations. It is still **cited rather than restated** — the area file owns the wording — but the subjects are named here so that a reader can tell what is covered and a runbook can tell what is owed.

| Subject | What the area fixes | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Stream and event retention | Unknown event **types** and unknown **fields on known events** are retained verbatim — the rule §9.6's raw-payload pattern depends on; verbatim event persistence across the run record | **AC-201**, **AC-227**; SM-061 |
| Isolation and adoption | Isolation asserted from the child's own `system/init`, adoption by **set equality** against the card's allowlist, and a run whose flags were not honoured marked non-comparable | **AC-202**, **AC-204**, AC-214, AC-215, AC-386 |
| Structured output | Refusal **before spawn** when a schema is demanded and the card cannot satisfy it; **exit 0 with no `structured_output` resolves to `Err`**, never to a pass — this is the area that owns the rule §8.1's AC-439 re-derives from the cli-doctor side | **AC-205**, **AC-206**, AC-208 |
| Prompt, cache and card delivery | Prompt on stdin never argv; the card body delivered as the static `--system-prompt` (the append route BLOCKED on S-H1c) | **AC-209**, **AC-210**, AC-211 |
| Model, effort, permissions, auth | Model and effort pinned per dispatch from the card; a **timeout human gate**; **never `--bare`**; **no API key in the child environment** — the harness-area home of a rule §2 and §8.4 currently cite through AC-488/AC-422/AC-511, which is also where §8.8's unreconciled AC-511 id conflict comes from | **AC-213**, **AC-216**, **AC-217**, AC-203, AC-212, AC-236 |
| Errors, headroom and cancellation | `is_error` / `api_error_status` at **exit 0**; the `RateLimited` mark; headroom read only from `rate_limit_event`; cancellation as an explicit port call | **AC-218**, **AC-219**, **AC-220**, **AC-221**, AC-222, **AC-223**, AC-224, AC-225 | 
| Version pinning and fan-out | **CLI-version mismatch** against `TESTED_CLI_VERSION` (pinned at **2.1.263**, §8.4) attaches a typed warning to every cache metric; the fan-out stagger | **AC-226**, AC-228 (PROVISIONAL), AC-385 |
| Unverified capability | A capability with no dated probe is `Unsupported`, never assumed | **AC-238**, AC-489–AC-495 |

**Two reconciliations this table forces.** §8.1 marks **AC-428** PROVISIONAL for `is_error`/`api_error_status` on the grounds that those strings appear nowhere in the brief — **AC-218 in this area covers the same behaviour**, and the two must be reconciled in the area files before either is built. And the `--bare` / API-key assertions have homes in *three* areas (AC-216/AC-217 here, AC-488 in ports-dist, AC-422/AC-511 in cli-doctor), which is the same id collision §8.8 records unresolved. Measures SM-062, SM-065, SM-066, SM-067, SM-069 and SM-070 remain uncited by any section and are owed a home in the runbook.

The two rules that bear on the kernel's shape, restated because §3.6's layering depends on them, are that a tool-restricted card **silently** voids `--json-schema` at exit 0 with `subtype: success` [measured, F20], which is why `StructuredOutput` is a configuration-time check and nothing parses JSON out of prose (AC-031's sub-clause, AC-208), and that `--agents` is **inert without `--agent`** [measured, F7], which is why adoption is verified from the child's own report.

### 3.7 No durable-execution framework — the exclusion, re-argued

The conclusion is unchanged and the rationale is not. Two of the three facts the corpus rested it on were checked against live sources on 2026-09-08 and are **false**: `temporalio-sdk` on `temporalio-sdk-core` v0.5.0 is a live Public Preview updated June 2026, not the abandoned 2021 `temporal-sdk-core` 0.1.0-alpha.1; and `graph-flow` (v0.6) makes postgres an **optional** Cargo feature and ships an in-memory session store [documented, 1P crates.io / GitHub temporalio/sdk-rust, fetched 2026-09-08; evidence-augmentation §1 row 5, severity medium — "the conclusion stands, the rationale does not"]. The exclusion survives on facts that hold (ADR-0086): Temporal's Rust SDK still mandates a server or Temporal Cloud with no embedded mode found; graph-flow's in-memory mode does not survive a process restart and so is not *durable*, while its postgres feature reintroduces the server; and `iopsystems/durable` — new in this pass, never rejected by the charter — requires **Postgres**, crossing ADR-0003, **and** compilation of workflow code to WASM components via WASI. AWS's own SDK is not Rust at all. **One candidate was named in the research pass and not surveyed here:** whether LangGraph's per-node checkpointing works as described was read **only via a secondary aggregator**, so it is neither excluded nor admitted — the exclusion above stands on the four crates actually read, and LangGraph is recorded as an unread alternative rather than a rejected one [documented, research pass §5 unresolved]. It is not Rust either, which is why it does not gate the decision. What is kept from the frameworks is the ban list of §3.4, adopted as a convergent idiom. What is taken on is everything they would have supplied: the memo table, the replayable log, retry and backoff, and the test harness for all of it. The "~800 LOC" figure is a projection with no implementation behind it and is not quoted here as a measurement. ADR-0086 also registers one scoped open item — whether `temporalio-sdk` ships a time-skipping local test server, which would soften "mandates a server" **for local development and testing only** and does not reopen the exclusion. The crate graph that realises this is §9's.

### 3.8 The two changes the research forced

#### 3.8.1 Port traits are SYNC, and every `.await` is confined to an adapter (ADR-0076)

The corpus implied that a dyn-dispatched async port needed no `async-trait` under current Rust. The research pass contradicts that at severity **high**: native `async fn` in traits has been stable since 1.75 but is **still not dyn-compatible**, and `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` remains nightly-only — seen in nightly symbol tables through roughly 1.93–1.96 with no stabilisation announcement found [documented, 1P rust-lang.github.io/async-fundamentals-initiative + docs.rs/async-trait + [blog] debasishg.github.io, fetched 2026-09-08]. So "no generics leak into the kernel" and "dyn-dispatched port" are in direct tension the moment a port method is `async`. The corpus never recorded a choice; ADR-0076 records it.

| Option | What it costs | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `#[async_trait]` on every dyn port | Makes the kernel async — an executor inside a core whose defining property is deterministic replay, with the replay path then depending on task scheduling no event log records. One heap-allocated `Pin<Box<dyn Future>>` per call, on the one boundary every operation crosses. A proc-macro dependency in the compile graph of every crate that names a port | **Rejected.** And it does not buy what it appears to — see 3.8.2 |
| Generic (statically dispatched) ports with native `async fn` in trait | Exactly the generics leak ADR-0003 exists to prevent; monomorphises the kernel per adapter set; the fake/real swap stops being a value substitution and becomes a type-level one that spreads through every signature | **Rejected** (AC-480 is its instrument) |
| Wait for `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` | Nightly-only, no stabilisation announcement found, and the pass could not confirm the 1.97 line at all — building the Phase-1 kernel on it makes ADR-0083's toolchain pin a hostage | **Rejected** |
| **Sync port traits; `.await` in adapters** | Adapters block the calling thread, so a kernel operation fanning out to three ports takes the sum of their latencies unless the composition root introduces threads. The `Handle::block_on`-inside-a-runtime-thread hazard becomes an adapter-authoring rule enforced by review, not by the type system. Streaming responses must be consumed inside the adapter and surfaced as a completed value or an explicit sync callback. Timeouts move into adapters and can drift apart | **Adopted** |

The decision: **no port method is `async fn` and none returns a future; the kernel contains no `.await`, holds no runtime handle, and depends on no async runtime crate.** An adapter needing async I/O owns its runtime handle, constructed once at the composition root and never per call. Concurrency across ports is the composition root's job — expressed as threads or an adapter-internal concurrent call, with results entering the kernel as values in a defined order, so the kernel never observes interleaving.

**Why the kernel may not depend on a live future.** Nothing may depend on a process surviving: ~12 background-job kills against one 12,000 s survivor under `Start-Process`, and `issue-81` killed three times as a background job while completing in **116 s** in the foreground [measured, operational, F22; one machine, one operator, **cause still unestablished**]. F22 licenses "do not depend on a process surviving" and nothing about *why* they died. Every wait is therefore a durable mark plus a later read, not an in-flight future — the same rule that makes the human gate a mark rather than a waiting process (§6) and `RateLimited` a `retry_at` mark rather than a sleep (AC-030, AC-222). A replayable core has no business owning an executor: what makes F19's lossless suspension (174/174) and F38's mid-graph resume (3/3, digests identical) expressible as replay over an event log rather than as executor state is precisely that there is no executor state.

The working assumption is recorded as such: as of 2026-09-08 a dyn-compatible native async fn in trait does not exist on stable Rust, and the evidence is **search-only** — nobody read the Rust 1.97 release notes [evidence-augmentation §5 row 1]. The check that settles it is a direct read of the release notes for the version pinned in `rust-toolchain.toml` plus the `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` tracking issue, performed as part of the review of any toolchain-pin bump. That review is the named consumer. It does **not** gate Phase 1, because the decision is correct under either answer.

#### 3.8.2 Cancellation is an explicit port operation on a handle, never an implicit drop

`async-trait`'s implicit drop-cancellation buys nothing here, and this is the second half of why the first option loses. **Dropping a boxed future does not reap a spawned process tree.** An explicit kill is required under either option [inferred — standard Rust/OS semantics, stated as reasoning; ADR-0076 records it as reasoning and not as a citation], and F22 is the measured shape of the failure that follows from assuming otherwise.

So any port whose operations can be long-running exposes a cancel path taking a handle minted when the operation started, and the adapter implements it as a **real kill**: for the Harness adapter, a process-group kill of the spawned `claude -p` tree, with no descendant surviving the call — verified by an integration test that spawns a fixture forking a grandchild, cancels, and asserts no live PID in the group, run in CI as a regression against F22's 12,000 s survivor (AC-224).

**Counter-evidence, named rather than smoothed over.** The programme recorded a case the "no descendant survives the call" claim does not cover: **a killed `vitest` leaves a Windows handle that no `Stop-Process` releases**, and parallel arms consequently need separate fixture trees [documented, `spikes/HANDOFF.md`, "Three defects worth carrying forward" (3), issues #26/#30]. A process-group kill reaps *processes*; it does not release an OS handle the dead process left behind. So AC-224's assertion is **scoped to live PIDs in the group**, which is what it actually tests, and **SM-068's "zero survivors" target is a PID count and not a claim that the filesystem is clean afterwards**. The operational consequence is §7.7's: one worktree per concurrent story is a *requirement*, not an optimisation, because a shared fixture tree cannot be reclaimed after a killed run on Windows. A handle-leak check on the cancel path is owed and is registered as **S-CANCEL-WIN** (§10.4/§10.5). The outcome of the adapter's own cancel is `StepError::Cancelled` and never `NonZero(143)`, `Timeout` or `Schema`, with a negative test that an *externally* killed child does not silently classify as `Cancelled` (AC-225) [documented, platform docs: SIGTERM → 143, no result message]. A cancel that races a completion is resolved by the durable mark, not by the future's state.

SM-068 measures descendant PID count after cancel, with a target of zero survivors and zero dispatches whose result depends on a detached process. Its baseline is F22's incident record, not a rate, and its kill is that a survivor in production reopens F22's unestablished cause and blocks any design treating cancel as reclaiming the account's headroom.

### 3.9 Decisions this section records

| Decision | Alternatives considered | Why this one | Evidence | ADR |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Clean-break single Rust binary owning the loop | keep the JS orchestrator; embed the loop in a harness plugin | **No difference was detectable, and the instrument could not have detected one** — detection **23/23 vs 23/23**, McNemar **p=0.625**, in-session **1.72×** the cost ($97.39 vs $56.47). The primary metric **saturated**: the result is "no discriminating power", not "headless wins on quality"; 20/20 across two harder tiers, blind and sighted — **saturated in all four cells, so no separation was detectable there either** | [measured, F23, F26] | ADR-0001, ADR-0002 |
| Five named ports, sync traits, one real adapter and one fake each; no Postgres adapter | ports at every seam; no ports at all; generic ports | A port exists where a substitute is named or the fake alone pays for it; the fake is what lets the kernel be tested at $0 (AC-498) | [documented] proposal §4.1 port table; AC-481 | ADR-0003, ADR-0076 |
| Git is not a port | a `Vcs` port with a gix and a shell adapter | The git surface has measured behaviour and no alternative VCS is named | [measured, F12, F19] | ADR-0003 |
| Port traits stay sync; `.await` confined to adapters; explicit cancel | `async-trait` on every dyn port; generic ports with native async fn; wait for nightly | Native async fn in trait is not dyn-compatible; a replayable core must not own an executor; a dropped future does not reap a process tree | [documented, 1P async-fundamentals-initiative, fetched 2026-09-08] + [measured, F22] | **ADR-0076** |
| No durable-execution framework; the memo table is built here | Temporal Rust SDK; graph-flow; `iopsystems/durable`; AWS Durable Execution SDK | Server mandate / not durable in-memory / Postgres + WASM / not Rust — re-argued after two of the corpus's three supporting facts were found false | [documented, 1P crates.io + GitHub, fetched 2026-09-08] | **ADR-0086** |
| Determinism ban list as a build failure, not a convention | rely on review; rely on a runtime to detect at replay | No runtime enforces it here, so a violation would be a latent silent memoisation bug; the list is convergent, independently re-derived by AWS | [documented, 1P AWS + Temporal, fetched 2026-09-08] | ADR-0022, ADR-0086 |
| Content-addressed, loop-aware memo key, per step | activity-level key only; iteration-blind key | An iteration-blind key makes loops unable to learn; a single-card activity key misses subgraph changes | [documented, P9b finding A18 — **no n, no figure**] | ADR-0021, ADR-0043→ADR-0059 |
| Three budgets, never shared; layered retry; typed degradation | one shared budget; a global retry count; silent substitution | Independence is a type-level guarantee; configuration failures are not weather | [measured, F7, F8, F20] for the never-retried classes | ADR-0037, ADR-0022 |
