## 5. Typed artifacts, the store, and durable state

### 5.1 The direction of travel: types render to markdown, and markdown never travels back

Every activity output is a Rust type; the harness receives its schema, the kernel validates the value at the boundary, and the store **renders** markdown from the value. No code path recovers a typed field from rendered prose. The rule is not stylistic — it is what the corpus census forces, and it is enforced by dependency reachability rather than by discipline.

| Rule | What it forbids | Instrument (all to be built — `crates/` is empty and there is no CI) | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Markdown is an output of the type, never an input to control flow | any store export that takes `&str` markdown and returns a typed field; any markdown-parsing crate reachable from store code outside `store::locator` | module-boundary architecture test; `cargo deny` / `cargo tree --invert` rule; exported-items test — **AC-120** | S-MIG: gate results free-form in **39/156** implementation reports and **19/23** reviews, so there is nothing to parse [measured, one corpus] · ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0075 |
| Rendering is a pure function of (typed value, template version) | a renderer that reads the prior section text; non-deterministic output across OS or working directory | golden-file test on both CI targets; `proptest` over every artifact type; a differential render over two prior contents; a sentinel-erasure unit test — **AC-121**, **AC-123** | design rule, proposal §4.4 [documented]. **Precondition:** AC-121's cross-OS half is checkable only if CI runs two OS targets; with one target it is unverified and is marked so, not assumed |
| One write path: only `rk-store` writes bytes under a managed artifact tree | a filesystem-write handle to the artifact tree exported from `rk-store`; an out-of-band edit that leaves no record | architecture test on exported items plus a `trybuild` compile-fail case — **AC-122**; digest-comparison `doctor` rule with a control fixture — **AC-159** | proposal §4.5 rule 4 [documented]. AC-159's instrument is a content digest, **not mtime**: a clone or a format-on-save advances mtime with identical content, and an mtime-preserving write hides a real mutation |
| The locator is a locator, not a grammar | fuzzy, first-match or nearest-match anchor resolution; any typed field populated from sliced bytes | signature test that the return carries a byte range and no content-derived field; adversarial decoy corpus (fenced block, indented block, HTML comment, blockquote); zero-match and duplicate-match error tests — **AC-149**, **AC-150**, **AC-151** | proposal §4.4 "a heading-anchored slice, not a grammar" [documented]; F33's lint that "accepted 5 guesses silently" is the failure mode being avoided [measured] |
| Opaque prose is accepted whole | any store path that inspects, validates, lints or summarises prose content | integration test writing arbitrary bytes (headings included) into a prose section and asserting acceptance — **AC-152** | S-MIG: everything designated opaque-by-design (3,100+ sections) lifted opaque with no exceptions — and S-MIG itself calls this the trivial half of its result [measured] |
| An edit inside a rendered structured section is typed drift — surfaced, never applied | applying a rendered-section edit to a typed value; re-importing edited text through a parser or a narrow LLM step | mutate-a-section integration test asserting a `structured_drift` event and an unchanged typed value; re-render equality after the drift record exists — **AC-124**, **AC-125**, **AC-153** | proposal §4.4, ADR-0005, ADR-0006 [documented] |
| Vouched roots and template bumps are different drift kinds | reporting a human-supplied root's change as blocking; reporting a template-version bump as human drift; silently re-stamping | table-driven test over {vouched root, rendered section} × {changed, unchanged} — **AC-127**; template-version drift kind test with the template version inside the section digest input — **AC-128** | ADR-0025 (vouched roots) [documented]; the old redkiln's `template_sig`/`rendered_sig` drift is the named failure to avoid [documented] |

**What the census can and cannot carry.** Every corpus share above comes from S-MIG — **1,312 files / 6,877 sections at corpus pin `47faec9f`, zero model cost** — and S-MIG has **no F-number**; it is cited by name. Its own instrument disclosure travels with every figure: the lifters were iterated four times against this corpus before the recorded run, over one repository at one frozen SHA, so these are shares under a locator tuned to the corpus, **not a blind parse**, and no interval is computed on any of them [measured, one corpus]. The quantity this subsection's central rule would most like measured — how often humans actually edit a rendered structured section — **has never been measured at all**. SM-037 establishes it on first use; F19's 12/174 = 7% is *world* drift under a human gate, a different quantity, and is cited as the nearest neighbour only. SM-037's kill is a per-section structured-drift rate that has **not fallen after 20 items**, which moves that field to opaque prose or to a CLI command.

### 5.2 Layout C, at the revision-4 refinements

Layout C — typed frontmatter, plus rendered read-only digest-stamped structured sections, plus declared opaque prose sections; machine-only artifacts as JSON sidecars rendered to `.md` on demand (**AC-144**) — stands unchanged from ADR-0006. ADR-0075 withdraws two clauses of ADR-0005/ADR-0006 and refines the rest against the census. Both predecessors stay `accepted`; this is a partial supersession, and each clause below is a type decision the census forced, not a parser defect.

| # | Refinement (ADR-0075) | Measured basis [measured, S-MIG unless noted] | What pins it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The AC type is `{ id, criterion: text, gwt: Option<Gwt>, verification: Option<text> }` — permissive by construction | **19 of 153 shipped specs (12%)** write criteria with no GIVEN/WHEN/THEN (45 ACs) or no verification note (46 ACs); acceptance criteria reclassify at **88%** strict against a 95% pre-registration | **AC-137** (minimal value accepted, empty slots rendered visibly empty and never fabricated) |
| 1a | The **policy**, not the type, decides what a missing `verification` blocks — per stage, per rigor | policy relaxation was free in F31 (lenient 9/23 blocked, detection unchanged) — **but that is measured against CLEAN, whose ground truth is wrong for ≥13 of 23 (57%), so it is an upper bound and never a point estimate** | **AC-138** (table-driven {blocks, advises} × {present, absent}; the block is computed from the policy row, not a constant in the type) |
| 1b | The Phase-1 **default** for that row is a recorded human decision, not a spike result | no instrument discriminates the two defaults; F37's nearest spike is **powerless at n=3** — every Wilson interval overlaps by construction [measured] | **AC-139**, routed to a human gate and left there; the machine-checkable consequence is that the value is a data change (AC-138) |
| 1c | An AC with no per-AC discriminating test renders as **unpinned**, never as a pass | F25's AC-015 probe: removing step 7 turned the suite RED, while inverting the ordering left **36 of 36 GREEN** [measured, n=1 AC, one operator] | **AC-140** (the rendered token for "no discriminating test" is distinct from every pass token) |
| 2 | Gate results are **emitted by the toolchain and rendered**, never parsed | free-form in **39/156** reports (25%) and **19/23** reviews (83%) — note the 23-file denominator; the review gate table reclassifies at **17%**, so there is no grammar to find | **AC-141** (gate-outcome constructor reachable only from the toolchain adapter; a report whose prose says "all gates passed" with no typed record renders no passing gate) |
| 2a | A missing emission is a **typed error against the activity**, never a rendered outcome | F20: an agent card's `tools` allowlist silently voids `--json-schema` — `structured_output` absent, **exit 0**, `subtype: success` [measured] | **AC-142** (absent is never rendered as pass, fail, or unknown-treated-as-pass) |
| 3 | **One sidecar per companion kind**, each with its own registered schema — never a shared union | companion frontmatter lifted at only **19/45 (42%)**, below the 80% threshold, and companions are three shapes, not one; 10 of 28 carry no frontmatter at all | **AC-143** (registry-completeness test; a companion kind with no schema cannot be written). **Open:** proposal §4.4, its machine-only list and the S-MIG census name **different companion sets** — the runbook settles the list, and until it does AC-143 cannot be verified against it |
| 4 | **`plan.md` does not exist** — removed from the human-facing set, unreadable if found | **110 of 110** were the unfilled `1.` scaffold with template prose; planning lived in `_plan.md`, `_storymap.md` and `spec.md` | **AC-145** (no producer declares it, no template renders it, a `plan.md` in a managed directory is reported as unmanaged and never parsed) |
| 5 | Every activity input emits an **`artifact.read`** event carrying kind, item id, `schema_version` and the tree hash the read was served at | the pathology this event class exists to expose is clause 4 — an artifact nobody read, invisible for the life of the corpus because nothing instruments downstream reads. **[inferred]**: S-MIG measured that `plan.md` was the unfilled scaffold in **110/110** cases, which establishes that a dead artifact existed and was never filled; that nothing instrumented *reads* is a property of the old system asserted from the absence of any such event, not a measured share, and §5.11 separately records that the `doctor` threshold is unset by measurement | **AC-146** ("an event", not "exactly one" — proposal §4.4 licenses emission, not uniqueness); **AC-147** (`doctor` lists produced-but-never-read stages) |
| 6 | Three section kinds fall below the **80% reclassify threshold** and are carried as **opaque prose**, located and rendered verbatim | implementation-report gate sections **75%**, companion frontmatter **42%**, review gate table **17%** — against the census **overall** of 98%. ("Spec sections" is its own S-MIG slot, also at 98%, and does **not** contain impl-report gates, companion frontmatter or the review gate table; the figure that averages over those three is the overall, as §8.7 states) | ADR-0075 clause 6. Typing a below-threshold kind is forbidden; so is silently dropping one |
| 7 | Per-AC mutation evidence is a first-class column; `unknown` is a distinguishable value | the column exists in **0 of 156** historical implementation reports; TDD evidence migrates `mutation: unknown` on 142 of 156 rows | ADR-0075 clause 7 — forbids rendering `unknown` as a pass, a blank or an omission. SM-044 targets the 0/156 moving off zero and explicitly does **not** target the 12% to zero |
| 8 | Gate-checked criteria are phrased as **EARS** single-trigger statements by preference; `gwt` stays optional | phrasing preference only, from the research pass [documented; Mavin et al., IEEE RE'09] — **no enforcement, and no measurement on this corpus** | ADR-0075 clause 8. The corpus and the research pass disagree here and both are acted on: the *type* stays permissive because the census requires it, the *phrasing* moves to EARS because the external evidence is about checkability |

**Two things this layout deliberately does not settle.** First, `doctor`'s produced-unread flag **never removes an artifact from a required set** — removal is a human pack edit, recorded as such, because the selection engine's context-vector source is **BLOCKED** (F36: the kill fired 4 of 4, modal `R` identical on only 5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20 **items** — each computed across all ten draws of that item — against an 80% line; ADR-0079, status `proposed`. Arm D has since been **scored**: no derived facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar and `auth` scores **0.00** [0.00, 0.26] over eleven positives [measured, F39], which discharges AC-071's fourth clause only and opens a second block — on the *derived* source — under ADR-0089). **AC-148** is written precisely under that block, and SM-038's kill says the same thing: an artifact kind never read across 20 items is a *candidate* for removal, never an automatic one. Second, the `doctor` threshold in clause 5 — "zero reads over N runs" — is **not set by any measurement**; S-MIG establishes that a dead artifact existed and was invisible, not how many runs of silence should flag one [inferred]. SM-038 carries it as a rolling 20-item window pending a first measurement.

**`ChangeSummary` is blocked here, and stays blocked.** The type that describes a change no diff renders is gated on **S-BIN — registered and UNRUN** — against the real Unreal project (ADR-0008, whose own text records that S-BIN had not been run). **AC-154 pins the rendering direction only** (binary entries render from typed fields; no binary content is embedded, parsed or diffed as text) and is **unverifiable until S-BIN runs** — its fixture cannot be written, and it must not be counted as covered in the runbook. Nothing in this section freezes that type's field set. S-BIN gates Phase 1 for it; see §10.

### 5.3 Schema design rules, and the golden snapshot that checks them

| Rule | Status | Instrument | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `additionalProperties: false` on every object node | design rule, externally corroborated | CI schema lint over the exported set; an open-object fixture that must fail — **AC-129** | proposal §4.4 [documented]; the vendor's published structured-output limitations require it on all objects and reject any other value [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08]. F20 is **motivation, not evidence**: it is a tool-allowlist defect, not a statement about `additionalProperties` |
| Every categorical field is a **closed enum with an explicit absent/unknown variant**; no bare `String` standing in for a category | design rule | enumerated-domain registry vs exported schema; a free-`String` fixture that must fail — **AC-130** | proposal §4.4 [documented]; F36's mechanism as motivation — `unknown` is treated as `high` by position, so an axis oscillating unknown↔low moves the band its full width [measured] |
| **No `oneOf`/`anyOf` nested inside another** | design rule — **and, as of the research pass, a constraint on the Rust types rather than a library guarantee** | schema lint walking the exported tree; a nested-union fixture — **AC-131**; plus the golden snapshot below | proposal §4.4 [documented]; **schemars 1.2.2 derives `oneOf` for data-carrying enum variants by default**, matching serde's externally tagged representation, and `#[serde(tag = "kind")]` renders a single discriminated object instead [documented, 1P docs.rs/schemars, fetched 2026-09-08; ADR-0084] |
| ≤ 24 optional fields per type; ≤ 16 union branches at any node; required properties before optional ones | **house rules, not vendor caps** | property-count lint with a 25-optional fixture — **AC-132**; branch-count lint with a 17-branch fixture — **AC-157**; property-order lint — **AC-158** | proposal §4.4's parenthetical carries no citation and the vendor's published limitations contain **no such caps and no ordering requirement** [documented]. AC-157 also discloses that proposal §4.4 says "union *fields*" while the criterion checks *branches*. **Precondition:** AC-158 is observable only if the export preserves insertion order (`serde_json` `preserve_order`); without it the criterion is unverifiable and the runbook must say so |
| A free-text `rationale` beside every judgement-bearing enum, and **every** shipped enum registered as judgement-bearing or not | design rule | registry-completeness test (an unregistered enum fails the lint) plus a rationale-sibling rule — **AC-133** | proposal §4.4 [documented]; F29 as motivation — 19/19 rejections were on target while class means wobbled (Reproduced 0.70 > MutationSurvived 0.37 > NarrowedPin 0.10 > control 0.00) [measured]. **Limit:** the lint cannot tell whether the yes/no is *right*; that is the human gate AC-156 |
| Enum values are compared case-insensitively and stored normalised | design rule | per-enum casing unit tests plus a persisted-form store test — **AC-134** | proposal §4.4 [documented]. The brief carries **no casing measurement** — F31's 52/52 was not stratified by casing [inferred] |
| A `schema_version` per type; a schema change is a **migration** | design rule | schema-snapshot diff that fails an unversioned change, with the previous version's snapshot retained — **AC-135**; named missing-migration error on read, never best-effort deserialisation — **AC-136**; SM-046 counts unmigratable artifacts, target zero at every release tag | proposal §4.4 [documented]; the old redkiln's `template_sig`/`rendered_sig` drift is the named failure. **Limit:** a semantics-only change that leaves the schema byte-identical passes AC-135 — the snapshot sees field sets, not meaning. Say so in the runbook rather than claiming coverage |

**None of the rules above is self-enforcing, and the instrument that enforces them is the golden-schema snapshot test (ADR-0084).** Every type crossing the closed-output boundary has its generated `schemars` output snapshotted, and the test asserts on the generated JSON that it contains no `oneOf` anywhere, that every object carries `additionalProperties: false`, that required properties precede optional ones, and that the optional-field and union-branch budgets hold; data-carrying enums that must cross the boundary are written `#[serde(tag = "kind")]`, and a changed snapshot **is** a schema change and is reviewed as one. The failure class it guards is F20's — closed-output violations fail **open**, at exit 0 with `subtype: success`, so there is no loud failure for a careful author to notice [measured, F20].

**The test itself, its pins, its three disclosures and its CI wiring are §9.5's; this section owns only the constraint on the types.** Two of those disclosures bear on how the rules above may be read and are named here rather than assumed: the optional-field and union-branch budgets are **this project's own** and must not be described as enforcing a documented vendor limit, and F31's schema conformance of **52/52 = 100%** measures that *emitted payloads conformed to a schema* and says nothing about whether the *generated schema* satisfies these constraints [measured, F31; ADR-0084].

### 5.4 Durable state: the tier-1 test

Two substrates, deliberately redundant, split by one question: **if the database were deleted, would `redkiln run` take a different next action?** If yes, the state may not live only in the database.

| Tier | Contents | Substrate | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Tier 1 — decision-bearing** | stage transitions, gate opened/answered, verdicts, slice seals | **git first**: trailers on a checkpoint commit, or an **empty commit** carrying the trailer when no checkpoint is due | **AC-160** (a state field with no `tier1`/`tier2` annotation is a compile error, witnessed by a `trybuild` fixture — not a silent default); **AC-161** (delete the database; the selected next action is equal under the canonical serialiser, with no manual repair); **AC-162** (the trailer commit exists before any store row for that transition is readable, and a failed git write leaves **zero** store rows — fault-injection, not a structural lint); **AC-163** (empty commit, never a store-only record) |
| **Tier 2 — telemetry** | usage, cost, headroom, wall clock, drift observations, inner retries | event store only; rebuildable or disposable | **AC-164** (truncate tier 2: the next action is unchanged and `doctor` classifies the loss as **telemetry-only**, never as corruption); the rest of the tier-2 contract is **AC-365–AC-370** and **AC-397–AC-398** in `ac-events-cost.md` §1.2 — rebuildability, disposability, the rebuild-from-git path and what a partial tier-2 loss may and may not change — with **AC-363** on degradation and **AC-380** naming `rate_limit_event` as the **sole** headroom source, and **SM-115**/**SM-116** as the tier's own measures. They are cited, not restated; §7.4's attribution table is their point of use. F6's 0.01 `utilization` quantisation — per-run cost is not measurable — is *why* cost sits here [measured; the cost model itself is §7's] |

**Two trailer axes, because one is provably insufficient.** `Story:`-trailer resume alone loses the rejection history: over **360 trailer commits across 74 slices**, **15/74 = 20.3%** of slices passed through a rejected state and 21.6% were sealed more than once [measured, F12; F12's registered primary was uncomputable at n=1 and that re-scoping is disclosed]. A `Slice-Verdict:` axis is therefore required (ADR-0018). The third axis, `Gate:`, is written when a mark is opened; its record semantics — `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` and the terminal expiry path — are §6's, and ADR-0017 enters here only at that boundary. A trailer that does not parse against the declared schema is surfaced as drift with the offending text attached, and is **never skipped and never coerced into a state transition** (**AC-165**).

**The limit on all of it, stated once.** F19's **174/174** is losslessness of *suspension*, and F19 explicitly **never replayed the FSM** — so "the same next action" is **unproven**, not proven [measured]. **AC-188** is the instrument written to close it (replay the deterministic workflow from the log, assert sequence equality, and deny clock/RNG/filesystem symbols in the workflow module with a fixture that must fail), and **AC-161 is only as strong as AC-188**. Until AC-188 exists, SM-056 reports **byte-recoverability, not decision-equivalence**, and this document claims no more. F38 adds mid-graph step granularity — **0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical** across a real `taskkill /T /F` — at **n=3, PARTIAL** (3 draws of a pre-registered 27+3, $3.01 of a $40 cap; all three no-kill controls unrun) [measured, F38].

### 5.5 Keying, orphans, and rewrite events

Every repo-referencing row is keyed by SHA or tree hash — never a branch, never a path. Rebase, reset, amend and force-push therefore produce **orphaned** rows, and an orphan is a signal, not a defect to be tidied away.

| Rule | Criterion | Evidence / source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every repo-referencing key column declares a key domain of `sha` or `tree`; anything else is a build failure | **AC-166** — schema lint over the migration files, with a `branch TEXT` key fixture that must fail. (A lint cannot recognise "a branch name" by inspection; a declared domain makes it decidable) | proposal §4.5 rule 2 [documented]; redkiln's existing verify cache already keys on `(cmd, base, tree)` |
| Orphans are **reported, never deleted and never re-anchored as a side effect of the report** | **AC-167** (orphan set equals the expected anchors; row count unchanged before and after); **AC-168** (no `DELETE` against a tier-1 table exists outside `doctor --rebuild`, and adding one fails the build) | ADR-0018 "surfaced, never deleted" [documented] |
| A successor is adopted only on a **recorded mechanism** — patch-id equality, `ORIG_HEAD`, or a reflog entry the operation itself wrote — and the re-anchor is preceded by a `rewrite(old_sha → new_sha, mechanism)` event that retains the old anchor | **AC-169** (a re-anchor with no preceding event fails) | ADR-0018 [documented] |
| Where no mechanism identifies a successor, the row **stays orphaned and drift-visible**; no successor is inferred from branch name, file path, commit message or timestamp proximity | **AC-170** — negative test whose rewritten commit shares message and path set but differs in patch-id: zero `rewrite` events, one surviving orphan | ADR-0018 [documented] |
| An orphan that blocks the next action routes to a **human gate** carrying the orphan set, both SHAs and the diff | **AC-171** — routed to a human gate; the machine-checkable half is only that the gate *opens*, never a silent continuation | **No finding in the brief measures this decision.** "Is this the same work" is a judgement about intent; a rebase that also edits content produces no patch-id match |

SM-050 has **no baseline**: the corpus offers only a different quantity — F12's 20.3% ever-rejected and 21.6% resealed, which is a **rejection/reseal rate, not a history-rewrite rate**, and must not be read as a bound on either [measured, F12]. Its target is that every orphan resolves to a `rewrite` event or an opened gate, with the count of orphans that are neither at **0**, and its kill is a steady-state orphan population growing monotonically over 10 consecutive entries — the sign that "surfaced, never deleted" is producing an unusable backlog rather than an audit trail.

### 5.6 `reconcile()` at every entry, git → store, never the reverse

| Property | Criterion | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every CLI entry that can cause an action obtains decision-driving state **only** as the output of `reconcile()` for that invocation | **AC-172** — the planner accepts only a `Reconciled<State>` value, so the compile failure is the instrument; plus exactly one `reconcile_started` event per invocation | F19: drift **12/174 = 7%**, **9/79 = 11%** on gaps over 1 h against **3/95 = 3%** at or under [measured, n=174, **one operator, one repository**]; F18: **45–48%** of gates exceed 1 h [measured, one operator] |
| No cache, TTL, "unchanged since" flag, configuration or CLI flag skips it | **AC-173** — CLI surface snapshot test; `reconcile_started` on every entry regardless of elapsed time | brief §C.3 forces reconcile on every entry and forbids an optimisation that skips it [measured]. **Note what is *not* claimed: no measurement of `reconcile()`'s own cost exists anywhere in the brief** — the frequency of drift is measured, the price of catching it is not |
| Reconcile mutates nothing in git | **AC-174** — runs to completion against a read-only git dir, with a before/after digest comparison of the git dir | proposal §4.5 rule 3 [documented] |
| Reconcile is idempotent | **AC-175** — `reconcile(reconcile(w)) == reconcile(w)` over corpus histories, with an empty second drift set | proposal §4.5 rule 3 [documented] |
| A gate answer bound to a tree hash that no longer matches is **void**, and the gate re-opens with the drift attached | **AC-176** — the reconcile-side half only; the gate record's own semantics are §6's | F19 + F18 as above; ADR-0017 at the boundary |

**Honest reading of the 3.6×.** SM-049's Wilson 95% intervals are overall [4.0%, 11.7%], ≤1 h [1.1%, 8.9%] and >1 h [6.1%, 20.3%] — **the two conditional intervals overlap**, so 3.6× is a point estimate on one operator's single repository, not a separated effect [measured, F19; intervals recomputed in the AC pass]. SM-049 also carries an explicit detection rule, without which its "100% of drift caught at entry" target could never fail: drift counts as *missed* when a later `reconcile()` records an observation whose causing commit predates an action already taken. The claim "none was missed in 174" does not exist in the brief and is not made here — F19 reports 12 drift events and 174/174 lossless suspension, and neither is an oracle for drift the system failed to see.

### 5.7 One store per repository, at the common dir

| Rule | Criterion | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The store resolves to `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`/redkiln/ and is shared by every worktree; **no second store file exists anywhere under a linked worktree** | **AC-178** — N worktrees, one store path, a recursive scan finding no store file in any worktree | proposal §4.5 rule 1 [documented]; redkiln's locks already live there |
| A store opened against a **different repository** (root commit + common dir identity) is **refused** with a typed error naming both identities, reading and writing nothing | **AC-179** — fixture store plus a second repository, asserting the error variant and an unchanged file digest | proposal §4.5 rule 5 [documented — Backlog.md #558; external reference, not in the evidence brief] |
| A store at an older schema version is refused with the required migration named; **no implicit in-place migration** | **AC-180** — checked-in fixture store at version N−1, bytes unchanged after the refused open | proposal §4.5 rule 5 [documented] |
| Two processes in two worktrees both land their writes, with no lock error and one store file | **AC-181** — two-process integration test. (The observable is both rows present; WAL and `busy_timeout` are implementation. SQLite serialises writers, so the design is **one writer connection, never a pool** — §9 owns that) | proposal §4.7 [documented] |
| **No git hooks feed the store.** Nothing is written under `hooks/`, and the product behaves identically where the hooks directory is absent or read-only | **AC-182** — source lint plus a run against a hooks-less repository asserting identical output | git-branchless's `reference-transaction` approach cannot be made atomic and forces full-log replays (its issue #1334) [documented, external]. ADR-0018's own evidence note records that #1334 "is about hooks, not worktrees", so it licenses **this** rule and not the compile-time export boundary of AC-122 |
| An out-of-band git operation in a plain shell is reflected by `reconcile()` alone — no notification, daemon or hook | **AC-183** — shell out to `git` between two invocations, run with the hooks directory removed, asserting no child process outlives the first invocation | F19 [measured] |

**Rebuild from a clean clone is a CI test, not a recovery procedure.** `redkiln doctor --rebuild` over a clean clone with no store present must produce tier-1 state **byte-identical under the canonical serialiser** to the tier-1 projection of the original store, consuming only the clone — a store file planted at the target path is not read (**AC-184**). The test runs in CI on every commit against the pinned `redkiln-corpus` revision (ADR-0013), includes an adversarial-store variant that must produce identical output, and asserts that the corpus pin is a 40-hex SHA and not a branch. **SM-051 has no baseline: nothing in the brief measures a rebuild.** The corpus (F12's 360 trailer commits over 74 slices) is the substrate the first measurement will run against, and nothing more; a claim that a rebuild implementation has already been validated against it is not supported by the brief and is not made here. SM-051's kill is sharp and is the tier test read backwards: **a non-zero divergence means tier-1 state is not fully in git**, and the offending field moves or is re-tiered. SM-056 runs the same differential on a schedule against a live repository, subject to the AC-188 limit in §5.4.

### 5.8 Resume: one idempotent entry point

| Property | Criterion | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `redkiln run <item>` is the sole entry for a first run and a resumed run; **no `--resume`, `--continue` or equivalent flag exists on any command** | **AC-186** — `clap` surface snapshot test that must be updated deliberately to add a flag | proposal §4.5 (L2.3) [documented] |
| A committed step is not re-dispatched — its memo key resolves — and the next action equals, under the canonical serialiser, the action an uninterrupted control run took at the same point | **AC-187** — memo hit, zero duplicate dispatch events, equality against a recorded control trace | F38: 3/3 across a real `taskkill /T /F` [measured, **n=3, PARTIAL**]. **PROVISIONAL at long durations:** P8's 65-minute and 24-hour arms are unrun, and **all three of its controls are unrun**, so the control trace this criterion compares against is a product-test artefact, not something the spike measured |
| Every output produced before a kill is present afterwards with an unchanged digest, and none is rewritten by the resumed run | **AC-185** — per-artefact digest capture before the kill, equality after | F38: **0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical** [measured, n=3, PARTIAL] |

SM-052 records mid-graph resume at **8,408 / 10,314 / 14,579** `cache_creation` tokens — **0.45× / 0.55× / 0.78×** of the 18,603-token stage-gate resume, against a pre-registered kill at >1.5× that **did not fire at n=3, where the absence means little** [measured, F38]. Two disclosures travel with that ratio wherever it is used: **all three P8 runs sat inside F10's ~1 h TTL** (measured `gap_minutes` 0.9, 28.4, 29.1, notwithstanding the `0-delay` arm label), and there is **no within-experiment baseline** because the controls are unrun — the comparison is only to F28. The target "at most one prefix rewrite per resume, invariant in the length of the interruption" is therefore **currently unfalsifiable in this programme's data** and stays open until P8's 65-minute and 24-hour arms run (ADR-0087; §10). Gate *placement* is a value decision and belongs to §6; the cache mechanics behind these token figures belong to §7.

### 5.9 The gix restriction — a Phase-1 risk with a decided fallback (ADR-0077)

Git is deliberately not a port (§3), which makes *how* `rk-git` talks to git a real architecture decision with no adapter to swap. The research pass withdrew two capabilities the store design had assumed.

| Direction | Owner | Operations | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Read** | `gix`, at the exact pin `=0.87.1` | `rev-parse` (including resolving the common dir AC-178 requires at startup), repository and worktree status, **shallow clone by SHA** for review checkouts, **trailer parsing** | Shallow clone at a specific commit is supported [documented, 1P gitoxide discussion #2309 — one thread, not a docs guarantee]. Nothing contradicts the read-side bet |
| **Write** | a **process-exec wrapper** invoking the `git` executable | `git worktree add` / `git worktree remove`; `git interpret-trailers --in-place` and/or `git commit --trailer` for **every** trailer write | gitoxide's own `crate-status.md`: "the delicate interplay between `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` isn't implemented" — **precisely the mechanism one-store-at-the-common-dir leans on** [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 2, severity **high**]. Separately, `crate-status.md` documents trailer **parsing**, and no first-class trailer-**writing** API was found — **but the pass could not fetch a definitive docs.rs listing either way, so the absence is UNVERIFIED, not proven** [documented; §1 row 8, severity medium; §5 names it "the highest-value single check in the list"] |

The constraints that come with the hybrid: **all** shelling out to git goes through **one wrapper type inside `rk-git`** — no `std::process::Command` for git exists anywhere else in the workspace; git's stderr is diagnostic payload and is **never parsed for control flow**; the wrapper asserts at startup (not at first use) that `git` is on `PATH` at or above a minimum version, **and that minimum is not yet established, so the assertion is incomplete until the wrapper is built**. Two git implementations in one process can disagree — notably about config resolution and Windows path handling — so anything read by gix and then written by the executable is **re-read after the write, never assumed**. Restricting gix to reads also moves it away from `gix-worktree-state` checkout permissions, `gix-worktree` path traversal and Windows reserved-device-name handling, where its advisory history clusters and which is exactly the disposable-worktree surface a merge queue exercises hardest; all of those are **fixed at or before 0.87.1, so the pin is CLEAN as of 2026-09-08** [documented, GitHub Advisory Database / RustSec, fetched 2026-09-08]. §9 owns the pin, the `cargo audit` job and the bus-factor notes.

**What this section does not say.** It does not say gix can write a trailer, and it does not say gix cannot: ADR-0077 forbids treating documented parsing support as evidence of writing support, and equally forbids narrowing the record on a docs read alone. The unknown is discharged as a named spike with a named consumer (ADR-0012):

| Spike | Question | Consumer | Gates Phase 1? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **S-GIX/a** | Against the pinned `gix = "=0.87.1"`, can a store rooted at the git common dir be opened and written correctly from a linked worktree — does the `GIT_COMMON_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` interplay behave, or is `crate-status.md` still accurate? | `rk-git` worktree lifecycle | **No** — the shell-out fallback is decided; it gates only the *narrowing* |
| **S-GIX/b** | Does the pinned gix expose a first-class trailer-**writing** API, and does a five-line unit test round-trip a trailer byte-identically to `git interpret-trailers --in-place`? | `rk-git` trailer write path (**tier-1 state**) | **No**, same reason |

A **go** on an arm may narrow ADR-0077 and nothing else may; the narrowing is written as a successor record, never as an edit. Both rows appear in §10's register.

### 5.10 Decisions

| Decision | Alternatives considered and lost | Why the alternative lost | ADR | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Layout **C** — typed frontmatter + rendered read-only structured sections + opaque prose; machine-only artifacts as JSON sidecars | A: sidecar `spec.json` canonical + generated `.md`; B: embedded canonical payload in a trailing fenced block with a digest | A gives two files per artifact in every git review; B's re-import path "is a place structure can rot back in". **Neither was benchmarked against C on any metric** — this is a design choice with a census behind its *refinements*, not behind the layout itself | ADR-0006, ADR-0075 | proposal §4.4 [documented]; S-MIG for the refinements [measured] |
| The AC type is permissive; the **policy** decides what a missing verification blocks | A strict `{gwt, verification}` grammar, failing migration on anything else | It fails **19/153 = 12%** of shipped, reviewed specs at the type boundary, converting a policy question into a migration error with no way to answer it differently per stage or rigor | ADR-0075 cl. 1 | S-MIG [measured, one corpus] |
| Gate results are emitted typed by the toolchain and rendered | Parse the gate prose with a grammar, or re-import it through a narrow LLM activity | At **17%** reclassify on the review gate table there is no grammar to find, and LLM re-import is the exact structure-from-markdown path ADR-0006 forbids | ADR-0075 cl. 2 | S-MIG [measured] |
| One schema per companion kind | A single union schema across `_integration`, `_grounding`, `_decomposition`, `_ledger` | They are three shapes; a union collapses to a mostly-optional record that validates almost nothing, and drags `oneOf` nesting into the type set | ADR-0075 cl. 3 | S-MIG: companion frontmatter **19/45 = 42%** [measured] |
| `plan.md` is removed | Keep it and treat 110/110 unfilled as a discipline problem | An artifact never filled once across a whole corpus is unwanted, not under-used, and keeping it costs a schema, a template, a locator and a stage output | ADR-0075 cl. 4 | S-MIG **110/110** [measured] |
| Three below-threshold section kinds are carried as **opaque prose** | Type them anyway; or drop them from the migration | Typing a kind that lifts at 17% manufactures structure that is not there; dropping it loses corpus content silently | ADR-0075 cl. 6 | 75% / 42% / 17% against an 80% threshold [measured] |
| A **golden-schema snapshot test** enforces the closed-output rules | Trust `schemars` to produce closed, flat schemas; or rely on author discipline | schemars 1.2.2 emits `oneOf` for **data-carrying enum variants by default**; the rule binds the Rust types, not the library. And an author-discipline rule is violated by the first payload-carrying enum, silently — F20's class fails open at exit 0 | ADR-0084 | [documented, 1P docs.rs/schemars]; F20 [measured] |
| Tier 1 to **git first**, tier 2 to the event store, split by the delete-the-database test | A single durable store; or trailers as a derived projection of the database | The test is the whole point: state that changes the next action may not live only where a `rm` removes it. F12 additionally shows a single `Story:` axis is insufficient | ADR-0018 | F12 **20.3%** ever-rejected over 360 commits / 74 slices [measured] |
| Rows keyed by SHA/tree hash; orphans surfaced; `rewrite` events on a recorded mechanism only | Key rows by path; or trust the working directory and re-key silently | A path key contradicts proposal §4.5 rule 2 flatly (and the source records no cost for it, which is stated rather than invented); silent re-keying destroys the audit trail a rewrite is supposed to leave | ADR-0018 | proposal §4.5 rule 2 [documented] |
| One store per repository at the common dir, refusing a foreign repo | A store per worktree | Nothing at the filesystem level prevents a second worktree writing to a shared store, so ownership is checked rather than assumed; a per-worktree store forfeits the shared lock location redkiln already uses | ADR-0018 | proposal §4.5 rules 1 and 5 [documented]; Backlog.md #558 [documented, external] |
| **No git hooks** | A `reference-transaction` hook feeding the store | It cannot be made atomic and forces full-log replays (git-branchless #1334) | ADR-0018 | [documented, external] |
| `doctor --rebuild` from a clean clone, run as a **CI test** | Treat rebuild as an operator recovery tool exercised on demand | A recovery path never exercised is a recovery path that does not work; run as CI it is also the standing test of the tier split | ADR-0018, ADR-0013 | SM-051 (no baseline; first measurement) |
| One idempotent resume entry, no `--resume` flag | A `--resume` flag distinguishing the two cases | A flag makes the caller decide something the kernel can derive, and creates a second code path that only runs after a failure | ADR-0018 | F38 [measured, n=3, PARTIAL] |
| `gix` reads, the `git` executable writes | gix for everything; `git2-rs`/libgit2 for everything; shell out for everything and drop gix | gix-for-everything means discovering both documented gaps at integration time on tier-1 state; libgit2 trades a documented Rust gap for a C toolchain and a second FFI surface; shelling out for everything loses typed rev-parse/status, shallow clone by SHA and structured trailer parsing, substituting porcelain parsing, which is worse | ADR-0077 | [documented, 1P gitoxide `crate-status.md`, fetched 2026-09-08] |

### 5.11 Open items in this section

Every row resolves to a spike with a named consumer in §10; none is discharged as prose (ADR-0012).

| Item | State | Hedge — what ships meanwhile | Discharged by | Gates Phase 1? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ChangeSummary` field set — artifacts whose change no diff renders | **BLOCKED** | AC-154 pins the **rendering direction only**; the type's shape is not frozen, and the criterion is **unverifiable and must not be counted as covered** | **S-BIN**, registered and unrun, on the real Unreal project (ADR-0008) → `rk-types::ChangeSummary` | **YES**, for this type |
| gix common-dir interplay; gix trailer **writing** | **BLOCKED**; hybrid adopted | The write path shells out to `git worktree add/remove` and `git interpret-trailers --in-place`; that route is itself decided and unblocked | **S-GIX/a**, **S-GIX/b** → `rk-git` (ADR-0077) | No |
| "Same next action" after a database deletion | Unproven | AC-161 ships as written but is **only as strong as AC-188**; SM-056 reports byte-recoverability, not decision-equivalence | the kernel replay test **AC-188** + a determinism lint (F19's stated limit) | No |
| Long-duration resume equality and duration-invariance | **PROVISIONAL** | AC-187 is demonstrated at short durations and n=3 only, with all three controls unrun; SM-052's duration-invariance target is labelled unfalsifiable in current data | **P8's 65-minute and 24-hour arms and its three controls** (ADR-0087) | No |

## 6. The three gates — human, review, verification

### 6.1 The three gates at a glance

| Gate | What it decides | Its record | What it may never do | Evidence | Ids |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Human** | Whether the loop proceeds past a point a human owns | `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` written as a `Gate:` trailer and a `gate_opened` event; the opening invocation exits 0 | Wait in a process; accept an answer against a tree that moved; hang past its deadline | F18, F19, F22, F28, F38 [measured] | AC-240..AC-279, SM-073..SM-084; ADR-0009, ADR-0017, ADR-0022, ADR-0038, ADR-0062, ADR-0063, ADR-0087 |
| **Review** | Which *findings* exist, as executable claims — never whether the item passes | A typed `Findings` payload with no decision field; the class computed in Rust; a block only after re-execution flips | Emit a verdict; clear an acceptance criterion; block on a class the deterministic gate has not re-executed | F17, F20, F24, F27, F29, F31 [measured] | AC-280..AC-319, SM-085..SM-096; ADR-0074 (fully superseding ADR-0004), ADR-0015, ADR-0020, ADR-0023, ADR-0025, ADR-0026, ADR-0036, ADR-0081, ADR-0082, ADR-0088 |
| **Verification** | Whether each AC is *pinned* by an assertion that flips under mutation | A `VerifyReport` carrying `basis`, `basis_reason`, `argv`, counts, `suite_sha`; an AC evidence ledger with an assertion locator per AC | Report `affected` for a run that did not narrow; treat a green suite as evidence at assertion granularity; accept a CI status in place of its own result | F11, F25, F31, brief §D [measured] | AC-320..AC-359, SM-097..SM-108; ADR-0019, ADR-0029, ADR-0073, ADR-0004→0074, ADR-0008, ADR-0014, ADR-0017, ADR-0030, ADR-0061 |

All three are instances of one mechanism, not three: a gate is a durable mark plus a later read (§3's kernel rule; brief §C.6, F22 [measured]). A mid-graph escalation is the same record shape and the same reconcile path as a stage gate — one gate type exists, not two (AC-272; ADR-0022, ADR-0038).

### 6.2 Neither gate is sufficient alone

This is the single most load-bearing statement in the section, and two findings force it. They are given equal weight because they fail in opposite directions.

| Direction of failure | The measurement | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The reviewer misses what the tests would catch — its verdict is a weighted coin** | A **reproduced regression was approved on 3 of 10 draws**; across 60 draws (0 void, 16.2 h) not one defect class is stable-reject, class means `reproduced` **0.70** > `mutation-survived` **0.37** > `narrowed-pin` **0.10** > negative control **0.00** — yet **19/19 rejections were on target** [measured, F29] | The reviewer's *claims* are sound; its *threshold* is not. So the model may **add** findings and its verdict channel is removed entirely (AC-280, AC-283) |
| **The tests miss what the reviewer would catch — a fully green suite is not evidence** | **Four adjudicated defects survived fully green suites of 1,890/1,890 and 1,907/1,907** [measured, F31] | So a green suite may never clear a finding the deterministic gate re-executed and saw flip (AC-316), and a review may never mark an AC verified (AC-315, AC-342) |

**The rule, stated once:** the review gate is high-precision and low-recall. It may **ADD** findings and may never **CLEAR** an AC. Blocking is computed in code from re-executed evidence, never from the model's `pass` (brief §C.7; ADR-0074 clauses 2–3, ADR-0019). Enforced by AC-315 (a clean review changes no AC's state), AC-316 (a green suite does not dismiss a confirmed block) and AC-342 (a review approval is not an input to any AC status), each with a type-level separation as its instrument rather than a test of model behaviour.

Externally corroborated, from the research pass: **SWE-Gate** derives review constraints from real PR review comments across 303 repair instances in 75 Python repos and finds **221 of 644 (34%)** functionally-*passing* agent repairs fail those constraints [preprint, arXiv 2609.04167, fetched 2026-09-08]. It measures a different quantity from anything in this corpus — benchmark constraint-failure, not a live override rate — and is cited as a second, fresher anchor for "green ≠ pinned", not as a rate this design inherits.

### 6.3 The human gate — a mark, not a process

#### 6.3.1 The mechanism

| Property | Rule | Evidence | Criterion |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Opening a gate is a write and an exit | The opening invocation terminates **exit 0** and leaves no child, background, detached or daemon process alive | ~12 background jobs died against **one 12,000 s `Start-Process` survivor**; `issue-81` was killed 3× as a background job and completed in **116 s** in the foreground — cause never established [measured, operational, F22] | AC-240 (exit), **AC-278** (no surviving descendant — a clean exit that leaks a detached child is a distinct failure) |
| No waiting construct on the gate path | Build fails on `thread::sleep`, a blocking channel receive, or a timer/scheduler spawn in the gate modules | F22 [measured]; F28 [measured] — a gate costs a flat ~18.6k tokens however long it lasts, so a waiting process would reintroduce the cost the mark removed | AC-242. **Scope limit:** this is a syntactic lint over named modules; it cannot prove no path anywhere blocks. The behavioural half is AC-240/AC-278 |
| Zero dispatches while a gate is open | No `dispatch` event may fall between `gate_opened` and the terminal event | The tempting regression is named: F10 puts the TTL at **~1 h** (full hit at 45.1 min, share **0.690** at 61.6 min — the boundary is *bracketed at (45.1, 61.6), never located*, n=1 per gap) and F18 puts **45–48% of gates over 1 h**, so "keep the cache warm" suggests itself. F28 proves it buys nothing: **18,603 tokens at 65.2 min and 18,603 at 49.7 h**, one measured point per duration [measured] | AC-243; SM-077 (saturates at zero, kept as an invariant alarm) |
| The two writes are atomic together | A kill during open leaves **both** the `Gate:` trailer and the `gate_opened` event, or neither | F38 is *motivating, not confirming*: P8 killed at three arbitrary instants and never enumerated write boundaries [measured, n=3] | AC-241 — a fault-point enumeration, not a sleep race |

Gate cost is settled and it is not what the proposal assumed: **duration is free, frequency is marginally costly, and placement is therefore a value decision, not a cost decision** (brief §C.2; ADR-0017, ADR-0087). AC-273 makes the machine check only that a placement decision record exists with an owner and a date, and forbids any code path that computes placement from a token or cost figure — because the evidence removed the quantity a machine could optimise.

#### 6.3.2 The record — `(token, tree_hash, deadline)`

The triple is unconstructible with a field missing, and a record read back from the store without all three fails deserialisation (AC-244). The token is single-use (AC-246) and scoped to one gate instance, so A's token against gate B is refused and B stays open (AC-247). An answer naming only an item is refused — item-keyed answering is ADR-0017's explicitly rejected alternative, because item identity alone cannot detect drift (AC-248).

**Why the tree hash, and why it is stronger than the prior art.** The research pass surveyed six durable-execution and CI platforms [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08]:

| Platform | Token semantics | Deadline | Bound to repository state? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AWS Step Functions | Task token, single-use | Server-enforced TTL, max activity TTL 6 h | No |
| Trigger.dev | Waitpoint token, single-consumption | Default 10 m, configurable; 1 h idempotency-key TTL | No |
| Inngest | `step.waitForEvent()` | Explicit timeout required | No |
| DBOS | `GetEvent`/`SetEvent` keyed by `(workflowID, key)` — no token object | Deadline-bearing | No |
| Restate | Awakeable, explicitly one-shot | **None built in** — the docs tell you to pair it with a separate durable timer | No |
| GitHub Environments | Approval requiring a real identity and a PAT, not the ambient `GITHUB_TOKEN` | Yes | **Commit SHA/ref only** |
| **Atlassian, "Agents in Jira"** (GA May 2026) | A **native workflow Approval step** holding a transition until a named approver signs off — on decades-old generic workflow machinery | Workflow-configured | No |
| Azure Durable Functions (external events) | **Attempted and not settled** — token expiry and single-use semantics could not be read this pass; the row is carried empty rather than dropped, and the six conclusions below rest on the other rows [documented, research pass §5 unresolved] | unread | unread |

Two consequences follow, and the second is the design's actual differentiator.

1. **Single-use plus a deadline is table stakes.** Every platform this pass could actually read mints a single-use signal and guarantees a terminal event; only GitHub pins to repository state at all, and **none binds a tree hash** [documented]. **The survey has a stated gap:** Azure Durable Functions' external-event token expiry and single-use semantics could not be read, so "every surveyed platform" means the six rows above it, not the field.

1a. **The differentiation claim, narrowed.** This design's novelty claim is **not** "nobody ships stage-gated, mid-run, resumable human approval on a durable work item" — Atlassian's *Agents in Jira* ships a native workflow Approval step that holds a transition until a named approver signs off, on generic workflow machinery decades old [documented, research pass §1 contradiction #10, §4 rec 15]. The claim that survives is the narrower one: **in an AI-coding-specific backlog or spec tool**, and on the *binding* rather than the gate — redkiln binds the answer to a **tree hash**, which no surveyed system does, Jira included.
2. **No surveyed system invalidates a stale-but-unexpired token automatically.** Stale-answer handling everywhere else is *workflow-side re-validation*: the token reports only that a value arrived, and consuming code is expected to re-check whatever external state it cared about. Only expiry and single-use consumption are platform-enforced [documented, 1P Restate external-events, DBOS workflow-communication, fetched 2026-09-08]. **This is precisely why the tree-hash binding is stronger, and why an explicit re-validation at the read site is required rather than optional** — redkiln moves the check from "the consumer remembers to look" to "the record cannot be applied against a tree it does not name". A commit-SHA pin (GitHub's) is weaker in one direction and noisier in the other: a history rewrite to an identical tree is not drift, and a touched-then-reverted file is not drift. AC-253 forbids deciding drift from mtimes, wall-clock elapsed time, commit counts or branch-tip identity, and requires the drift predicate to read no clock.

The recorded hash is the hash of **the tree the human actually saw**, not HEAD's commit and not the index at a later instant (AC-245) — otherwise the void rule checks a claim nobody made.

#### 6.3.3 Void, not reconciled

An answer arriving against a tree that is no longer `T` resolves **void**: a `gate_answer_void` event is written and no approve or changes-requested decision reaches the item (AC-249). There is no merge path, no re-application path and no deferred reconciliation — ADR-0017's rejected option is literally "reconcile stale answers". AC-250 splits the claim honestly: a property test over generated `(commit, answer, resume, expire)` interleavings checks that no generated sequence advances the item, and an **architecture test** over the code carries the universal half, because a fuzz test cannot establish "there exists no sequence of inputs".

| Quantity | Figure | Reading |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Drift, all gates | **12/174 = 6.9%** (Wilson 95% **4.0–11.7%**) [measured, F19; one operator, one machine] | The void path is reachable in normal operation at roughly 1 gate in 14 — neither a corner case nor a hot path; the same corpus is characterised as "the world moves rarely and predictably" |
| Drift, gates ≤ 1 h vs > 1 h | **3/95 = 3.2%** (1.1–8.9%) vs **9/79 = 11.4%** (6.1–20.3%) [measured, F19] | The 3.6× duration effect is **directional only — the intervals overlap**. It is never to be stated as an established ratio (SM-075) |
| Median in-gap commits | **0** [measured, F19] | Licenses no claim about the drifted subset's payload size: that statistic is over *all* gates, 93% of which did not drift. AC-254 requires the drift attachment to be exact and byte-identical across two renders **on principle**, not because it is known to be small |

A void re-opens the gate with a record differing in **all three** fields — fresh token, current tree hash, fresh deadline (AC-252) — carrying a non-empty drift attachment naming the commits and changed paths (AC-277, split out because a re-open with three fresh fields and no attachment satisfies AC-252 completely while losing the only thing that tells the human why they are being asked twice). Voids are excluded from approve/changes-requested rates and reported in their own denominator: **a void is never a miss** (AC-251). That is a reporting discipline transferred by analogy from every spike that reported a rate (F29 60 draws 0 void, F36 400 dispatches 0 void, F37 297 records 0 void [measured]) — a spike void is a discarded draw, a different quantity from a gate answer voided by drift, and only the discipline transfers.

The price of the rule is named rather than hidden: a human's answering work is discarded and must be redone when the tree moved before it landed. **SM-081 measures that cost and has no baseline** — it is the only number that would justify revisiting the all-or-nothing binding in favour of a path-scoped drift test.

#### 6.3.4 Deadline and a terminal expiry path

Every gate past its deadline is, after `reconcile()`, in exactly one of `fail`, `escalate` or `auto-decide`; the set of gates simultaneously past-deadline and open is empty, and a non-empty result from that `doctor` rule is an error (AC-256, SM-076). Expiry is observed **at the next read** — `redkiln run <item>` or `redkiln answer` — with no timer, cron entry, daemon or scheduled job required to have been alive at the deadline instant (AC-257), which is F22's lesson applied: a process that must be alive at a future moment is not a mechanism this system has.

Two honesty constraints attach.

- **Expiry in the corpus was unobservable, not observed-absent.** The F18/F19 gates carried no deadline mechanism, so no gate *could* expire; the longest was answered at **226 h (9.4 days)** [measured, F18, n=191, one operator]. Every expiry criterion here rests on design and prior art, and the corpus supplies **no expiry base rate in either direction** [inferred] (AC-276, SM-079).
- **Which terminal action applies to which gate class is a human decision, per class** (AC-276). The lint refuses a policy-table row with no expiry action declared, because a silent default in code would make the riskiest of the three — `auto-decide` on an unreviewed tree — reachable by omission (AC-260). The research pass shows the *style* is a free choice with a uniform guarantee: Step Functions raises `States.Timeout` and invalidates the token, Trigger.dev resolves to an incomplete status the run branches on, Inngest resolves `waitForEvent()` to `null`, DBOS raises an error — **every platform guarantees a terminal event; none leaves a workflow blocked with no observable outcome** [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08]. Redkiln picks the exception style, deliberately, and records it against ADR-0017. SM-079's target for `auto-decide` borrows Cloudflare's break-glass **288 of 48,095 MRs = 0.6%** [documented] as an *order of magnitude only* — a different mechanism in a different organisation, never a threshold.

#### 6.3.5 Resume re-derives from the world first

Every entry into `redkiln run <item>` re-derives the item's state from the world — tree, trailers, event log — before deciding the next action, with **no cached-state fast path that skips the derivation** (AC-261; brief §C.3). There is one idempotent entry point and `--resume` is not an accepted flag on any command (AC-264), because a separate resume verb is a second code path that would not get the re-derivation. Every channel writes through the same reconcile path, and the observable form of that is channel-independence: the same answer through the CLI adapter and through a stub adapter must produce byte-identical event sequences (AC-262). In Phase 1 the CLI is the only enabled channel and a stub tracker-comment or web-write answer is refused with a named outcome, writing no event (AC-263; ADR-0009). No agent card exposes the answer, mint or resolve path — checked by a card validator over the whole catalog and a build-configuration check, not by observing that an agent asked to answer a gate declined (AC-269; the model-behaviour observation is demoted to an optional smoke test, because one compliant sample proves nothing about reachability and F27 puts verdict flakiness at 2/4 items [measured]).

The verification-gate side of resume asserts the mirror negative: resume **re-derives** the changed-file set from the world, while the *audit* replay of a completed run reproduces the recorded path list **without reading the working tree** (AC-353 against AC-321 — each states the other's negative, so a builder cannot satisfy both with one implementation).

**The standing limit, restated because it is easy to lose:** F19 proved the state *carrier* lossless at **174/174** (Wilson 95% lower bound 97.8%) and F38 proved it at mid-graph step granularity **3/3** across a real `taskkill /T /F` with 0 changed, 0 lost and identical digests — but **the FSM was never replayed**, so "same next action" is unproven and ships as a kernel replay test (AC-258 under an injected clock and an injected filesystem root; AC-270 reconstructing gate state from the log alone). AC-258 asserts injected-clock and injected-root replay only; genuine cross-machine reproduction is not claimed, because nothing tests it.

#### 6.3.6 Mid-graph gates, and what P8 did not settle

**The decision (ADR-0087).** Escalation to a human gate **suspends the activity graph and resumes into the same step** with the answer bound as that step's gate input; it is not activity-terminal. Resume must cross a real process boundary — a reload that reads state back in-process is not a valid implementation and is not evidence for one, because the risk being managed is that a *fresh* process reconstructs something different.

**The measurement.** Mid-graph resume cost **0.45× / 0.55× / 0.78×** of the ~18,603-token stage-gate resume (8,408 / 10,314 / 14,579 tokens), against a registered kill at >1.5× which did not fire [measured, F38, n=3 of a pre-registered 27+3, $3.01 of a $40 cap]. **The kill did not fire at n=3** — with no within-experiment baseline (all three controls unrun) and all three draws inside F10's ~1 h TTL, where the absence means little (§5.8). So the pre-registration's assumption — that a mid-graph gate would price like a stage gate *plus* the graph's replay — is **untested, not falsified**, and **nothing here establishes that mid-graph placement is safe**. The decision below is taken because it is safe under *either* answer, not because this measurement licensed it.

**What P8 did not settle, at equal prominence.** Per ADR-0087's own decision text, three questions are open in ways that matter, and no sentence elsewhere in this document may treat them as closed.

| Open | What is actually true | Consequence for this design |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Delay is entirely unestablished** | All three runs sat **inside** F10's ~1 h TTL — measured `gap_minutes` **0.9, 28.4, 29.1**, notwithstanding the `0-delay` arm label, because two cycles were driven manually after the orchestrator stalled. **The arm label is not the measurement.** The 65-minute and 24-hour arms — the ones that straddle the TTL and test the cache-cold resume path — and **all three no-kill controls are unrun** [measured/PARTIAL, F38] | There is **no within-experiment baseline**; the ratio is compared only to F28. "Mid-graph gates are cheaper than stage gates" may not be written without n=3 and the missing baseline named in the same sentence. AC-266 therefore enforces the **1.5×** bar P8 registered, not the ≤1× aspiration — a test that passes at 1.4× cannot enforce a ≤1× claim, and asserting ≤1× on three short-duration draws would over-read them. The aspiration lives in SM-073's target with a **1.0×–1.5× warning band that triggers no automatic action** |
| **The second-arrival question is not answerable and must not be reported as "did not fire"** | P8 §5.4(3) is **circular by construction**: the gate's own predicate was false on every second arrival, arrivals happened only because `--force-gate` was in effect, and the mark was minted by `repro-probe` — the driver that implements the semantics under test. Measuring A8 against an implementation that assumes A8 settles nothing | AC-271 is **PROVISIONAL with a deliberately empty evidence line**: the chosen semantics (first answer applies, second refused as spent, no second mark minted) are pinned by a concurrency test so they are at least *stated*, not claimed to be evidenced. **SM-078 is a discovery instrument with no target and no kill** — the arrival-rate histogram is the only route to the answer, and no spike can supply it |
| **The §5.2 fixture design failed outright, and that is P8's other real finding** | "Remove the repro steps so the gate fires" does not work against an agent holding `Read` and `Bash` in a real repository: across three redactions of increasing severity and two selection rules the gate **never fired** (reproduced on attempt 1 at 754 s, 650 s, 480 s). Sharpest form: `issue-74-plan-briefs-dead-plan-anchor` was localised **0 times in 6** Opus attempts in P7 and **reproduced on the first haiku attempt** in P8 — **reproducing a symptom and localising its cause are different tasks of different difficulty**, and §5.2 assumed the harder gates the easier | Every gate arrival in P8 was forced, so **the arrival rate — how often a triage gate opens in practice — is entirely unmeasured**. Any future gate-frequency argument (SM-082, also a discovery instrument) starts from zero |

P8's shortfall is one of the nine blocked rows of §0.1 and it gates the **"design final" label**, not the build: Phase 1 starts on the decided rows, and no document, section or commit message may describe this design as final. The unrun arms and controls are registered in §10 with ADR-0087 as their consumer.

### 6.4 The review gate — reviewer proposes, machine confirms

ADR-0074 **fully supersedes ADR-0004**; ADR-0004 is citable as history only (it chose *findings, not verdicts* "pending S-VAR" and named no mechanism). Both gating spikes have since run and each answered a different half.

#### 6.4.1 The eight clauses, and where each is enforced

| # | Clause (ADR-0074) | Evidence | Criterion |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The card returns `Findings` with **no `pass` field**; each item is an executable claim `{ ac, claim, evidence (advisory label), rationale, files[], repro { command, observed, expected_when_met } }` | Schema conformance **52/52 = 100%**; `repro.command` fill **147/147** under the typed contract [measured, F31] | AC-280 (an enumerated decision-field denylist — `pass`, `verdict`, `approved`, `ok`, `status`, `decision` — refused at configuration time, before any dispatch; a name check, not a semantic one, so a verdict under an unlisted name still gets through and that is stated); AC-281 (an incomplete `repro` is rejected at ingest and never reaches the classifier) |
| 2 | **The evidence class is computed in Rust from the repro record**, never taken from the model; the model's label is stored beside it for drift monitoring only | Detection is stable and the verdict is not: detection **0/7 items flaky over 21 draws**, verdict **2/4 items flaky** [measured, F27, n=2–3] | AC-284 (a total decision table over a closed `EvidenceClass` enum with no `_ =>` fallthrough to a blocking class; every unrecognised input lands on `advisory`); AC-285 (`review_class_drift` event carries both labels; an architecture lint forbids the model label having a reader inside policy); AC-286 (1,000 in-process computations plus a fresh process, identical; a module-syntax lint over `std::fs`, `std::time`, `std::env`) |
| 3 | **A finding blocks only after the deterministic gate re-executes it and it FLIPS** — fails on the candidate, passes on the reference, or the mutant survives on re-application. Re-execution is a precondition of blocking, not a confirmation step that may be skipped under load | F31 adjudication **14/14 confirmed** (the human procedure, at zero model cost — not mechanical re-execution); F11: 15 mutants, **9 of 10 test-reachable killed, 1 survived**, test-reachable 90.0% (Wilson 59.6–98.2% — the interval on 1/10 is wide and is reported as wide), **over a corpus 8 of whose 23 items S0.1 could not reach at all** (§1.2) [measured] | AC-288 (four-cell candidate × reference truth table); AC-289 (re-apply the named mutant at the reviewed SHA; block only if the suite is still green with it applied, with the S0.1 fixtures as the regression set); AC-287 (no re-execution record ⇒ no block, reported `Unverifiable`; replay assertion `blocks ⊆ re-executed`) |
| 4 | **Both-fail or cannot-run is `Unverifiable`, and `Unverifiable` is advisory** — a finding whose evidence the gate cannot separate does not block, in either direction | Timeout history is the precedent: a **900 s default written for the fast condition cost a CLEAN record**, then 2,400 → 3,600 → one 5,400 s retry [documented, brief §D] | AC-290 (three reasons — `both_fail` \| `cannot_run` \| `timeout` — persisted; a `doctor` rule that every `reproduced`/`mutation-survived` finding carries either a re-execution record or an `Unverifiable` reason). The rule is per-finding: "a review with 0 executed and 0 `Unverifiable` findings is impossible" is **false**, because a review producing no findings has exactly those counts and is legal |
| 5 | **The policy table is the only place a class becomes a block**, parameterised by stage rigor | Class means `reproduced` **0.70** > `mutation-survived` **0.37** > `narrowed-pin` **0.10** > control **0.00** set the default *order and nothing else* [measured, F29] | AC-294 (a private constructor plus a module-boundary lint: the build fails if a block value is constructed outside `rk-kernel::policy`, with a `trybuild` compile-fail test); AC-296 (`advisory` is in no block set at any weight — a design invariant of the table with **no measurement behind it**, and labelled as such) |
| 6 | **`Quarantined { reason, by, until }` is a first-class finding state** | Proposal §4.6 item 5 [documented]; F18 — a gate can outlive its own assumptions (max **226 h**) | AC-304 (quarantined ⇒ absent from the block set, still persisted and rendered with its reason); AC-319 (a quarantine with no `until` fails to construct); AC-306 (on expiry the finding is re-raised and subject to the prevailing weight **exactly as a newly raised finding of its class** — it blocks iff its class blocks, which is false for `advisory` and `Unverifiable`); AC-305 (identity is the computable tuple *(AC id, repro command digest, mutant id)* — "the same normalised claim" is a judgement no test can make, and the acknowledged cost is that a restatement with a different command re-raises) |
| 7 | **Negative-control fixtures stay in the corpus** as the reviewer's own regression test, and the S-SUBTLE family gains a "presented as already approved" arm | S-VAR control **0/10** rejections (Wilson 0–27.8%); S-SUBTLE **5/5 sighted and 5/5 blind, twice** [measured, F29, F26] | AC-312 as a **conjunction**: every seeded `reproduced` fixture blocks **and** every control produces zero — "zero blocks on controls" alone passes on a gate that never blocks anything. See §6.4.5 for the new arm |
| 8 | **Every block and every override is an event**, and **false-block is measured against adjudication, never against historical approval** | F24: all six E3 rejections factually **true 6/6**, at zero model cost — the corpus's own labels were reversed by adjudication [measured] | AC-307 (the event carries `{ finding_id, ac, computed_class, model_label, stage_weight, sha, re_execution_id, actor, adjudication: null }`; replay invariant `count(blocks) == count(block_events)`); AC-308, AC-309 — see §6.4.4 |

Two structural criteria sit under clause 1 and are worth naming separately, because both are F20's lesson generalised. A review dispatch that exits 0 with **no `structured_output` block** fails the step with a missing-structured-output error and produces no finding set, empty or otherwise (AC-282) — F20 measured an agent card's `tools` allowlist silently disabling `--json-schema` at `exit 0`, `subtype: success` [measured], which is exactly how a review consumer fails open. And an architecture lint asserts **no code path in the review path extracts findings from assistant prose** — no JSON-from-text parse, no regex over the transcript body (AC-318; brief §C.5). Free text may not reach policy at all: AC-283's load-bearing instrument is a *signature* test that no free-text field is an input type to `policy::classify`, so two payloads identical except that one's rationale asserts "approved" compute byte-identical block sets.

#### 6.4.2 The policy table, the lenient default, and the Advisory sink

| Computed class | Lenient weight (default) | Strict weight | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `reproduced` | Blocks | Blocks | Class mean 0.70, highest of four; 19/19 rejections on target [measured, F29] |
| `mutation-survived` | Blocks | Blocks | 1 of 10 test-reachable mutants survived S0.1's affected-area gate [measured, F11 — wide CI on 1/10, and **8 of 23 corpus items sat outside S0.1 by construction**, §1.2]; four defects survived green suites [measured, F31] |
| `narrowed-pin` | **Advises** | Blocks | Class mean 0.10 [measured, F29]; the relaxation cost no detection [measured, F31] |
| `advisory` / `Unverifiable` | Never blocks | Never blocks | ADR-0074 clauses 4–5 [documented] |

**The lenient default is the resolved weight when a stage sets none** (AC-298; ADR-0015 as renumbered by ADR-0020's deontic enum, `0.2 → optional`, `1.0 → mandatory`). The measurement behind it: lenient blocked **9/23** against strict's **12/23** on the pinned CLEAN set with **0/23 verified false positives** and detection unchanged (S-SILENT 23/23, S-SUBTLE 5/5) [measured, F31]. **Two caveats travel with that number and may not be dropped.** First, **the severity call itself — "narrowed-pin advises" — is one operator's judgement (Ryan's), stated as such**; what was measured is only that the relaxation cost no detection. Second, **CLEAN's ground truth is wrong for ≥13 of 23 (57%)** [contested, brief §E], so *0/23 verified false positives* is an **upper bound** (Wilson 0–14.3%) and **CLEAN is never a denominator for an accuracy or false-positive-*rate* claim anywhere in this design** — the fractions in this paragraph are block **counts** over the pinned 23-item set, and the Wilson interval is quoted only to show how wide the floor result is, never as an accuracy rate. AC-295 makes the weight a one-line replayable change: moving lenient→strict alters the block set **only** by adding `narrowed-pin` items, with no re-dispatch and a byte-identical classifier output.

The relaxation leaves a hole, and **ADR-0082 fills it**: **seven `narrowed-pin` findings, every one of them adjudicated TRUE**, move from blocking to non-blocking [measured, F31 — `issue-130` AC-005, `issue-81` AC-002, `issue-82` AC-003, `issue-83` AC-001, `fix-doctor-git-config-repair` AC-002 and AC-004, `memoize-repo-root` AC-003]. F29's branch 4 says what the sink is *not* for: the negative control rejected 0 of 10 draws, so no sink is needed to absorb noise. It is needed to carry **real, adjudicated-true signal that does not block**.

| Advisory-sink rule (ADR-0082) | Enforcement |
| --- | --- |
| **Written, not dropped** — in the same typed `Findings` shape as a blocking finding, `repro` record, rationale and advisory label unaltered; the only on-disk difference is the computed disposition | AC-310: replay assertion `findings_in == blocks + sink_records`, plus a `doctor` rule that errors when a completed review's non-blocking finding count exceeds its sink's record count; the seven F31 findings are the seed fixture and must all land |
| **Surfaced at the next gate without blocking** — as typed attachments bound to the gate's question (ADR-0063), under their own heading, excluded from the block computation; a gate answer is never conditioned on one and their presence alone never opens a gate | AC-310 rendering; ADR-0063 attachment schema |
| **Retired only by re-review or an explicit recorded human act** — age, run count, gate passage and stage advancement retire nothing | AC-311 (the triage disposition is a human gate — whether an advisory finding is worth a backlog item is a value call; the machine-checkable half is that every sink record reaches a terminal disposition or is surfaced as ageing) |
| **Advisory and `Quarantined` are disjoint** — quarantine applies **only** to a finding policy computed as blocking; quarantining an advisory finding is an **error, not a no-op**, because the override telemetry would record a suppression that never happened | A finding is in exactly one of `blocking`, `quarantined`, `advisory` at any tree (ADR-0082 clause 4) |
| **The disposition is computed, never asserted** — the model may not label its own finding advisory to avoid a block, and code may not promote an advisory finding to blocking without re-executing the flip criterion | AC-284, AC-296 |

**Named gap:** nothing in the corpus measures whether a sink is *read*. SM-089 tracks routed-equals-created and age at disposition, with an explicit landfill kill — if the sink's p90 age exceeds F18's **stage→verdict** p90 (~9 h, n=191; a *different series* from the 10.5 min gate→verdict median, n=174) for a month, advisory findings should be dropped at source rather than pretended into a backlog.

#### 6.4.3 The reviewer's environment — the disposable shallow clone

F17 is the reason this is structural rather than instructed: the E3 reviewer **restored the fix from the object store** — 42 turns, 22,853 output tokens, 526 s — and returned `approved` [measured as an instrument defect]. The reviewer keeps `Bash`, in a **disposable shallow clone at the reviewed SHA** (ADR-0074), and the clone's shape is asserted on four properties: `--depth 1`, no `objects/info/alternates`, no remote pointing at the parent repo, and `git cat-file -e <pre-change blob>` failing (AC-292). Write isolation is likewise asserted on the **spawn configuration** — the reviewer's process has no write path to the candidate worktree or the store — with a before/after `rev-parse HEAD^{tree}` and `status --porcelain` probe kept only as corroboration (AC-291). The reasoning is explicit: a probe that depends on the model *choosing* to attempt a write cannot establish a negative, and F17 shows it took 42 turns to find its route. Every review outcome carries the tree digest of the SHA reviewed, non-optional, with no default, no empty string and no `None` (AC-293).

#### 6.4.4 Telemetry — and what false-block is measured against

| Measure | Instrument and baseline | Discipline |
| --- | --- | --- |
