| **SM-086 / AC-308 — verified false-block rate** | Denominator is **adjudicated blocks only**; historical approval (merged, human-approved) is **never** usable as ground truth; unadjudicated blocks are reported as a separate `pending_adjudication` count. **No compliant baseline exists yet.** The rule is adjudicated-blocks-only, and F31 adjudicated **14/14** — so the only reading with a compliant denominator is **0 of 14 adjudicated**. The figure quoted elsewhere as 0/23 is over CLEAN's *item* count, not over adjudicated blocks; it is carried strictly as an **upper bound against a ground truth wrong for ≥13/23** (Wilson 0–14.3% over that wrong denominator) [measured, F31]. Comparator: Cloudflare break-glass **288/48,095 = 0.6%** [documented] | **NO COMPLIANT BASELINE** — and the reason is the denominator, not the adjudication. The E3 severity adjudication **closed 2026-09-09** (facts settled 6/6; severity is Ryan's, one operator, blind), so the two FP rates and two MCCs are settled — arm (a) 1/23 / +0.978, arm (b) 0/23 / +1.000 — and both rates remain **upper bounds** against CLEAN's wrong ground truth, which is what this measure's own rule forbids using. The research pass found **no published false-block comparator beyond Cloudflare's 0.6%**; SWE-Gate's 34% measures a different quantity [documented] |
| **SM-087 / AC-313 — confirm-half determinism** | The k=10 replay job over recorded findings, no dispatch, $0: the block-set digest must be identical on 10/10 **and equal to the pinned non-empty expected digest** — without the non-empty clause a gate that blocks nothing scores 10/10 | This is the measure that converts F29's 3-of-10 verdict flip from a correctness problem into a **recall** problem: the machine half must not flip at all |
| **SM-091 — model label vs computed class drift** | `review_class_drift` events; **no baseline**. Nearest measured shape is F27 (verdict flaky 2/4, detection flaky 0/7) | **Diagnostic, never gated.** Two-sided and both directions informative: drift ≈ 0 for three releases means the model label is redundant and can leave the schema; sustained high drift is direct evidence for keeping computation in Rust and **must never be "fixed" by trusting the label** |
| **SM-085 — block rate by computed class** | Strict 12/23 (Wilson 33.0–70.8%), lenient 9/23 (22.2–59.2%) [measured, F31] — **counts, not accuracy** | No target rate. The signal is a step change in the ratio *between* classes, never the level. Kill: if the `reproduced` share falls below the `advisory` share for three consecutive releases, the executable-claim contract has stopped producing executable claims |
| **SM-092 / SM-093 — controls and seeded recall** | Control 0/10 (0–27.8%); seeded detection 23/23, 5/5, 23/23 vs 23/23 at McNemar **p=0.625** [measured, F29/F26/F23] | **Both saturated, and reported as saturated.** SM-093 is a one-way canary with **no kill** — a drop below ceiling is a signal, a stay at ceiling means nothing, and it is never used to compare two configurations. The pair cannot discriminate in either direction; what keeps them from being satisfied by a gate that never blocks is AC-312's seeded half, which is a criterion, not a measure |
| **SM-096 — wall clock and timeout pressure** | Review median **1,071 s (~18 min)**, slowest **4,561 s** [measured, F31]; the deterministic gate it feeds has **no affected-area selection today** — every run is the whole 236-file suite, ~250 s to >900 s over 1,890–1,927 tests [measured] | The 5% timeout-`Unverifiable` target is **chosen, not derived**, and says so. The original second half ("review p90 inside the item's gate budget") is dropped: no gate budget exists anywhere in the evidence, so it could neither pass nor fail |

Review **cost** per tier — the $ figures, the tier comparators and the cache arithmetic — belongs to §7 and is not restated here.

#### 6.4.5 The direction this gate is *not* yet guarded against

Everything above measures one direction of judge bias: **false rejection**. Jin & Chen measure rationale-heavy prompting driving false rejection (GPT-4o FNR **26–36% → 58–88%**; a fix-guided verification filter recovers 54.8%→16.3% HumanEval, 69.0%→28.9% MBPP, 51.0%→24.0% QuixBugs) [preprint/peer, arXiv 2603.00539], and ADR-0074's rebuild answers it directly.

**The citation correction, because it changes how much the rebuild rests on.** The proposal cited "*Are LLMs Reliable Code Reviewers?* (arXiv 2603.00539)" and "Jin & Chen, fix-guided verification filter" as **two entries. They are one paper** — arXiv 2603.00539's author list is exactly "Haolin Jin, Huaming Chen", the filter is proposed in its abstract, and both number sets live in that one paper, the filter's in §7.2 / Table 4. Every individual figure re-verifies exactly; the defect is **provenance, not arithmetic** [documented; research pass §1 row 9, §4 rec 11]. Merged here into one citation, because counting one paper as two **overstated the independent replication behind the D4 rebuild**: the rebuild rests on fewer independent replications than the proposal implied. It still rests on this programme's own F29/F31/F27 measurements, which is where its weight actually is.

**The mirror risk, registered and unrun (ADR-0088).** A controlled experiment over **250 CVE vulnerability–patch pairs** finds that framing a change as bug-free before review reduces vulnerability-detection by **16–93%**, **strongly asymmetric: false negatives rise sharply while false positives barely move** [preprint, arXiv 2603.18740, fetched 2026-09-08]. The S-SUBTLE family therefore gains a **"presented as already approved" arm**: the same items presented twice, **paired per item**, framing as the only manipulated variable, same schema, same policy table, same stage weight. It reports **detection, not verdict** — the manipulation predicts a false-negative shift and detection is the stable half of this instrument (F27) — and reports missed seeded defects **separately** from spurious findings, because a pooled error rate hides an asymmetric effect. **CLEAN is an observational instance of the framing, never the controlled arm**: it is genuinely prior-approved input carrying real defects (≥13/23), which makes it evidence that the framing occurs in the field and unusable as the comparison, since its framing cannot be removed and its ground truth is known wrong.

> **Until that arm runs, no section of this document — and no other document — may claim the review gate is guarded against false approval, and no S-VAR or S-TYPED false-positive rate may be presented as evidence of overall gate calibration. Those rates measure the other direction.** The arm must be **sized before it is run**: at the family's present n=5 with detection at ceiling, only a large effect is observable and a 16% drop is not, so the registration carries its own sizing or reports a bound rather than a rate (ADR-0088 clauses 5–6). Registered in §10 with its named consumer.

### 6.5 The verification gate — affected-area selection and per-AC mutation evidence

#### 6.5.1 Selection must prove it selected, and the fallback is recorded as basis

The failure this half exists to catch is measured and silent: a filter that did not survive package-manager forwarding turned `pnpm test -- --run <file>` into a **whole-suite run on 26 of 68 invocations** (**38.2%**, Wilson 27.6–50.1%), where `npx vitest run <file>` is **1 file / 6 tests / 15.6 s** against 236 files and 1,890–1,927 tests at ~250 s to >900 s — a **~60× difference that ran undetected** [measured, brief §D; one repo, one machine].

| Rule | Criterion |
| --- | --- |
| A run that did not narrow records `basis: whole-suite`, **never** `affected` | AC-322 |
| The **argv actually handed to the OS process API** is recorded as an argument vector, not the templated command string, and re-executing it verbatim reproduces the same executed-test count | AC-325 |
| A run claiming `affected` whose executed count meets or exceeds the toolchain's last recorded whole-suite count is reclassified `whole-suite / selection-ineffective` with an event | AC-326. **Stated gap, not repaired:** this catches only a *fully inert* filter; one that drops most of its arguments but not all — selecting 200 of 236 files — passes. AC-327's probe and SM-097's narrowing distribution are the compensating instruments and neither is a substitute |
| `doctor` executes the declared selection command against a single-file probe and fails the pack unless the executed count is **strictly less** than the fixture's whole-suite count; the rule emits zero harness dispatches | AC-327 |
| No `basis: affected` record may exist for a toolchain whose `affected-selection` capability has no probe result at the current version pin — selection is refused `Unsupported` and the pack routes to an explicit whole-suite policy | AC-328; SM-105 (**zero toolchains carry such a probe today**; first measurement establishes it) |
| A non-empty changed set selecting **zero** tests never renders green — it escalates to `whole-suite / selection-empty` or fails per declared policy | AC-332 |
| `basis` and `basis_reason` are **closed enums** matched exhaustively with no wildcard arm, so a new variant is a compile error; reasons are `no-selection-support`, `probe-unverified`, `selection-empty`, `selection-ineffective`, `policy-forced`, `toolchain-unsupported` | AC-352 |
| **The basis is shown wherever a human reads the outcome** — a reader cannot see "green" without seeing what ran, and a rendering that omits it fails | AC-323 |

Two anti-vacuity controls hold the set together: AC-355's paired fixtures (a lockfile change whose correct basis is `whole-suite`, a single-file change whose correct basis is `affected`) fail a selector that always answers either way; and AC-350 requires every changed path to be **accounted for** across a multi-toolchain repo — one result per claiming toolchain, the gate their conjunction, and a path claimed by no configured toolchain is a configuration error rather than a silent exclusion.

Selection itself is **code, not a dispatch**: a pure function of the changed-file set, the toolchain config and `facets.toml`; a verify run emitting any harness dispatch fails the activity (AC-347, SM-103 — expected to saturate at zero and tracked anyway, because the value is the moment it stops being zero). **This is where the BLOCKED selection engine touches this gate.** P1's kill fired **4 of 4**: the modal `R(stage, ctx)` repeated on only **5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20** items against an 80% line, with alpha drift **−0.168 on byte-identical anchors** [measured, F36; 400 dispatches, 0 void]. **Two claims are licensed now, and they are about two different vectors.** F36's claim is **instability**, and it is about the **LLM-emitted** vector: nothing there is repaired by a temperature change. F39's claim is **inaccuracy**, and it is about the **derived** one: scored against 140 truth cells at pin `47faec9f`, **no derived facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar** — `auth` **0.00** [0.00, 0.26] over eleven positives every one of which is false, `flag_exists` **0.00** [0.00, 0.66], `binary_change` 0.60 [0.23, 0.88], `change` 0.72 [0.54, 0.85], `toolchain` 0.73 [0.48, 0.89], `ui` 1.00 [0.65, 1.00] whose point clears and whose interval does not, and `data_migration` **not measurable** — while the ≥ 0.95 clause is itself unreachable at n=20 [measured, F39; $0, panel-adjudicated]. The earlier revision of this sentence read "instability, not inaccuracy" flat; **F39 overturns that for the derived facets** and ADR-0089 is the record. AC-302 carries the same standing negative on the review side: the **review tier** derives only from the authored stage rigor weight and authored facets, and no LLM-emitted context vector is an input. AC-351 completes it at the registry: a `require` row or selection rule referencing a facet with no live entry in `facets.toml` is a lint error naming the facet — which today fails on `binary_change` (measured but unregistered — and now measured at precision **0.60** [0.23, 0.88] as well, F39) and has no entry for `dynamism`; `recent_deploy` is underivable at this pin [contested, brief §E]. **ADR-0089 adds the second half of that lint:** a live entry is no longer sufficient, because a facet may not declare `source = "derived"` without a named `deriver` and a scored precision record for it, and a lexical scan of free text does not satisfy PC8 rule 2 for `require`-row purposes at all.

#### 6.5.2 CI is independently authoritative — and independence runs both ways

A green CI status reported through the Tracker port **never satisfies verify**: with CI green and verify `Err`, the item does not advance. And no verify result writes to the Tracker port's status-check state (AC-341). The instrument is a static check that the verify gate's input type contains **no tracker or CI field**, plus a port-call assertion that a verify run issues zero status-check writes. The two systems are separately authoritative over separate questions and neither substitutes for the other; this is the same shape as §6.2's rule applied to a third party.

A verify result that lacks its `basis`, suite digest or executed-command record resolves to `Err`, never to a pass — `Result<VerifyReport, StepError>` at the boundary with no `Default` impl and a lint forbidding `unwrap_or_default` (AC-324; F20's fail-open mechanism again). A run killed by its timeout or by the OS is `Unverifiable` and is **never** recorded as a pass or as a test failure attributable to the candidate (AC-331; F22). A model-authored `pass`, `verdict` or `approved` field reaching this gate is a schema violation, not a field to ignore (AC-343).

#### 6.5.3 Per-AC mutation evidence — the measured mechanism

**An AC is a claim about ONE ASSERTION, and Red→Green is evidenced at TEST granularity.** On the AC-015 probe, removing step 7 turned the suite RED while **inverting the ordering left 36 of 36 GREEN** [measured, F25; **n=1 AC, one operator**]. That single probe is the mechanism the whole area defends, and it is a mechanism finding rather than a rate — consistent with the programme's own method finding that every discriminating result came from cost, stability or mechanism, never from success rates (brief §C.12).

| Rule | Criterion |
| --- | --- |
| Every AC row in the evidence ledger carries an **assertion locator** — file, test identifier, and the assertion within that test. A row naming only a test file fails validation, and an AC with no evidence is materialised `unpinned`, never omitted | AC-333 |
| An AC is `pinned` **only if the named assertion flips** when the mutation derived from its THEN clause is applied; if the suite stays green the AC is `mutation-survived`. The AC-015 inversion is a named regression row that must yield `mutation-survived` | AC-334 |
| A green suite changes **nothing** about an AC whose mutation survived — enforced by a static check that the AC-status function does not take the suite verdict as an argument | AC-335 (the mechanism: F31's four defects surviving 1,890/1,890 and 1,907/1,907) |
| The mutation is verified **still present at completion**; a run in which it was reverted or restored before the suite finished is `Unverifiable`, never `mutation-survived` and never `pinned` | AC-338 — the F17 fixture, an agent-restorable mutation, is the named case |
| Mutation runs in a disposable clone or worktree; the item's own tree hash is identical before and after and `git status --porcelain` is empty | AC-339 |
| A surviving mutant is a `mutation-survived` **finding**, blocking under the pack's declared default weight, and is never dropped because the rest of the suite is green | AC-340 — with the honest note that F31's severity call is Ryan's, so the default weight is a decision this criterion **enforces**, not one the evidence sets |
| Evidence recorded at sha *N* does not count toward `pinned` at *N+1* unless the memo key (inputs, card digest, toolchain version, suite sha) is identical; a reuse is recorded as a memo hit with its key, never as a fresh run | AC-336 |
| A stage may not exit with an AC that is neither pinned by a flip-verified assertion nor answered at a human gate; the refusal names the unpinned AC ids and is an **event**, not only a console line | AC-346 |

**Where mutation is not feasible**, the toolchain declares it explicitly — an absent declaration is a configuration error — and the AC records `mutation: not-feasible(reason)`, which is not `pinned` (AC-337). The residual routes to a human gate carrying the AC text, the assertion source and the suite result, answered with `by` and `at` (AC-357): deciding whether an assertion discriminates a criterion with no executable mutation available is a judgement about meaning, and F24 and F31 both show severity judgements in this corpus remained one operator's even where the facts were mechanically settled. That human gate is the *same* gate mechanism as §6.3 — AC-359 binds it to the tree hash, voids a drifted answer, re-opens with the drift attached, and forbids carrying pre-drift verify evidence across as `pinned`.

**False-block telemetry on this gate follows §6.4.4's rule exactly** (AC-358, SM-104): the denominator is adjudicated blocks only, a block never counts as false because the item was historically approved, and an unadjudicated block is excluded rather than counted either way. SM-104's ≤1% target is an **aspiration set without a supporting baseline** — 0/23's Wilson interval does not exclude 14.3%.

**Coverage has no baseline at all.** SM-100's ≥0.90 target is likewise stated as an aspiration: **no AC-level pinning rate has ever been measured**, the nearest evidence being F25's single probe and F31's four survivors, neither of which is a rate. Its kill is the honest one — if coverage cannot exceed ~0.5 because mutation is infeasible for most ACs in real repositories, per-AC mutation evidence is not the *normal* TDD evidence and **ADR-0019's "norm" claim needs rewriting rather than enforcing**.

#### 6.5.4 Where this gate touches a blocked row

- **`ChangeSummary` — BLOCKED on S-BIN, registered and unrun.** AC-356 is **PROVISIONAL, and its evidence standard is BLOCKED on S-BIN rather than provisional-but-specified**. The *sketch* is binary paths represented by manifest plus capture references rather than text diff, with per-AC mutation evidence not required for a `binary`-class AC (its evidence would be "asset touched + test/capture result"). **Switching off §6.5.3's per-AC mutation norm for a whole AC class is a substantive gate decision**, and taking it on the one class for which — in this document's own words — no review evidence of any kind exists would be exactly the move §0.1 forbids. So it is not taken: the per-AC mutation norm stays in force for every class until S-BIN reports, no fixture naming `ChangeSummary`'s fields may be written until then, and this sentence is a design sketch under a block, not a relaxation. The field names **must not be frozen** before S-BIN runs against the real Unreal project, and this criterion may not be promoted to binding on the strength of this document (ADR-0008, ADR-0012). It gates Phase 1 for the type.
- **The Advisory sink does not exist yet.** AC-344 routes `Unverifiable` findings to it; that is a *requirement on the sink*, not an assumption that one is there, and it is unmeetable until ADR-0082's sink is built.
- **No whole-suite p95 exists.** AC-330's `doctor` rule fails a pack with no numeric `timeout_secs` (an inherited default is a failure) and *additionally* fails a declared timeout below a recorded whole-suite p95 — but the field was never recorded, so at first run only the declaration half can fire. Stated as a property of the rule rather than hidden inside it.
- **The quality shorthand's default is not set here.** ADR-0080 leaves it explicitly unset, and the forbidden predicate `findings.none(evidence in [Reproduced, MutationSurvived])` may not be a quality block's terminal condition — it is satisfiable only by a reviewer that finds nothing, which F23/F26/F31 prove this reviewer is not. What this section *does* enforce is monotonicity: **no rigor escalation may remove the review-confirm step from the resolved graph** (AC-303), with F37's `mandatory` + `has_repro` case — which selects `bisect`, an activity with no adversary and no policy step — as a named lattice-lint regression row. AC-303 is written so it does not depend on F37's cost ratio, which is **unreconciled at 3.66× (F37) vs 4.4× (decisions log)**.
- **Cascades are refused, not priced.** A pack configuring a cheap-first review cascade (a read-only pass gating whether execution-grade runs) fails validation with the reason "cascade recall loss unmeasured"; the refusal lifts only when a named spike measures that loss, and **none is registered today** (AC-301). AC-299's read-only tier is a definition with no measurement behind it.

### 6.6 Decisions and open items

| Decision | Alternatives considered | Why this one | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Human gate is a durable `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` mark; the opening process exits 0 (**ADR-0009, ADR-0017**) | A waiting/daemon process; an item-keyed answer with no token; a commit-SHA pin as GitHub does | Nothing may depend on a process surviving; item identity cannot detect drift; a SHA pin is weaker in one direction and noisier in the other | F22, F28 [measured]; 1P platform survey [documented] |
| A stale answer is **void**, not reconciled | Merge the stale answer; defer and re-apply; path-scoped drift test | ADR-0017's rejected option is literally "reconcile stale answers"; drift is 6.9% overall and its cost (SM-081) is unpriced, so a cheaper binding cannot yet be justified | F19 [measured] |
| Expiry is terminal — `fail` / `escalate` / `auto-decide`, chosen per class by a human, resolved at the next read | An implicit default action in code; a timer or cron; no deadline at all | A silent default makes `auto-decide` reachable by omission; a process alive at a future instant is not a mechanism this system has | F22 [measured]; ADR-0017 [documented]; 1P terminal-event survey [documented] |
| Escalation **suspends and resumes into the same step**; it is not activity-terminal (**ADR-0087**) | Activity-terminal escalation, discarding partial work and re-entering from the top — the pre-registered branch had the >1.5× kill fired | Measured 0.45×/0.55×/0.78× against the >1.5× kill, so the kill did not fire — **at n=3, short duration only, with no within-experiment baseline** | F38 [measured, PARTIAL] |
| Review gate is **reviewer proposes, machine confirms** (**ADR-0074**, fully superseding ADR-0004) | Trust the model verdict; trust the model's own evidence label; block on findings without re-execution | The verdict is a weighted coin (3-of-10 approval on a reproduced regression) while the claims are sound (19/19 on target); removing the verdict cost no detection and conformed 52/52 | F29, F31, F27 [measured] |
| **Lenient default** (`narrowed-pin` advises) plus a first-class **Advisory sink** (**ADR-0074, ADR-0082**) | Strict default; drop advisory findings silently; reuse `Quarantined` for advisory | The relaxation is free on detection; the seven adjudicated-true `narrowed-pin` findings are the most expensive evidence the programme produced and need a destination; quarantine on a non-block would record a suppression that never happened | F31 [measured]; **severity call is one operator's judgement** |
| Verification gate: **affected-area selection with the fallback recorded as basis**, plus per-AC mutation evidence (**ADR-0019, ADR-0029, ADR-0073**) | Whole-suite only; per-test Red→Green as sufficient evidence; trust the toolchain's own claim of selection | A ~60× wall difference ran undetected for 26 of 68 invocations; and an AC is a claim about one assertion — inverting AC-015's ordering left 36/36 green | brief §D, F25, F11 [measured] |

| Open item | State | Consumer | Hedge |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P8's **65-minute and 24-hour arms and all three no-kill controls** | Unrun; **BLOCKED** for the "design final" label | ADR-0087; SM-073 | AC-266 enforces the 1.5× bar, not ≤1×; SM-073's 1.0–1.5× band triggers no automatic action; every mid-graph cost sentence names n=3 and the missing baseline |
| **Second-arrival semantics** (does a re-reached gate mint a new mark?) | Not answerable by any spike run — P8 §5.4(3) circular by construction | ADR-0087 clause 4; SM-078 (discovery instrument, cannot fail) | AC-271 is PROVISIONAL with an empty evidence line: the semantics are pinned by a test, not claimed to be evidenced. No `rearm` flag is added **or** ruled out |
| **Gate arrival rate** | Entirely unmeasured — every P8 arrival was forced after the §5.2 fixture design failed | SM-078, SM-082 (both discovery instruments) | Frequency arguments start from zero; placement stays a value decision with a required decision record (AC-273) |
| **Confirmation-bias arm** ("presented as already approved") | Registered and **unrun** (ADR-0088) | ADR-0074's negative-control fixture design; the review card's framing rules | **No claim that the gate is guarded against false approval**; no S-VAR/S-TYPED FP rate offered as overall calibration; the arm is sized before it runs or reports a bound |
| **E3 severity adjudication** — **CLOSED 2026-09-09** | Facts settled 6/6; severity adjudicated 2026-09-04, **NOT BLIND** (filled by the model at Ryan's direction; κ forfeited, the two sheets are not independent raters), **one operator**: 4 BLOCKING, 2 ADVISORY | AC-308, AC-309, SM-086, SM-104 | Both false-positive rates and both MCCs are settled — arm (a) 1/23 / **+0.978**, arm (b) 0/23 / **+1.000** — and both rates stay **upper bounds**, because CLEAN's ground truth is wrong for **≥13 of 23** and may never be a denominator. The design does not depend on them, only claims about its calibration do, and §6 makes none. **A settled severity vocabulary is still owed** — see §10.4 |
| **Advisory-sink readership** | No prior art measured; sink record count is 0 by construction | SM-089 | Landfill kill declared in advance: p90 age above F18's stage→verdict p90 (~9 h) for a month means drop advisory findings at source |
| **Cascade recall loss** | No spike registered | AC-301 | The cascade is **refused at pack validation**, not priced |
| **`ChangeSummary` for binary-change items** | **BLOCKED** — S-BIN registered and unrun; gates Phase 1 | AC-356, ADR-0008 | AC-356 stays PROVISIONAL and its field names are not frozen |
| **Whole-suite p95 per toolchain** | Never recorded | AC-330, SM-102 | Only the declaration half of the doctor rule can fire at first run, and the rule says so |
| **Per-AC pinning coverage** | No baseline; SM-100's ≥0.90 is an aspiration | ADR-0019 | If coverage cannot exceed ~0.5 in real repositories, ADR-0019's "norm" claim is rewritten, not enforced |

Every row above appears in §10's spike register with a named consumer and a "gates Phase 1?" column, per ADR-0012. **Cascade recall loss now does too** — it read "No spike registered" here while ADR-0012 forbids exactly that, and **S-CASCADE** is registered in §10.5. The whole-suite p95, per-AC pinning coverage and advisory-sink readership rows resolve to **first measurements from the running product** rather than to spikes, and are enumerated as such in §10.5's closing paragraph. None is discharged by prose here.

---

## 7. Cost, cache, parallelism and telemetry

### 7.1 The split cache rule — the two mitigations point in opposite directions

**This is the headline of the section and the one thing in it that is routinely got wrong (ADR-0078).** There is no "cache rule". There are two, on two different axes, and each buys its benefit by giving up the other's.

| Axis | Mitigation | What it buys | What it forfeits | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Space** — commits, worktrees, directories | A static `--system-prompt` carrying the card body | Read share **1.000** across commits, against **0.689** for commit-each under the default prompt; **1.000** across steady-state worktrees w2–w10 after a cold w1 at 0.807 | The **shared-prefix** subsidy across elapsed time — not an account-level one; there is no account-wide shared head (§7.2), and "account-wide" is retired alongside "org-scoped": **29,680** tokens created at 66.8 min at read share **0.000** (**n=1**); S-H1b independently 1.000 at 1.5 min → **0.000 / 29,619** at 66.1 min (**n=1**, no finding number). **Two single draws, never an interval** | [measured] F16, 50 dispatches, within-arm range ≤0.001; S-WT (**no finding number — F30 was reserved for S-H1b/S-WT and never written**; the figures live only in the design recommendation and the proposal); F28 as corrected, S-H1b, n=1 per cell |
| **Time** — idle gaps, human gates, resumes | The default prompt (no static card) | Flat cost across the two duration points taken: **18,603** tokens at 65.2 min and **18,603** at 49.7 h — identical to the token, **n=1 per duration point**, at most one prefix rewrite | Share across commits (**0.689**) and across worktrees (**0.685**) | [measured] F28 with its **published correction: the flatness was shared-prefix subsidy, not durability**; F16 |

**The forbidden inference, stated because the earlier proposal made it.** "Held across commits" does not imply "helps across time". That inference is not merely unsupported — it is **falsified** by the same static prompt reading share 0.000 at ~66 min (ADR-0078 clause (i); brief §C.1). Any citation of F16 or S-WT that does not name the *space* axis, or of F28/S-H1b that does not name the *time* axis, is a defect.

The design consequence is that the axis is a **card property, not a global setting**: AC-383 requires every agent card to declare `cache_profile = "static" | "default"` and refuses to load a card that declares neither, and AC-384 makes it a pack-lint error to place a `static`-profile activity immediately after a human gate — the placement that guarantees the ~30k rewrite once the ~1 h boundary passes, against 18,603 flat. That lint fires on a **bracketed** boundary, not a located one: F10 measured a full hit at 45.1 min and share **0.690** at 61.6 min, so the TTL is bracketed in (45.1, 61.6) and **was never located** [measured, n=1 per gap, date-stamped platform behaviour]. It matters because **45% of gate→verdict intervals (n=174) and 48% of stage→verdict intervals (n=191) exceed one hour**, at a gate median of 10.5 min, a stage p90 of ~9 h and a maximum of 226 h [measured, F18 — **one operator, one machine**].

The Phase-1 default is the static `--system-prompt` (ADR-0016's delivery clause, untouched by ADR-0078's partial supersession). **The append route is BLOCKED: S-H1c is registered and unrun**, so `cache_profile` has exactly **two** legal values today — `"static"` and `"default"` — and gains a third **only if S-H1c runs** (AC-383). **There is no shippable append-route option in Phase 1**: the append flags are absent from argv (AC-211). Where this document says the route "may not be enabled as anything but an unmeasured config option", it means that *if* S-H1c ever licenses a third value it enters unmeasured — never that a third value exists today.

### 7.2 Scope, corrected — and one candidate mechanism, marked inferred

The proposal's "the cache is org-scoped" is **withdrawn** (ADR-0078 clause (iii)).

| Path | Isolation boundary | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Claude API · Claude Platform on AWS · Microsoft Foundry | **Workspace**-scoped — a workspace is a sub-unit of an org, so two workspaces in one org share nothing | [documented, 1P] platform.claude.com prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08 |
| Bedrock · Google Cloud Agent Platform | **Org**-scoped | [documented, 1P] as above |
| **Claude Code — the path this design drives** | Additionally **machine + directory + git-status** scoped: cwd, platform, shell, OS version and a git branch/commit snapshot are embedded in the system-prompt layer | [documented, 1P] code.claude.com prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08 |

Two consequences follow directly and are budgeted, not chased:

1. **Two worktrees of one repository never share a prefix.** Every worktree pays a cold first turn. This is **structural, not a rig defect** [documented, 1P], and ADR-0078 forbids any budget that assumes otherwise.
2. **The CLI-version pin survives on a different mechanism.** Not "an upgrade anywhere on the account invalidates a shared head" — there is no account-wide shared head. A new version typically changes the system prompt or tool definitions, so the first request after an upgrade rebuilds from the top; auto-update applies on next launch, never mid-session; and the real exposure is a **resumed** long session reprocessing its history uncached [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08]. That is precisely the human-gate resume path of ADR-0017. AC-385 therefore records the observed CLI version from the child's own `system/init` on every event and attaches a typed warning to every cache metric taken under a mismatch against `TESTED_CLI_VERSION`.

**[inferred]** The embedded cwd/platform/shell/OS/git-status block is a **candidate explanation** for F13's measured two-segment prefix — **~17,439 tokens shared plus ~7,827 per-directory, identical across 19/19 fresh repos** [measured, F13] — which is what reprices a directory change at ~31% rather than 100%. The shapes agree: a machine-and-directory-varying block sitting inside an otherwise shared prefix. **Nothing measured the correspondence and no token-level attribution was attempted.** It is marked inferred and is not licensed as the cause (ADR-0078, evidence section).

### 7.3 TTL: two buckets, two variables, and a contradiction that is open

The TTL is **bucketed, not a single value** [documented, 1P code.claude.com prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 1, severity **high**].

| Bucket | Contents | Default TTL | Raised by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Main conversation | Interactive turns, `-p` runs, Agent SDK | 1 h — **only** on a subscription drawing within plan usage; usage credits or an API key/cloud provider drop it to 5 m | `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` |
| Everything else | **Subagents, workflows, teammates, forks, compaction** | **5 m regardless of billing type** | `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h`, or a subagent's `cacheTtl` frontmatter — itself forced to 5 m on usage-credit billing |

Precedence is layered: `FORCE_PROMPT_CACHING_5M=1` > bucket env var > bucket setting > subagent frontmatter > `ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H=1` > bucket default. Both the setting and the variable require Claude Code **v2.1.242+** [documented, 1P].

**Every dispatch this design generates is bucket two.** The runner therefore sets **both** variables, never one (ADR-0078 clause (ii) forbids setting the first without the second), records the pinned CLI version with the measurement, and records **billing mode — plan-included versus usage credits — as a declared confound on every cache measurement**, because usage-credit billing silently forces 5 m even on an explicit 1 h subagent request. ADR-0010 fixes the adapter to subscription-only auth, which makes plan-usage-versus-credits the live confound rather than the API-key one.

**Setting is not verifying, and the verification surface is contested.** The documentation names `usage.cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` against `ephemeral_5m_input_tokens` as the per-run evidence that a TTL took. **F3 measured those fields ABSENT** on this instrument — `usage` uses plain cache naming and exposes no TTL split, which is why charter E1's TTL-split metric was reported untestable and appears in the brief's register of broken pre-registrations [measured, F3]. The measurement and the first-party documentation cannot both be true of the same instrument at the same pin.

**And the contradiction is two-sided, not one.** F3 is the negative-signed observation — the verification fields are absent. The programme also holds a **positive-signed one pointing the other way**: `references/2026-09-04-findings-for-product.md` §14.5 Part A records **in-session subagents on a 1 h cache, not the documented 5 m**, and `spikes/HANDOFF.md` records that the RFC's "subagents get 5 minutes, always" was **only ever documentation**, with E3 arm (a) named as the first chance to observe it [documented; HANDOFF "What is genuinely unresolved" #3]. So the documented two-bucket rule is contradicted by a measurement in one direction (the fields are absent) and by an observation in the other (the bucket-two default may not hold in-session). **S-TTL1 must settle both**, and until it does neither the 5 m default nor the 1 h override may be asserted of any run on this rig.

> **BLOCKED — cache-TTL verification. Spike S-TTL1, consumer: the cost model and every cache figure in §7.4 and SM-113.** A one-dispatch check on the pinned CLI version with both variables set and billing mode recorded: dump the raw `usage` block verbatim and record whether the two `ephemeral_*` fields are present. Until it runs, **no run may be reported as having a 1 h TTL** — the correct report is **`TTL-unverified`**, and clause (ii)'s verification requirement is a standing obligation that no run on this rig can currently discharge (ADR-0078 clause (iv)). A CLI-version skew between F3's pin and the version that introduced the settings is the obvious candidate reconciliation and is **[inferred] only**; F3's own pin was not re-read. The design acts on the documentation for the **setting** — free and reversible whichever way the fields fall — and refuses to act on it for the **claim**.

`TTL-unverified` is the same treatment F6's quantisation already forced on utilisation figures via `resolution_limited` (§7.6): a flag on the reading, not a silent assumption.

### 7.4 Attribution: one child, one event, one cost row

Attribution is free because every activity is its own child process with its own card, schema, tool set, MCP config and cost record (§3; ADR-0022). It is not a reporting layer bolted on afterwards.

| Rule | Criterion | Mechanism that makes it hard to violate | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Exactly one dispatch event per dispatch, on every termination path — success, `StepError`, cancellation, timeout | **AC-360** | Exhaustive `match` over `StepError` so a new variant fails to compile without an event arm; the `EventStore` fake (ADR-0003) as the assertion surface | [measured] F1, F3, F6 |
| Rollup is the sum of child events and nothing else — no apportionment, allocation or estimated share exists | **AC-361** | Parent record has no cost field; CI grep forbids `apportion`/`allocated_cost`/`estimated_cost` in `rk-kernel` | [documented] ADR-0022 |
| A field the harness does not report is recorded **absent**, never zero | **AC-362** | `Option` with no `Default` impl and no `unwrap_or(0)` at any call site; round-trip test that absent and zero stay distinguishable | [measured] F3 — this is the criterion the missing `ephemeral_*` fields land on |
| A run whose isolation failed is quarantined, not merely failed | **AC-386** → **AC-399** | `StepError::NotIsolated` on a `system/init` tool set unequal to the card's allowlist, never retried; every metric row from that run marked non-comparable and excluded from every aggregate | [measured] F8, brief C.11 |
| Tier 2 is rebuildable-or-disposable, and a partial loss changes no next action | **AC-365**–**AC-370**, **AC-397**, **AC-398**; **AC-164** (§5.4); SM-115, SM-116 | The delete-the-database test applied to the telemetry half: every tier-2 row must be reconstructible from git plus the log, or classified disposable at the point it is written | [documented] ADR-0018; the split test is §5.4's |
| Headroom has exactly one source | **AC-380** | `rate_limit_event` is the **sole** admissible source; no other field, header or estimate may feed a headroom figure, and a degraded reading is `AC-363`'s typed degradation rather than a substituted number | [measured] F1, F6 |
| Telemetry cannot be switched off | **AC-394** | `EventStore` is a required parameter of the dispatch entry point — a build in which an activity can dispatch without one does not compile (`trybuild`) | [documented] ADR-0003 |
| Events are append-only; a correction is a new event | **AC-364** | No `update`/`delete` on the `EventStore` port | [documented] ADR-0018 |

**Isolation is a precondition of every cost figure in this section, not hygiene.** A default `claude -p` inherits the operator's whole environment: 12 MCP servers and ~280 tools, with cache creation **8,243 → 3,611** under `--strict-mcp-config` — MCP alone was **4,631 tokens, 56% of the default cache write** — and tools 280 → 34, at a TTFB of ~1,480 of ~3,400 ms [measured, F8]. The flag must be verified from the child's own `system/init`, because **an ignored flag looks identical to an honoured one from the parent's side** (brief C.11), and F20 shows a card's own `tools` allowlist can silently void a flag at exit 0 with `subtype: success`. SM-114 tracks MCP share of the cache write with a target of 0% on 100% of dispatches, verified from the child.

`redkiln cost <item>` (AC-395) renders the per-activity breakdown — token counters, `total_cost_usd`, wall clock, cache counters, and the batch headroom delta with its `resolution_limited` flag — **from the event store alone, dispatching no model call**, verified with the harness adapter replaced by a fake that panics on dispatch. Reading the record is free: F24, F25, F34, P6, P9, P9b, S-MIG and the F31 adjudication all cost **$0** [measured].

### 7.5 The three budgets

| Budget | Scope | On exhaustion | Rule | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `attempts_total` | Per activity, counting **agent/tool steps per pass**, never summed retries | That counter's failure path only | Harness-internal retries observed on the stream are recorded as tier-2 events and are **not** folded into the budget — the kernel cannot see or bound platform-side behaviour | ADR-0037; **AC-390**, **AC-391** |
| Token appetite | Per item, **fixed at triage** | Typed budget error, a durable mark, `redkiln run` resumes from it | No stage, activity or agent may raise it; raising requires returning the item to triage. It never silently truncates inputs or downgrades the model | ADR-0037; **AC-389**, **AC-392** |
| Wall clock | Per run | That counter's failure path only | The three are independent: none may be replenished, borrowed against or converted into another | **AC-390** |

The appetite is enforced **from the item's own measured token counters and never from a headroom reading**, because a per-item draw is not resolvable at the instrument's resolution (**AC-393**; §7.6). It is set at a human gate bound to the tree hash with a deadline (**AC-396**) and is **explicitly not machine-decidable**: it trades expected quality against cost, and this programme has no measurement that discriminates on quality — five of its metrics saturated, and P7's rigor-default experiment was **powerless at n=3**, every Wilson interval overlapping by construction [measured, F37; brief C.12].

**The quality shorthand's default remains BLOCKED (ADR-0080).** P7's per-run costs are usable as *scale* (§7.8) and may not be used to set a default rigor for a lane. The re-run at **n = 10 per cell** plus a settled severity vocabulary is what unblocks it.

### 7.6 The throttle, and the three things the instrument cannot see

| What the instrument shows | What it cannot show | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `rate_limit_event.unifiedWindows.{five_hour,seven_day}.utilization`, readable only from an **undocumented top-level stream event**; `result.rate_limit` is **null** [measured, F1] | Anything below **0.01** — `utilization` is quantised to one tick, so **per-run cost is not measurable** [measured, F6] | Batch-delta reporting only, with a `resolution_limited` flag (**AC-372**); **no per-run headroom accessor exists**, asserted by a compile-fail test and a CI grep rule (**AC-373**) |
| That `five_hour` and `seven_day` are **proven independent, not mirrors** [measured, F6] | **The binding limit.** There is **no Opus/Sonnet family sub-limit key** in `unifiedWindows` at all [measured, F1] | Throttling runs on the visible windows as a **proxy**, with margins of **≥2 ticks (0.02)** refused below that at configuration load (**AC-377**); no code path may read, derive, estimate or synthesise a family-keyed value (**AC-375**) — an invented figure would be a confident fiction |
| Nothing, at the volumes this programme ran: 40 dispatches / ~1.9M tokens moved `seven_day` **0.100 → 0.100** [measured, F14] | Whether cache reads draw down the window — **E0's cache-cost gate was STRUCK at 0.01 resolution, not answered** [measured, F14, F6] | **The cache is a latency and cost lever, never a headroom lever.** No cache metric may be an input to a headroom figure and no `headroom_saved` field exists anywhere (**AC-381**), enforced by type-level separation plus a positive test so the rule cannot be satisfied by gutting the calculator |

Headroom is quoted **as a batch mean over an explicitly named set, with n on it, and never per run**: **0.0098 `five_hour` and 0.0017 `seven_day` per CLEAN draw**, both derived from 60 draws, 0 void [measured, F29]. **F21's earlier per-draw headroom figure is superseded — it was 7.8× too high** [measured, F29], and reading a per-draw number off a small sample is the exact error AC-373 exists to forbid. SM-111 sets the reporting floor at **batches of ≥10 dispatches** (10 × 0.0098 ≈ 0.098 of the window, about ten ticks) and states plainly that **this instrument saturates at the low end and is kept anyway** — it is the only view of the platform ceiling that exists.

Cache share is likewise not quotable at small n: an identical prompt read **0.584 / 0.698 / 1.000 / 0.722** at n=2, which the brief marks **explicitly NOT a result** [measured, F5]. AC-382 attaches the sample size to every emitted share and flags anything below the configured minimum (SM-113: n=10) as `low_sample`, refused as an input to any automatic decision.

**The measured binding constraint is wall clock, not headroom**: 60 draws took **16.2 h** [measured, F29]. SM-111's kill fires if two or more batches in a month halt on a margin refusal before exhausting their wall-clock budget — at which point the proxy has failed and the parallelism design changes. **Overage has no baseline and none is obtainable from this programme**: no overage payload was ever recorded, so AC-378's *detection* half is blocked pending one and only its retry-policy half (`StepError::Overage` maps to zero attempts) is assertable today. Absence of a report is not a measured zero (SM-112).

Nothing waits. A `StepError::RateLimited { resets_at }` is handled by writing a durable `retry_at` mark and **exiting 0** — no sleep, no poll loop, no live wait (**AC-379**), because nothing may depend on a process surviving [measured, operational, F22; the incident record and its figures are §6.3.1's].

### 7.7 Parallelism, the merge queue, and the fan-out stagger

| Rule | Shape | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Sequential within a tree** | One item's stages run strictly in order; one dispatch, one schema, per activity | Scaling study: sequential decomposition **−39% to −70%**, parallel **+80.9%**, error amplification **17.2× independent versus 4.4× centralised** [preprint, arXiv 2512.08296; Google Research — a v3 revision expanded to 260 configs / 6 benchmarks, the quoted figures are the original 180-config / 4-benchmark version] |
| **Parallel across project-DAG antichains** | One worktree per concurrent story — **required, not an optimisation**: parallel arms need separate fixture trees, because a killed `vitest` leaves a Windows handle that no `Stop-Process` releases [documented, `spikes/HANDOFF.md` defect (3), #26/#30], so a shared tree cannot be reclaimed after a cancel (§3.8.2, **S-CANCEL-WIN**) | [documented] per-agent worktree isolation is the 2026 default for parallel coding agents, with the convention "worktree-per-task, assign agents to worktrees rather than owning them permanently" [blog, augmentcode.com, fetched 2026-09-08] |
| **Serialised at a merge queue** | The queue, not the agent, is the authority on what lands | [vendor] Mergify *State of Merge Queues 2026*, 200,000+ merges across 477 teams: AI-assisted PRs broke main **1.9%** versus **4.4%** for non-AI **when routed through a merge queue** — the risk reduction appears to come from the queue, not from the agent being safer |
| **Per-story failure isolation** | A failed story quarantines its dependents rather than failing the antichain | **[inferred]** from a documented negative premise, and no criterion instruments it. The premise is [documented]: a worktree buys **file-level isolation only** — not task decomposition, semantic conflicts or merge selection, which stay the orchestrator's job [blog, augmentcode.com]. **That premise says nothing about quarantining dependents**; the quarantine rule is this design's own inference from it, and it is registered as owing a criterion in §7.10 |
| **Fan-out staggered and CPU-bounded** | Identical-prefix siblings dispatched ~**5 s** apart so the first pays the cold write; the interval is configurable, **including to 0** | [documented, 1P] workflow fan-outs stagger identical-prefix parallel agents by up to 5 seconds by default; **AC-388** requires the configurable interval so SM-114's A/B has a comparison arm |

**The stagger's benefit has no local measurement.** The rule is `[documented]` from platform documentation and nothing in this programme measured it. SM-114 makes it a decision rule rather than a target — first-write-pays share among siblings, staggered (`i` = 5 s) against simultaneous (`i` = 0), A/B over **n ≥ 50 sibling pairs** — and **if the A/B shows no difference at n ≥ 50, AC-388's stagger requirement is dropped**. Until it runs the stagger is tracked, not decisive.

**The per-worktree cost is refused a number, deliberately (AC-387, CONTESTED EVIDENCE).** Brief §C.1 states that `--system-prompt` holds 1.000 across worktrees w2–w10 per S-WT, i.e. that the combination *was* measured and costs ~0 after the first; F16's worktree-each arm measured **0.685**, i.e. ~31% unmitigated. **The brief does not record which prompt configuration S-WT ran**, and the two readings point opposite ways. The cost model is therefore required to return an explicit **`Unmeasured(cell)`** refusal naming the cell rather than a number, and may never interpolate a cell from two separately-measured arms. **Resolving this needs the S-WT run record, not another spike** — and a cost model that shipped 0.685 hard-coded would misprice a parallel fan-out by roughly 31× if §C.1 is right. This is the single most consequential defect the acceptance-criteria pass caught in the area.

#### The merge queue and the worktree lifecycle — the instruments, which §7.7 previously stated without

The four rules above were stated as policy with one criterion between them. `ac-git-reconcile.md` §1.7 (**AC-189**–**AC-198**) owns them, and is cited here rather than restated:

| Rule | Criterion |
| --- | --- |
| Strictly sequential **within** a tree; **one worktree per antichain member** | **AC-189** |
| Worktree rows are keyed by **SHA, never by path** — the same keying rule §5.5 applies to every repo-referencing row | **AC-190** |
| The review clone is constructed against F17's object-store escape: `--depth 1`, no `alternates`, no remote to the parent | **AC-191**, **AC-192** (and §6.4.3's AC-292, which states the same shape from the review-gate side — the two areas must be reconciled to one instrument) |
| **No process survives** in the clone; `repro.command` is **executable inside** it; MCP is isolated there | **AC-193**, **AC-194**, **AC-195** |
| **The merge queue serialises**: the queue, not the agent, is the authority on what lands | **AC-196** |
| Admission is `GreenAt(sha)` against a declared basis — a green result at a different basis does not admit | **AC-197** |
| Nothing in the queue depends on a **surviving process** — F22's rule applied to the landing path | **AC-198** |

Mergify's figure (1.9% against 4.4%) is the *motivation* for having a queue at all; **AC-196**–**AC-198** are what make this one checkable, and the per-story quarantine rule above still has none.

**The documented worst case in the whole design.** `/compact` reads from cache when warm, but **after a break longer than the TTL it reprocesses the entire history uncached — the single most expensive documented call pattern**; `/rewind` truncates back to an already-cached prefix and is explicitly recommended over it for discarding a dead-end path [documented, 1P code.claude.com prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08]. Triggering `/compact` immediately after an idle human-gate wait is exactly that pattern, and ADR-0017 institutionalises idle human-gate waits. Phase 1 does not compact after a gate; it resumes from the durable mark and, where a session must be shortened, rewinds.

**Mid-graph placement is PROVISIONAL, not decided (ADR-0087).** Mid-graph resume was lossless across a real `taskkill /T /F` — 0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical — at **0.45× / 0.55× / 0.78×** of ~18.6k against a >1.5× kill, i.e. **8,408 / 10,314 / 14,579** tokens [measured, F38; **n=3**]. But **all three runs sat inside F10's ~1 h bracket** (measured `gap_minutes` 0.9, 28.4, 29.1, notwithstanding the arm's `0-delay` label), **P8's 65-minute and 24-hour arms and all three no-kill controls are unrun**, and there is no within-experiment baseline — the comparison is only to F28. "Cheaper than a stage gate" may not be stated without n=3 and the missing baseline named in the same sentence, which is what this paragraph does.

### 7.8 The cost table

All in-programme figures are [measured] and are quoted from the evidence brief.

| Unit | Cost | Wall clock | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Trivial haiku dispatch | $0.021–0.027 | — | F3 |
| E3 S-SILENT item | $0.45–0.64 | 97–424 s | brief §D |
| E3 CLEAN item | $1.06–4.89 | 317–2,055 s | brief §D |
| P7 graph run, `optional` | **$1.02** | — | F37 — **instrument confound:** this arm ran **wholly on `claude` 2.1.263** |
| P7 graph run, `recommended` (adds `adversary → policy`) | **$2.69** — **2.64×** the optional block | — | F37 — **instrument confound:** the CLI moved to **2.1.265 during P7**, catching exactly this arm's **r3 draw on all ten items** while `optional` ran wholly on 2.1.263. F37 §7 reports the per-repeat split and no effect is detectable; **the arms are nonetheless not instrument-matched**, and the 2.64× is quoted as scale under that confound |
| P7 graph run, `mandatory` | **No price is quoted, here or anywhere in §7.** The arm was dropped at **n=1**, and DECISIONS-2026-09-09 #7/#33 record that it priced an **unreachable configuration** — at `rigor = mandatory` with `has_repro` the engine selects `bisect`, which carries no block at all. Its single run's ratio is additionally **unreconciled: 3.66× in F37 against 4.4× in the decisions log** | — | F37, brief §E; DECISIONS-2026-09-09 #7, #33 |
| P7 adversary dispatch | $1.68 | 676 s | F37 |
| **Execution-grade review (S-TYPED CLEAN), the Phase-1 review tier** | **median $3.03**; the typed contract cost **+74%** over the prose comparator ($112.25 against $64.61) | **median 1,071 s (~18 min)**, slowest **4,561 s** | F31 |
| Whole spike programme, per campaign | E3 $153.86 (arm a $97.39 against arm b $56.47 = **1.72×**) · E3-H $36.18 · F28 $18.85 · S-VAR $176.96 · S-TYPED $112.25 · P1 arm L $21.37 · **P1 arm D's scoring $0** — 140 truth cells scored offline at the pin, model cost zero [measured, F39] · P7 $131.62 of a $200 cap · P8 **$3.01 of a $40 cap** · AFK session $156.00 of $300 | S-VAR 16.2 h for 60 draws | brief §D; F39 |

**The review tier's price is what selects it — on ADR-0015's authority, and this is NOT the ADR-0080 quality shorthand.** ADR-0015's stage weight picks the *review tier* — read-only advisory against execution-grade — and the fix lane ships execution-grade as an **authored ADR-0015 decision**, taken independently of P7 and not read off any figure in this table. The tier costs **+74% and a median of ~18 minutes** [measured, F31], and what it buys is what §6 establishes: findings whose evidence class is computed in Rust and re-executed before anything blocks. **What P7's per-run costs may not do is set a default rigor for a lane** — that is the BLOCKED row of §7.10 (ADR-0080), it is a different decision from the ADR-0015 tier assignment, and no figure in this table is used for it.

External comparators, quoted for orientation only and never as targets [documented]:

| Comparator | Figure | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cloudflare, *Orchestrating AI Code Review at scale* | Overall average **$1.19**; median **$0.98** — **the label is unreconciled**: the evidence brief calls it the *read-only tier* median, the 2026-09-08 re-fetch calls it a median *pooled across all tiers*; both readings are carried and the discrepancy is in the label, not the figure (§11) — full tier **$1.68**† | 131,246 review runs across **48,095 merge requests** over 30 days; "break glass" **288 times = 0.6% of merge requests** — the only public false-block comparator this pass found [1P/blog, blog.cloudflare.com/ai-code-review/, fetched 2026-09-08] |
| Anthropic Code Review | $15–25 | Verification-heavy tier [documented] |
| Agentless against CodeR | **$0.70/instance at 32.00%** against **$3.34 at 28.33%** | Cheaper *and* better on that benchmark; a warning against pricing rigor by spend alone [documented] |

> **†Footnote, because pairing these two figures unfootnoted invites a wrong reading.** **$1.68 is the full tier's MEAN, not its median. The full tier's median is $1.47**, and **$0.98 is a median pooled across all tiers**. Reading $1.68 as a median against $0.98 overstates the tier's premium. Everything else in the Cloudflare set re-verified verbatim on the 2026-09-08 fetch [1P/blog; research pass §1 row 13, §4 rec 14].

**Wall clock is the binding constraint and the timeouts must be sized from measurement, not from the fast condition.** SM-117 requires every activity kind's configured timeout to sit above its measured p90. This has already failed once: a **900 s default written for the fast condition cost a CLEAN record** before being raised to 2,400 → 3,600 with one 5,400 s retry; and a `confirm`-step defect ran the **whole suite for 26 of 68 invocations** where `npx vitest run <file>` is 1 file / 6 tests / **15.6 s** — a ~60× difference [measured, brief §D]. The full verification gate today has **no affected-area selection**: every run is the whole 236-file suite, ~250 s to >900 s over 1,890–1,927 tests [measured].

### 7.9 Decisions in this section

| Decision | Alternatives rejected | Why | Record |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| State the cache mitigation as **two rules on two axes**, never one; declare the axis per card | One global cache rule; pick the static prompt and claim the default prompt's durability | The two mitigations are in opposite directions and the cross-axis inference is falsified, not merely unsupported | **ADR-0078** (i); AC-383, AC-384 |
| Set **both** TTL variables; report a run as `TTL-unverified` absent per-run `ephemeral_*` evidence | Set only `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL`; hold the TTL clause at `proposed` until S-TTL1 runs | Every dispatch here is subagent/workflow traffic on a 5 m default; setting both is free and reversible, while the *claim* needs evidence the rig may not be able to produce | **ADR-0078** (ii), (iv) |
| Restate the scope as workspace/org/machine+directory+git-status; retire "org-scoped" | Restate as "workspace-scoped" and stop | A wrong scope licenses a wrong mitigation (coordinating account-wide upgrades), which is worse than no claim | **ADR-0078** (iii); AC-385 |
| Batch-delta headroom with `resolution_limited`; **no per-run accessor exists** | Report per-run headroom; interpolate below the tick | `utilization` is quantised to 0.01 and a per-draw figure read off a small sample was already wrong by 7.8× | ADR-0003; AC-372, AC-373, AC-374; F6, F29 |
| Throttle on `five_hour`/`seven_day` with ≥2-tick margins; never synthesise a family value | Derive a per-family estimate; throttle on a fixed dispatch rate from day one | The binding sub-limit has no key at all; a synthesised figure is unfalsifiable, and SM-112's kill is what tells us the proxy failed | ADR-0003; AC-375, AC-376, AC-377; F1 |
| Cache is a latency/cost lever; **no cache metric feeds a headroom figure** | Credit cache reads against the window | The question is **unanswerable** at 0.01 resolution — E0's gate was struck, not answered | ADR-0016, ADR-0003; AC-381; F14, F6 |
| Sequential within a tree; parallel across antichains, one worktree each, serialised at a merge queue | Parallel decomposition within an item; agents merging their own work | Sequential decomposition loses 39–70% of the benefit; the measured main-breakage reduction comes from the queue, not the agent | ADR-0003, ADR-0018; arXiv 2512.08296, Mergify 2026 |
| Cost model returns **`Unmeasured(cell)`** rather than a number for an unmeasured (profile × axis) cell | Hard-code 0.685 (~31%); hard-code ~0 from S-WT | The two readings point opposite ways and the brief does not say which configuration S-WT ran; either choice misprices fan-out by ~31× | **AC-387** — evidence contest recorded, not resolved |

| Item | State | Named spike / consumer | Hedge held meanwhile |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Do the `ephemeral_1h_*`/`ephemeral_5m_*` fields exist on this instrument? | **BLOCKED** — F3 measured them absent, first-party docs say they are the verification surface; the two contradict | **S-TTL1** → the cost model, SM-113, every cache figure in §7 (ADR-0078 iv) | Both variables set; every run reported **`TTL-unverified`**; billing mode recorded as a declared confound on every cache measurement |

| What does a worktree cost under a static prompt? | **CONTESTED** — §C.1 (S-WT, ~0) against F16's worktree-each arm (0.685, ~31%) | Recovering the **S-WT run record** (not a new spike) → the fan-out cost model, AC-387 | The model refuses the cell with `Unmeasured(cell)`; per-worktree cold cache budgeted as structural |

| Is mid-graph resume genuinely cheaper than a stage gate? | **PROVISIONAL** — n=3, all inside the ~1 h bracket, **65-min and 24-h arms and all three controls unrun**, no within-experiment baseline | **P8's unrun arms and controls** (ADR-0087) → gate-placement guidance | Placement is safe under either answer; the claim is never made without n=3 and the missing baseline in the same sentence |
| Does the 5-second fan-out stagger pay? | **UNMEASURED locally** — `[documented]` from platform docs only | SM-114's A/B at **n ≥ 50 sibling pairs** → AC-388 | Interval configurable including to 0; no benefit claimed; **the requirement is dropped if the A/B shows no difference** |
| What does an overage envelope look like? | **No payload ever recorded**; no baseline obtainable from this programme | A recorded overage envelope → AC-378's detection half, SM-112 | Only the retry-policy half asserted (`Overage` → zero attempts); the adapter test runs against a synthetic fixture, and absence of a report is not reported as a measured zero |
| The static prompt's cost across a **24-hour** gap | **Never taken** (S-H1b); bounded below by 29,619 and unbounded above | The **h1b 24-h read** → AC-384's lint figures, SM-113's time-axis cell | The time-axis cell is reported as measured at ~66 min only |
| A default rigor for a lane, priced from P7's per-run costs | **BLOCKED** (ADR-0080) — P7's primary criterion is **powerless at n=3** | The **n = 10 per cell re-run** plus a settled severity vocabulary → §7.5, SM-110 | P7's figures are used as **scale only**; each quality block is authored explicitly per activity; the mandatory arm's **3.66×/4.4×** ratio stays flagged unreconciled |

The full register, with costs and a "gates Phase 1?" column, is §10. Every row above now appears there — including the S-WT run-record recovery (**S-WT-REC**), which had a consumer but no register row until this revision, and the lane-default row, whose authority is ADR-0080's block and **not** the ADR-0015 tier decision §7.8 takes.
