## 9. Rust realisation — crates, dependencies, and engineering policy

**Read this section knowing what grounds it.** The external pass asked directly whether authoritative guidance exists for a ~12-crate, one-binary, per-adapter-crate workspace and answered **no** — "feature-unification mechanics are well documented; 'best practice for this exact shape' is practitioner synthesis" (evidence-augmentation §5, fetched 2026-09-08) [documented]. Everything below the dependency table is therefore **synthesis**, recorded as ADR-0083 and ADR-0084 so it is citable rather than assumed. The same pass found, by direct repository inspection and in this severity order: **no `.github/workflows` directory exists in the repository at all**; **no `rust-toolchain.toml`** anywhere; **no `[lints]` table and no `clippy.toml`**; and **no `tests/` directory**, with 17 files using inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (evidence-augmentation §3, Rust practice, direct inspection — **no URL**) [measured]. Those four absences are the starting position, not a risk forecast.

### 9.1 The crate graph

Ten crate families. The column that changed since the rev. 4 proposal is **runtime**: ADR-0076 makes every port trait sync and confines every `.await` to an adapter, so `tokio` is **not** a workspace-wide dependency — it appears in adapter manifests only, and `async-trait` never enters the graph at all.

| Crate | Owns | Origin | May depend on an async runtime? | Evidence / decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `rk-types` | ids; typed artifacts (`Spec`, `ImplReport`, `Findings`, `DocSet`, digests); schema derivation; `StepError`; `Drift`; `GateBasis` | new | **No** | ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0075; `StepError` shape carried from `spikes/runner/src/step.rs` [measured, direct inspection] |
| `rk-types` — `ChangeSummary` | the non-diff change type | new | — | **BLOCKED.** S-BIN is registered and **unrun**; no review evidence of any kind exists for items whose change is not a text diff (ADR-0008, ADR-0012). The crate ships the other artifact types; `ChangeSummary`'s field set is **not fixed in Phase 1** and the five toolchain-keyed kinds (ADR-0073) cannot be finalised until S-BIN runs. See §0.1 and §10 |
| `rk-kernel` | packs (process + technology), stages/activities, policy, memo, gates, budgets, retry, replay | new | **No — forbidden** | ADR-0021, ADR-0022, ADR-0086; ADR-0076 forbids `tokio`, `async-std`, `futures` and `async-trait` here. No dependency edge, direct or transitive, on any `rk-adapter-*` (**AC-484**); no generic parameter bound by a port trait (**AC-480**) |
| `rk-store` | the typed `.bklg`/`.kb` store, canonical serialiser, markdown rendering, locks, index | new | No | ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0018; layout and the single write path are §5's |
| `rk-git` | trailers, `reconcile()`, worktrees, disposable clones, merge queue | `reconcile.rs` rewritten in the spike + new | No | ADR-0003 (git is **not** a port; a concrete crate the kernel names — **AC-483**); `reconcile.rs` validated on 360 commits / 74 slices [measured, F12]. **gix scope is restricted by construction — see §9.2.1** |
| `rk-ports` | the five port traits and one fake each | new | **No** | ADR-0003, ADR-0076. Fake count equals trait count and the equality is asserted as a number (**AC-481**); divergence is measured by **SM-151** |
| `rk-adapter-{sqlite,claude,github,mdbook}`, `rk-adapter-toolchain-{node,dotnet,python,unreal}` | one crate per adapter; each owns its own runtime handle, constructed once at the composition root and never per call | mixed — `-claude` lifts `runner.rs`, `event.rs`, `step.rs`, `throttle.rs`, `journal.rs` from `spikes/runner` | **Yes — and only here** | ADR-0003, ADR-0076; `-claude` is subscription-only and refuses construction when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is present (ADR-0010, **AC-488**) |
| `rk-adapter-codex` | the second harness | new | Yes | ADR-0007 — validation-only, and **S-CODEX is unrun**; it is not in Phase 1 |
| `rk-cli` | `clap` router; one idempotent `run` entry; `answer`, `status`, `doctor`. **The composition root** | new | Yes (it constructs adapters) | ADR-0009, ADR-0011; the adapter set is resolved once at startup and is a run-scoped constant (**AC-485**), and an adapter that is not compiled in or is misconfigured refuses the run before any spawn or store write (**AC-486**) |
| `redkiln-migrate` | the old-corpus converter; a separate binary | new | Yes | ADR-0002, ADR-0013; behaviour and the migration differential are §8's |
| `rk-plugin-gen` | emit harness artifacts from packs and cards | new | Yes | Phase 2 (ADR-0011); the distribution surface and its borrowed-evidence caveat are §8's |

**Three properties the graph holds by construction, not by care.** `anyhow` exists in exactly one crate family (`rk-cli` and the two other binaries); the process reads its environment in exactly one place (the composition root, while building `Config`); and the async runtime exists in exactly the crates that spawn processes or open sockets. Each is a manifest-level fact a reviewer can check by reading `Cargo.toml`. **Only one of the three is instrumented today:** the runtime property is carried by **AC-484** (no `rk-kernel` → `rk-adapter-*` edge) plus the ADR-0076 dependency ban listed in §9.2's "where it may appear" column. **The `anyhow`-in-one-crate-family and environment-read-in-one-place properties have no criterion in any of the thirteen area files** — they are conventions with a manifest-level *shape*, not checks, and a manifest-level assertion for each is owed. §9.3's structural-assertion list is where they would live; until they are authored, "by construction, not by care" is true only of the third. [inferred, no criterion — recorded rather than asserted]

**What the sync-port decision changed in the imported code.** `spikes/runner` is async top to bottom — `tokio` with `rt-multi-thread`, `macros`, `process`, `io-util`, `time`, `sync` [measured, direct inspection of `spikes/runner/Cargo.toml`]. Under ADR-0076 that body survives unchanged **inside** `rk-adapter-claude`, wrapped by a sync port method that blocks on an adapter-owned runtime. The two consequences ADR-0076 records are carried here rather than softened: an adapter must never call `Handle::block_on` from a thread its own runtime is driving, and the `-p` stream events of F4 must be consumed inside the adapter and surfaced as a completed value — the kernel cannot iterate a stream.

### 9.2 Dependencies

Two provenance classes, kept apart deliberately. **Proven** means the crate is in `spikes/runner/Cargo.toml` today and has run the P-series [measured, direct inspection, 2026-09-08]. **New** means the version was verified in the external pass on 2026-09-08 and has never been compiled in this repository. ADR-0084 explicitly forbids citing its version list as a current-versions source after 2026-09-08; this table inherits that expiry.

| Dependency | Version | Pin style | Where it may appear | Proven in `spikes/runner`? | Source / note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `serde` / `serde_json` | 1.0.229 / 1.0.151 | caret | everywhere | **proven** | direct inspection [measured] |
| `thiserror` | 2.0.20 | caret | **library crates only** | **proven** | ADR-0083; `StepError` already uses it |
| `anyhow` | 1.0.104 | caret | **binary crates only** | **proven** | ADR-0083 — the split rests on **two 2026 practitioner blogs and no first-party source** (see §9.3) |
| `clap` (derive) | 4.6.6 | caret | `rk-cli`, the two other binaries | **proven** | direct inspection [measured] |
| `tokio` | 1.53.1 | caret | **adapter crates only** | **proven** | ADR-0076 forbids it in `rk-kernel`/`rk-ports`/`rk-types` |
| `minijinja` | 2.24.0 | caret | `rk-store` (rendering) | **proven** | markdown is a rendering, never a source (ADR-0005) |
| `toml` | 1.1.5 | caret | `rk-cli`, pack loading | **proven** | direct inspection [measured] |
| `sha2` | 0.11.0 | caret | digests, memo keys, provenance | **proven** | `fingerprint::sha256_hex` [measured] |
| `chrono` (serde) | 0.4.45 | caret | event log, journal headers | **proven** | direct inspection [measured] |
| `jsonschema` | runner has **0.52.1**; current is **0.55.1** | caret, `default-features = false` | boundary validation | **proven at 0.52.1**; the 0.55.1 move is new | `default-features = false` removes network and filesystem `$ref` resolution and **not** regex/format validation [1P docs.rs/jsonschema, fetched 2026-09-08]. **Bus-factor note:** essentially one primary author per crates.io publisher records [1P crates.io] |
| `schemars` | 1.2.2 (MIT, published 2026-07-27) | caret | `rk-types` | **new** | stable, out of alpha. Data-carrying enum variants still derive to `oneOf` **by default** — see §9.5 [1P docs.rs/schemars] |
| `rusqlite` (`bundled`) | 0.40.2 (2026-08-08, MIT) | caret | `rk-adapter-sqlite` only | **new** | WAL + `busy_timeout` + **one writer connection or a queued write path, never a pool** (ADR-0084). The WAL semantics were read **via search only, not against docs.rs/rusqlite** — evidence-augmentation §5 asks for a re-fetch [documented, **UNVERIFIED**] |
| `gix` | **0.87.1** | **`= "=0.87.1"` — exact** | `rk-git`, **read path only** | **new** | **Restricted scope — §9.2.1** (ADR-0077, ADR-0084) |
| `insta` | 1.48.0 | caret | dev-dependency | **new** | carries the golden schema snapshot (§9.5) |
| `trycmd`, `assert_cmd` | 1.2.1, 2.2.2 | caret | dev-dependencies | **new** | CLI-surface tests; §8 owns the command surface |
| `cargo-audit` | version not verified in the pass | CI tool, not linked | CI | **new** | ADR-0084 makes it a required job; **its own version was never checked** [documented, gap] |
| `cargo-semver-checks`, `git-cliff`, `release-plz`, `cargo-dist` | 0.50.0 (2026-08-01), 2.14.1 (2026-09-01), 0.3.163 (2026-09-08), 0.32.0 | release tooling, not linked | release pipeline (§8) | **new** | **Naming hazard:** the CLI rebranded to `dist` at v0.24.0 but the crate is still published as `cargo-dist`, and crates.io separately carries an **unrelated abandoned crate literally named `dist` at 0.0.0** — depending on `dist` is forbidden (ADR-0084) [1P crates.io / axodotdev] |

**A clean row is not a clean bill.** No advisory check was run in the external pass for `schemars`, `insta`, `trycmd`, `assert_cmd`, `cargo-semver-checks`, `git-cliff` or `release-plz` — that is *absence of evidence*, and ADR-0084 says so in terms. The CI `cargo audit` job against the committed `Cargo.lock` is what will actually establish their status; it is also the reason the job is part of the decision rather than a nice-to-have, since rustsec.org 404'd on direct fetch and the `gix-*` advisory list below was reconstructed per-advisory from the GitHub Advisory Database and **is not guaranteed complete** (evidence-augmentation §5) [documented].

#### 9.2.1 `gix` — restricted scope, exact pin, and an audit job that watches `gix-*`

`gix` is at **0.87.1**: pre-1.0, and a facade over dozens of independently versioned `gix-*` sub-crates. It is multi-maintainer (GitoxideLabs, Sebastian Thiel leading), so it is not a single-author risk — it is a 0.x risk with load-bearing semantics behind it [documented, 1P crates.io + GitHub org, fetched 2026-09-08].

| Advisory | Sub-crate | Nature | Fixed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RUSTSEC-2025-0021 / CVE-2025-31130 | `gix-features` | missing SHA-1 collision detection | ≥ 0.71.0 |
| RUSTSEC-2025-0001 | `gix-worktree-state` | checkout sets files world-writable | ≥ 0.17.0 |
| RUSTSEC-2024-0349 | `gix-worktree` | path traversal / arbitrary file write via crafted repo names, described as enabling RCE via malicious hooks | before 0.87.1 |
| RUSTSEC-2024-0351 / 0353 | `gix` | Windows reserved device names | before 0.87.1 |
| RUSTSEC-2024-0355 / 0367 / 0371 | `gix-path` | config resolution | before 0.87.1 |

*Source: [1P] GitHub Advisory Database / RustSec, via evidence-augmentation §3, fetched 2026-09-08 [documented].*

**Every one is fixed at or before 0.87.1, so the pin is CLEAN as of 2026-09-08.** The density is what drives the policy, not the current status: the cluster is **checkout and path handling, several of them Windows-specific**, which is precisely the disposable-worktree and shallow-clone-of-an-external-branch surface a merge queue exercises hardest, on the platform this system is being built on. Hence three rules from ADR-0084, none of which is optional:

1. `gix = "=0.87.1"` — an **exact** pin, never a caret range. Other 0.x dependencies adopted later inherit the rule; stable 1.x dependencies use ordinary caret ranges, because pinning everything is cargo cult.
2. `cargo audit` runs against the committed `Cargo.lock` on every push and pull request, **watching `gix-*` specifically**, and a new advisory fails the build. The pin *without* the audit would be strictly worse than a caret range, because a pin makes security patches arrive only when someone bumps it.
3. Bumping the pin is a deliberate PR that re-runs `cargo audit`, re-checks the `gix-*` sub-crate set in `Cargo.lock`, and re-runs **S-GIX** (ADR-0077) if the pin crosses a version where either arm's answer could have changed. gix currency is a **release-blocking check**, not a one-time approval. The **Windows CI target is not optional** given where the cluster sits.

**Scope, and what stays blocked.** gix owns `rev-parse`, status, shallow clone by SHA and trailer **parsing**. The write path — `git worktree add/remove`, `git interpret-trailers --in-place` / `git commit --trailer` — shells out (ADR-0077). The two documented gaps that keep it that way are stated in full in **§5.9** and are not restated here: the unimplemented `GIT_COMMON_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` interplay, precisely the mechanism a store at `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`/redkiln/ leans on, and trailer **writing** as an **UNVERIFIED absence rather than a proven one** over what is tier-1 state, named in the pass itself as the highest-value single check in its list [documented, 1P GitoxideLabs/gitoxide `crate-status.md`, fetched 2026-09-08; evidence-augmentation §1 rows 2 and 8, and §5]. **This document makes no claim that gix can write a trailer.** Both are discharged by **S-GIX**, whose named consumer is `rk-git`'s write path (§5, §10). The shell-out route is decided and unblocked; only gix's ownership of it is not.

### 9.3 Engineering policy (ADR-0083)

| Policy | The rule | What it forbids | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Edition** | `edition = "2024"` on every crate, set once as a workspace package field and inherited | — | `spikes/runner/Cargo.toml` already carries it — settled by demonstration [measured, direct inspection] |
| **Toolchain** | A root `rust-toolchain.toml` pinning an **exact** rustc version (`channel = "1.NN.N"`), declaring at minimum `rustfmt` and `clippy` | a floating `channel = "stable"`; having no `rust-toolchain.toml`; substituting `package.rust-version` | A pin and a floor answer different questions and are not substitutes; the product is one binary plus internal crates, so the pin is the mechanism [documented, [blog] swatinem.de + [1P] RFC 3537 — the "pin for a binary you control" framing is the blog's] |
| **Lints — location** | `[workspace.lints.rust]` / `[workspace.lints.clippy]` in the root manifest; every member carries `[lints] workspace = true` **and nothing else** | `#![deny]`/`#![allow]` in source as a restatement of policy; a per-crate exception without a comment naming why | one place to read the policy, one place a divergence shows [documented, 1P Cargo Book "Lints"; rust-lang/cargo #13157] |
| **Lints — content** | `clippy::all = "deny"`; an **enumerated** pedantic subset at deny — `semicolon_if_nothing_returned`, `explicit_iter_loop`, `match_same_arms`, `redundant_closure_for_method_calls`, `items_after_statements`; `clippy::nursery = "warn"` only; `unsafe_code = "deny"` | denying `clippy::pedantic` or `clippy::nursery` **as groups** | `clippy::correctness` is the only deny-by-default group; pedantic and nursery are documented false-positive-prone, and **nursery lints can change or be removed without notice**, so a group deny across a dozen crates is a CI outage waiting on the next clippy release [documented, 1P Clippy Lints book]. Adding a lint to the subset is a routine PR; the subset is a list, never a group name |
| **Errors** | `thiserror` in libraries so callers can match variants; `anyhow` **confined to binaries** | `anyhow::Error` in any library crate's public API | Restated by **two 2026 practitioner blogs and by no first-party source** [blog, OneUptime 2026-01-25; Pi Stack 2026-06-22]. Adopted as a house convention **on that basis** — it is not a documented standard, and this document does not present it as one |
| **Tests** | Unit tests stay inline in `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`; integration tests consolidate into at most **one binary per crate under test**, `tests/it/main.rs` with `mod` declarations per case | one integration-test file per case | One file is one binary relinking the whole stack; consolidating Cargo's own suite cut build time **3×** and artifact size **5×** [measured, external — [blog] matklad.github.io, measured on Cargo's suite by its author. It licenses the direction and the mechanism, **not** a predicted 3×/5× for this workspace]. The current total absence of any integration-level test against a public API is the fourth measured gap, recorded as a known one |
| **Configuration** | Configuration reaches the kernel **as a value** — a `Config` constructed at the composition root and passed in. The process may read the environment **once**, at the edge, while building it | `std::env::set_var` / `remove_var` **anywhere in the workspace, tests included**; the kernel reading the process environment at all | `set_var`/`remove_var` became `unsafe fn` in edition 2024 because POSIX `setenv` is not thread-safe and races a concurrent `getenv` — directly implicating `cargo test`'s default multi-threaded harness [documented, 1P Rust Edition Guide; rust-lang/rust PR #124636]. This licenses "environment *mutation* is unsound under the default harness"; it does **not** license a claim that reading the environment once at process start is unsafe, which it is not |
| **CI** | A workflow from the first commit, on every push and PR, on the pinned toolchain: `cargo fmt --all --check` · `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` · `cargo test --workspace` · `cargo audit` (ADR-0084) | merging without all four green | **There is no CI at all today** — the single largest of the four measured absences [measured, direct inspection]. This is specified as a **floor that must never again be absent**, not a ceiling |

**The env-var ban and the child's environment are different things.** The ban is on this workspace reading or mutating *its own* process environment outside the composition root. Constructing the *child's* environment explicitly — including `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` and `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` in the runner env (ADR-0078) — is the opposite behaviour and is required: it is "explicit over discovered", and it is what F8 forces, since a default `claude -p` inherited 12 MCP servers and ~280 tools with MCP alone accounting for **4,631 tokens = 56%** of the cache write [measured, F8]. Whether either variable took effect is a **BLOCKED** question, not a settled one: the `ephemeral_1h_*`/`ephemeral_5m_*` fields that would verify it were **measured absent** from `usage` (F3), which is S-TTL1's job (ADR-0078) and §7's subject. Nothing here may be read as verifying a TTL.

**CI carries structural assertions, not only lints.** The AC set names instruments that have to live somewhere, and that somewhere is this workflow: a `cargo metadata` dependency-graph assertion that no `rk-kernel` → `rk-adapter-*` edge exists (**AC-484**); an API-surface lint rejecting port-bound generic parameters in `rk-kernel` (**AC-480**); the port-inventory lint with a denylist on the git surface (**AC-483**); the `rk-ports` count assertion `traits.len() == fakes.len()` (**AC-481**); a grep rule forbidding `unwrap_or(0)` / `unwrap_or_default()` on the usage struct's fields (**AC-362**); and the golden schema snapshot of §9.5. Each is cheap, deterministic and zero model cost — which is the only reason a rule stated in prose is worth converting into one.

### 9.4 Two things this policy does not settle

| Open item | Why it is open | Named spike / check | Named consumer | Hedge |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Per-adapter feature isolation** | Resolver v2 scopes feature unification by target kind but still unifies workspace-wide *within* a target for any shared dependency, so a `cargo build --workspace` can leak a test-only or adapter-only feature into the shipped binary's graph. The only reliable isolations are separate `cargo build -p` invocations per adapter, or identical feature sets across every crate sharing a dependency. ADR-0083 records this as **left open and not to be assumed away** [documented, [blog] nickb.dev; 1P Rust internals] | **S-FEAT** (registered in §10.5) — build the workspace both ways against the Phase-1 adapter set and diff the resolved feature sets in `Cargo.lock`/`cargo tree -e features` for the shipped binary | the Phase-1 release build and the `cargo-dist` pipeline (§8) | Until S-FEAT runs, the release build uses **per-crate `cargo build -p`**, which is correct under either answer and costs only CI wall time |
| **rusqlite multi-process WAL and `busy_timeout` semantics** | The single-writer rule rests on general SQLite behaviour read **via search, not from first-party docs**; evidence-augmentation §5 asks for a docs.rs re-fetch before the multi-process store design is finalised [documented, **UNVERIFIED**] | **R-SQLITE** (§10.5) — a docs.rs/rusqlite read plus a two-process contention test against the pinned version | `rk-adapter-sqlite`; the store design is §5's | One writer connection ships either way (ADR-0084) — a re-fetch can only narrow the rule, not licence a pool |
| **Whether a dyn-compatible native `async fn` in trait now exists on stable** | Search-only. `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` was seen in nightly symbol tables ~1.93–1.96 with no stabilisation announcement found, and the pass explicitly did not read the Rust 1.97 release notes (evidence-augmentation §5 row 1) [documented] | a direct read of the release notes for the version in `rust-toolchain.toml`, plus the tracking issue | **the review of any toolchain-pin bump** — ADR-0076 names it | ADR-0076 is correct under either answer, so this gates nothing; the safe working assumption stays "still requires `async-trait` for dyn ports", and the kernel needs neither |

Every other unknown this section touches is registered in §10 (S-BIN for `ChangeSummary`, S-GIX for the git write path, S-TTL1 for the cache TTL, S-CODEX for the second harness). None is discharged here in prose.

### 9.5 Schema-generation discipline

The closed-output rules — no `oneOf`, `additionalProperties: false`, required-first, bounded optional and union fields — are constraints on the **Rust types**. They are **not** something `schemars` enforces: data-carrying enum variants derive to `oneOf` by default, matching serde's externally tagged representation, and `#[serde(tag = "kind")]` is what renders a single discriminated object instead [documented, 1P docs.rs/schemars, fetched 2026-09-08]. A rule that lives only in prose is not a rule, and this is a boundary where violations are known to fail **open**: F20 measured an agent card's `tools` allowlist silently disabling `--json-schema` — `structured_output` absent, **exit 0**, `subtype: success` [measured, F20].

So ADR-0084 requires a **golden schema snapshot** (`insta`) for every type crossing the closed-output boundary, asserting on the generated JSON that it contains no `oneOf` anywhere, sets `additionalProperties: false` at every object level, orders `required` first, and stays within the project's optional-field and union-field budgets. A changed snapshot is a schema change and is reviewed as one.

Two honesty constraints on that test, plus the residual ADR-0084 names about itself. It asserts **this project's own budget**: the "≤24 optional / ≤16 union" figures trace to an SDK issue's illustrative arithmetic ("4 tools × 6 optional params = 24"), and **no limits table was found** on the platform structured-outputs page, which truncated on fetch — so the caps are **issue-reported and illustrative, never a platform constant** (evidence-augmentation §5) [documented, thin]. And F31's schema conformance of **52/52 = 100%** measures that *emitted payloads conformed to a schema* [measured, F31]; it says nothing about whether the *generated schema* satisfies the closed-output constraints — a distinct property **nothing in the corpus has ever checked**, which is exactly why the snapshot exists. And the residual is recorded in ADR-0084 rather than mitigated: a reviewer can rubber-stamp `cargo insta accept`, and the guard against that is convention, not mechanism.

### 9.6 What transfers from `spikes/runner`

The runner is the only Rust this programme has actually run. Nine patterns in it are load-bearing and carry forward by construction — each because a specific failure is otherwise representable, not because it is tidy.

| Pattern | The failure it makes unrepresentable | Where it lands | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Result<_, StepError>` with a **`spoke()`** predicate — only `Schema` means the agent produced a judgement; everything else is silence | RK-B0013: one HTTP 529 produced a **committed `changes-requested` seal** whose entire surviving finding was "review failed to produce a digest", because `!review` conflated "the reviewer said no" with "the reviewer never spoke" | `rk-types` (the enum), `rk-kernel` (the gate evaluator) | [documented, RK-B0013]; **AC-229** pins one closed variant at the port boundary with no catch-all, **AC-230** pins silence as a block with no synthesised findings, **AC-231** pins the empty-findings case as distinguishable from it; **SM-063** instruments the variant mix |
| **Prompt on stdin, never argv** | A Windows command line caps near 32 KB; the `redkiln-review` card alone is 6.6 KB and the fix-lane prompt bodies are larger. Argv works in testing and fails on a real review | `rk-adapter-claude` | [measured, direct inspection of `runner.rs`; the TS tree already carried an `--obs-file` workaround for the same cap] |
| **A record is produced even when the step fails** | A dispatch that dies without a usage record — the exact thing inversion exists to end, since cost only becomes visible if failures are priced too | `rk-adapter-claude` → the event log (§7) | [measured, `StepRecord` is written on both arms of the result]; the batch-delta cost model is §7's |
| **Raw payload retained beside the typed view** | A malformed digest and a refusal reading identically. `StepError::Schema` carries `raw`; the final assistant text is kept even when a digest parsed, and an unrecognised stream event is preserved verbatim | `rk-types`, `rk-store` | [measured, `runner.rs` / `step.rs`]; the harness area pins verbatim preservation of unknown events, and **AC-230**'s "no synthesised findings" depends on the raw text surviving |
| **Fully-resolved specs — no ambient discovery** | A design leaning on `.claude/` discovery, on a surface documented to change (`--bare` becoming the `-p` default), and on the operator's laptop leaking into the run: F8's default `-p` inherited 12 MCP servers and ~280 tools, MCP alone **4,631 tokens = 56%** of the cache write | `rk-kernel` (resolved activity inputs), `rk-adapter-claude` (`StepSpec`) | [measured, F8; direct inspection of `step.rs`]; ADR-0016 |
| **Structural resume keys** | A retry replaying a stale verdict — the dispatch identity separates attempts — and a resume that reconstructs only what the author predicted it would need | `rk-kernel` (memo key, resume) | [measured, F19 174/174; F38 3/3 across a real `taskkill /T /F`, digests identical]. **Both limits carried:** the FSM was **never replayed**, so "same next action" is unproven and ships as a kernel replay test (§3), and P8's state holds **exactly one step** with its 65-minute and 24-hour arms and all three controls **unrun** (§0.1) |
| **Provenance at dispatch time, including a digest of the child's EFFECTIVE config** | Asking after the fact what the child was actually configured with. An ignored flag looks identical to an honoured one, so isolation is verified from the child's own `system/init`, and the git snapshot (`head`, `branch`, dirty count, porcelain digest) is captured at the instant of dispatch because **it cannot be retrofitted** | `rk-adapter-claude`, the event log (§7) | [measured, F8; `fingerprint.rs` — "a run recorded without it is not a weaker measurement, it is an unusable one"] |
| **Write-path guards enforced by construction** | Telemetry written inside the tree under test, which perturbs the very thing being measured — the journal refuses to open at any path inside a forbidden root, and hygiene assertions void the run **before** dispatch (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` absent, `claude` version floor) | `rk-store` (the single write path, §5), `rk-adapter-claude` | [measured, `journal.rs` / `hygiene.rs`]; ADR-0010 and **AC-488** make the API-key check a construction failure, not a warning |
| **`Option` over `0`** where a missing measurement would read as a measured zero | An absent field laundered into a measured zero. F3 measured the `ephemeral_1h_*`/`ephemeral_5m_*` fields **absent** from `usage`; a `Some(0)` there would have made a TTL claim look verified | `rk-types` (usage and cost types), `rk-adapter-claude` | [measured, F3]; **AC-362** discharges it with an `Option` carrying **no `Default` impl**, plus a CI grep rule forbidding `unwrap_or(0)` / `unwrap_or_default()` on those fields — and §7's SM-112 records the same laundering caught in the measure set, where an unreported overage count had been written up as a measured zero |

### 9.7 Decisions

| Decision | Alternatives rejected | Why | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tokio` in adapter crates only; the kernel names no runtime | `async-trait` on every dyn port; generic (statically dispatched) ports; wait for `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` | async-trait puts an executor inside a core whose defining property is deterministic replay, costs a boxed future on the one boundary everything crosses, and does not deliver the cancellation it appears to — killing a `claude -p` tree needs an explicit call either way. Generics are the leak ADR-0003 exists to prevent. Nightly makes the toolchain pin a hostage | ADR-0076; [documented] native async-fn-in-trait is not dyn-compatible and `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` is nightly-only, 1P async-fundamentals-initiative, fetched 2026-09-08; [measured] F22 |
| Exact `gix = "=0.87.1"`, read path only, `cargo audit` in CI watching `gix-*` | caret range and upgrade on cadence; `git2-rs`/libgit2 for everything; audit at release time only | pre-1.0 + a facade over dozens of independently versioned sub-crates + load-bearing worktree/trailer semantics is exactly what the house 0.x rule is for; the advisories that matter arrive with a **transitive** `gix-*` bump, so a release-time check finds them after they have been merged; libgit2 would take on C-toolchain risk *and* the worktree gap at once | ADR-0077, ADR-0084; [documented] RustSec / GitHub Advisory Database + gitoxide `crate-status.md`, fetched 2026-09-08 |
| `clippy::all` denied + an enumerated pedantic subset + nursery at warn | deny `pedantic`/`nursery` as groups; `#![deny]` per crate; no lint policy (the status quo) | group denies break CI on a clippy bump for reasons unrelated to the code and train contributors to sprinkle `#[allow]`; a per-crate deny puts the policy in a dozen files where divergence is invisible | ADR-0083; [documented] 1P Clippy Lints book; Cargo Book "Lints" |
| Configuration as a value; `set_var` banned workspace-wide | environment variables as the CLI-native configuration shape (how the spike rig works today) | `set_var` is `unsafe` in edition 2024 because it races `getenv`, and the default test harness is multi-threaded — any test that sets a variable is a latent flake against any test that reads one; it also hides the kernel's inputs from the event log | ADR-0083; [documented] 1P Rust Edition Guide + rust-lang/rust PR #124636 |
| Consolidated integration binaries | one file per case (the Rust-by-Example default) | one file is one binary relinking the whole stack; consolidating Cargo's own suite cut build time 3× and artifact size 5×. Organisation is preserved by modules at no cost | ADR-0083; [measured, external] matklad, on Cargo's suite — direction and mechanism only |
| Golden schema snapshot over authorship discipline | trust the "no `oneOf`" rule to careful authoring | the first `enum` with a payload violates it silently, and F20 is the measured shape of a closed-output defect failing **open** at exit 0 with `subtype: success` | ADR-0084; [measured] F20; [documented] 1P docs.rs/schemars |

## 10. Phasing, open questions and the spike register

Every unknown named anywhere in §§1–9 appears below as a row with a named spike and a named consumer. That is the discharge mechanism ADR-0012 requires, and it is the only permitted one: a prose hedge does not discharge an unknown in this document. The blocked rows of §0.1 are not re-argued here — they are carried into the phase table as prohibitions and into the register as gating rows.

### 10.1 How to read a phase row

An **entry condition** is a state of the world that can be checked before the phase starts. An **exit criterion** is a command with a pass condition, not a narrative milestone — the proposal's Phase-1 exit read "runs its own fix lane end to end (self-hosting)", and AC-473 is what makes that mechanical: at least one recorded fix-lane run whose event log contains every lane stage from intake to a landed commit, with no stage performed by the old redkiln, and the claim refused if any stage is missing from the log [documented; ADR-0002, ADR-0011]. Where a phase's exit criterion depends on a blocked row, the criterion names the spike and the phase ships the hedge, not the answer.

### 10.2 The phase table

| Phase | Enter when | Scope | Exit criteria, as runnable checks | May not | Evidence / decisions |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **0 — close the programme, run the gating spikes** | Now | Commit the working tree; extract `redkiln-corpus` at a pinned SHA; run every register row marked **gates Phase 1** (S-BIN and **S-FACET**; the P1 arm-D panel is done and P2 still refuses); run the cheap unblockers (S-GIX, S-TTL1); re-budget and re-run P8's unrun arms | (a) `git status --porcelain` is empty in `redkiln-rs` — the brief records the tree **not clean as of 2026-09-09**, HEAD `ab58733`, with nothing from the AFK session committed [measured, brief §E]. (b) Corpus reads resolve to the pinned SHA via the object store and never to a working tree (**AC-464**), which itself records the corpus checkout as *dirty at pin time* [documented; ADR-0013]. (c) Every register row in §10.5 marked "gates Phase 1" has a written finding with an F-number in `spikes/FINDINGS.md`, or the row is explicitly re-scoped by an ADR. (d) The blind panels are filled — **both are now DONE**: P1 arm D was adjudicated and scored on 2026-09-09 against 140 truth cells at pin `47faec9f`, $0 model cost, reported **panel-adjudicated** and never as human-panel [measured, F39], and `e3-severity-annotation-ryan.md` came back filled at 4 BLOCKING / 2 ADVISORY. **(d) therefore gates nothing further.** What F39 opened is carried by (c) — where **S-FACET** is a new register row marked "gates Phase 1" — and by **AC-071's clause 1**, identity ≥ 0.80, which the arm-D scoring did not touch and which F36 measured at **0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10** | Be skipped for the three gating rows. A Phase-1 commit that touches `ChangeSummary`, an `llm`-sourced facet, or a `derived`-sourced facet with no scored `deriver`, before (c) is out of order | ADR-0012, ADR-0008, ADR-0013, ADR-0079, **ADR-0089**; F36, F38, **F39** |
| **1 — kernel, store, fix and story lanes on a fresh repo** | Phase 0 exit (a)–(d) | `rk-types` / `rk-kernel` / `rk-store` / `rk-git` / `rk-ports`; SQLite, Claude and Node adapters each with a fake; `bug-standard` and `story` process packs only (ADR-0011); mark-based human gate with CLI as the only answer channel (ADR-0009); typed artifacts under layout C (ADR-0005, ADR-0006); the hybrid git write path (ADR-0077); `redkiln-migrate` v0 | (1) **AC-473** self-hosting check passes on a recorded fix-lane run. (2) `redkiln doctor --rebuild` reproduces the same next action from git alone (**AC-184**, **SM-124**) — this is also the **kernel replay test**, and it is the first time the FSM is replayed at all: F19 proved suspension lossless **174/174** but explicitly left "same next action" unproven [measured, F19]. (3) Deterministic resume across a real `taskkill /T /F` at **more than one step** — F38 held exactly one step at n=3 [measured, F38]. (4) The determinism ban list fails the build: `cargo clippy -D warnings` plus a `trybuild` must-fail fixture that reintroduces a clock read (**AC-001**–**AC-004**). (5) The `--stay`-class error is unrepresentable — a `trybuild` must-fail fixture, not a runtime assertion [documented, proposal §7]. (6) `redkiln lint --facet-stability` (**AC-071**) passes on **both** source classes, not one. For `source = "llm"`: **zero** such facets, or a scored P1 stability record meeting identity ≥ 0.80, n ≥ 20 items, **≥ 10 draws per item**, arm-D panel scored (**discharged 2026-09-09** — F39, 140 cells at pin `47faec9f`, $0, panel-adjudicated), corpus pinned. For **`source = "derived"` — the clause this exit criterion did not have, and the gap F39 found** (ADR-0089), now carried by its own criterion **AC-520**, `redkiln lint --facet-provenance`: **zero** facets declaring `source = "derived"` without a named **`deriver`** and a scored precision record for that deriver; no facet whose only computation is a lexical scan of free text keying a `require` row; and every `require` row over a boolean facet handling **`null`** explicitly rather than resolving an absence of evidence to `false`. (7) Per-activity cost is recorded and carries `resolution_limited` wherever the 0.01 quantisation binds (**SM-109**) — F6 measured `utilization` quantised to 0.01, so per-run cost is not measurable and batch-delta is the only honest report [measured, F6] | **Author a `require` row keyed on an LLM-emitted `criticality`, `novelty`, `size`, `blast_radius`, `market_facing`, `external_dep` or `has_repro`** — blocked, ADR-0079, enforced by AC-071 (§4). **Author a `require` row or `applicable_if` keyed on `auth`, `ui`, `flag_exists` or `data_migration` as computed by the lexical deriver, or let the replacement `surfaces` facet key a `require` row before S-FACET scores it** — blocked, ADR-0089 (§4). **Finalise the `ChangeSummary` type** — blocked on S-BIN (**AC-154**, **AC-356**). **Enable the card append route as anything but an unmeasured config option** — blocked on S-H1c (**AC-211**). **Set a quality-shorthand default**, or use `findings.none(evidence in [Reproduced, MutationSurvived])` as a terminal condition — ADR-0080. **Report any run as 1 h TTL without per-run `ephemeral_*` field evidence** — ADR-0078. **Let gix own the write path** — ADR-0077. **Describe this design, or any commit in it, as final** | ADR-0005, 0006, 0009, 0011, 0016, 0074, 0076, 0077, 0078, 0079, 0080, 0082, 0084, 0086, **0089**; F6, F19, F20, F22, F36, F38, **F39** |
| **2 — packs and parallelism** | Phase 1 exit (1) recorded, i.e. self-hosting is a log fact, not a claim | Remaining process packs (incident, spike, initiative, project) with weights and the fast path; project DAG in parallel worktrees plus a merge queue; .NET, Python and **Unreal** toolchain adapters (ADR-0008); `rk-plugin-gen`; migration of the old corpus | (1) **AC-463**, which defines "migration differential green" mechanically and admits no other reading: section accounting balances (**AC-451**), zero unrecorded manual interventions (**AC-461**), and every sub-80% slot carries an Accepted ADR id (**AC-462**). (2) An initiative advanced end to end headlessly. (3) A UE item verified through the toolchain adapter — **conditional on S-BIN**; if S-BIN has not run this criterion is not evaluable and the phase does not exit on it. (4) Per-worktree cache cost measured, not assumed: **SM-053**, against the structural fact that two worktrees of one repo never share a cache [documented, 1P prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08] | Ship the plugin surface as evidenced — every distribution finding is borrowed from *shipmates'* issue history, one project, one maintainer, one release series, and redkiln-rs has **no distribution baseline of its own** (§0.1, §8) | ADR-0008, 0012, 0013; S-MIG; AC-451, AC-461, AC-462, AC-463 |
| **3 — surfaces** | Phase 2 stable on one initiative | GitHub adapter and comment grammar; mdBook adapter and docs pipeline; web view; DORA metrics from the event log | (1) A gate answered from a phone produces the identical `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` mark and re-validates staleness **at the read site**, because no surveyed platform invalidates a stale-but-unexpired token automatically [documented, 1P Step Functions / Restate / Trigger.dev / Inngest / DBOS / GitHub, fetched 2026-09-08; ADR-0017]. (2) A collaborator with no CLI sees status. (3) Every projection is provably derived: `doctor --rebuild` drops and rebuilds all three from git plus the log (**AC-184**) | Let a projection become authoritative (§8) | ADR-0017; R-LINEAR, R-DOCS feed the port shapes |
| **4 — reach** | Phase 3 stable for a quarter | Codex adapter — **only after S-CODEX passes**; Linear and Astro adapters as demanded | None registered. **AC-239** is PROVISIONAL for exactly this reason: S-CODEX is not reported in the brief | Put a non-Claude adapter in a gate before it passes E3-shaped checks | ADR-0007; AC-239 |

**The Phase-1 gate on P1, stated once and mechanically.** Phase 1 is gated behind AC-071 for anything that touches selection over LLM-emitted facets, and the gate is not a review convention — it is **AC-071**, which fails pack load and CI when any facet is declared `source = "llm"` without a scored stability record. The warrant is F36: the modal `R(stage, ctx)` was identical on only **5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20** items against an 80% line, the prescribed anchor-rewrite repair did not work, `external_dep` collapsed to a constant (**200/200** false), and three axes with **byte-identical** anchors drifted up to **−0.168** — larger than the effect the alpha<0.6 rule exists to detect, so that rule **is not a decidable predicate on this corpus** [measured, F36; 400 dispatches, 0 void, $21.37]. **The arm-D panel clause is now discharged and the gate is not:** arm D was scored on 2026-09-09 over 140 truth cells at pin `47faec9f` at $0 model cost, panel-adjudicated, and no derived facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar — `auth` **0.00** [0.00, 0.26] over eleven positives, `flag_exists` **0.00** [0.00, 0.66], `binary_change` 0.60 [0.23, 0.88], `change` 0.72 [0.54, 0.85], `toolchain` 0.73 [0.48, 0.89], `ui` 1.00 [0.65, 1.00] whose interval does not clear, and `data_migration` **not measurable** — while the ≥ 0.95 clause is itself unreachable at n=20 for any facet [measured, F39]. That satisfies **AC-071's fourth clause and no other**; the gate now fails on the first, identity ≥ 0.80 against a measured 0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10. Until a record clears it, every ordinal axis is `source = "human"`, supplied or confirmed at the triage gate, and the operator carries the load that **SM-024** measures. `kind` is out of scope of the block (human input, ADR-0030 rule 4). **And the same exit criterion now carries a second, distinct gate**: no facet may declare `source = "derived"` without a named `deriver` and a scored precision record for it, `surfaces` may not key a `require` row until S-FACET reports, and a derived boolean that cannot be computed is `null` rather than `false` (**ADR-0089**, accepted). Nothing in this section amends ADR-0079, which remains `proposed` and forbids being amended into a decision; F39 measures a derived vector, not an LLM-emitted one, and narrows 0079's options without deciding among them.

### 10.3 What Phase 1 ships instead of an answer

| Blocked row | The hedge Phase 1 ships | Where it is specified |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Context-vector source, LLM-emitted (ADR-0079) | Human-sourced ordinal facets; the journal records whatever vector was emitted and replay reads the journal, never a re-classification | §4; AC-009, AC-071 |
| Context-vector source, **derived** (ADR-0089) | `facets.toml`'s `source` gains a **`deriver`** field and no facet may declare `source = "derived"` without a scored record for it; the repo-wide `auth` boolean is withdrawn for a set-valued **`surfaces`** facet over a checked-in `surfaces.toml`, which may be authored and linted and may **not** key a `require` row until **S-FACET** scores it; boolean facets are tri-valued, and an absence of evidence is **`null`** routed to AC-079's `gate_opened { reason: unknown_facet }` rather than a silent `false` | §4; **AC-520** (the gate), AC-072, AC-079, AC-425; **SM-157** |
| `ChangeSummary` (S-BIN) | Historical change material is carried as opaque content with a migration-loss marker; **no `ChangeSummary` value is synthesised** (**AC-475**) | §5, §8 |
| Card delivery (S-H1c) | Static `--system-prompt` is the default and the append flags are absent from argv (**AC-211**) | §3, §7 |
| Quality shorthand (ADR-0080) | Each quality block is authored explicitly per activity; no default | §4, §6 |
| gix write path (ADR-0077) | Hybrid: gix for `rev-parse`, status, shallow clone by SHA and trailer *parsing*; `git interpret-trailers --in-place` / `git commit --trailer` and `git worktree add/remove` shell out | §5 |
| TTL verification (ADR-0078) | Both `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` and `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` set; no run reported as 1 h without per-run `ephemeral_*` evidence | §7 |
| "Design final" (ADR-0087) | The label is withheld; the build proceeds on the DECIDED rows of §0.1 | Header, §0.1 |

| **S-CODEX — harness portability** | The harness port has exactly **one** real adapter; portability is asserted, not demonstrated. **AC-239** is PROVISIONAL because S-CODEX is not reported in the brief | No non-Claude adapter enters a gate before it passes E3-shaped checks (ADR-0007); Phase 4 is the earliest it ships | **S-CODEX** | Harness port viability, capability registry contents; **AC-239**, **SM-154** | No |
| **R-LINEAR, R-DOCS — Phase-3 port shapes** | The Tracker and DocsSite ports are designed against one tracker and one docs generator; which invariants are generator-agnostic is unread | Both ports ship with a fake and one real adapter; Phase 3 is the earliest either is exercised | **R-LINEAR**, **R-DOCS** | Tracker port shape — **AC-507**, **AC-508** (derived-never-authoritative, idempotent writes); DocsSite port shape — **AC-510** (the three generator-agnostic invariants, each with a negative case against the fake). **SM-145** is the plugin re-sync tax and belongs to neither port; it is dropped from this row | No |

Three register rows carry a discipline note rather than a cost. **S-BIN, P1-D and S-FACET are the only three rows that stop Phase-1 work**, and they stop *specific* work — the `ChangeSummary` type, `require` rows over LLM-emitted facets, and `require` rows over the derived semantic facets — not the phase. Everything else in the table is either a hedge already shipping or a label withheld.

**Three residual unknowns resolve to a recorded decision rather than to a spike, and are named here so that none escapes the register.** The companion **kind list** — proposal §4.4, its machine-only list and the S-MIG census name different sets — is a runbook decision recorded against ADR-0075 clause 3, and **AC-143** cannot be verified until it is taken (§5.11). **AC-121's cross-OS determinism half and AC-158's property ordering** are unverifiable as specified until the CI shape fixes two OS targets and an order-preserving schema export; both are marked unverified rather than assumed, and §9.3 owns the CI shape. And the retry envelope constants — **`K`, `N`, the backoff base and cap, the two-tick headroom margin and the per-item token appetite** — have no measured value anywhere in the corpus; they are declared in the pack and refused as code defaults, and the criteria verify conformance to the declared envelope rather than that the envelope is right (§3.6, §3.10). Consumer: pack authoring. None of the three is a measurement this programme can take, which is why each is a decision with a named home rather than a spike with a cost.

**And a fourth class, enumerated so that no section's "every row resolves to §10" is false.** Nine open items named in §§4.9, 5.11, 6.6 and 7.10 resolve to **a first measurement from the running product**, not to a spike — which is why none carries a cost, and why they appear as one row in §10.4 rather than nine in the priced register: whole-suite p95 per toolchain (**AC-330** / **SM-102**), per-AC pinning coverage (**SM-100**), advisory-sink readership (**SM-089**), the structured-drift rate (**SM-037**), `reconcile()`'s own cost (**SM-049**), lattice scaling at 2× catalog size (**SM-025**), delivered-artifact quality-block coverage (**AC-112** / **SM-032**), per-activity usage over a rolling 90 days (**SM-036**), and `doctor`'s unread-artifact window `N` (**AC-426** / **AC-472** / **SM-139**, whose target cannot be missed until `N` is declared). Each ships the hedge its own section states; none is a spike, and none is load-bearing for a decision taken here. **AC-114's ship/extend decision id** is a tenth of the same kind, except that its discharge is a criterion to be authored in `ac-packs-catalog.md` rather than a measurement.

### 10.6 What would falsify this design

Each is a specific result from a named check, and each names what would have to be rewritten. None is hypothetical hand-wringing: every one is a spike already in §10.5.

| The result | Named check | What it forces |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`doctor --rebuild` does not reproduce the same next action from git alone.** F19 proved suspension lossless 174/174 but **explicitly left "same next action" unproven — the FSM was never replayed** [measured, F19], and P8's state holds exactly one step [measured, F38]. This is the largest untested assumption in the document | Phase-1 exit (2), **AC-184** / **SM-124** | The tier-1/tier-2 split test ("if the database were deleted, would `redkiln run` take a different next action?") does not hold, and the store layout, the memo key and the replay contract (ADR-0021, ADR-0022) are rewritten. **§3 and §5 both fall** |
| **Arm D scores poorly, and a human-sourced facet set proves too costly to author.** **The first half has now happened**: arm D was scored on 2026-09-09 and **no derived facet clears 0.95** — `auth` **0.00** [0.00, 0.26] over eleven positives, `flag_exists` 0.00, `data_migration` not measurable — while the bar itself is unreachable at n=20 [measured, F39]. That is a **partial realisation of this falsifier, not the whole of it**: the second half is unmeasured, because SM-024 has no baseline and the four abstaining items on twenty are a property of an oracle-construction exercise rather than a forecast for a shipped triage gate | **P1-D** (panel done; the identity re-run outstanding), **S-FACET** for the derived replacement, then **SM-024** human-supplied facet load | If S-FACET's declared boundary map also fails to clear at an absorbable abstention rate, the `require`-row mechanism keyed on a per-item context vector has **no viable computed source at all** and those facets route to ASKED — a legitimate pre-registered result, with the gate load carried openly by SM-024. If the gate load then proves unaffordable, §4's selection function must be replaced by something that does not need a per-item vector, and ADR-0023 and ADR-0030 are reopened. What F39 has already forced is narrower and is recorded rather than deferred: ADR-0089 |
| **S-BIN: the reviewer cannot verify ACs or catch a seeded regression on a no-diff item** | **S-BIN** | `ChangeSummary` is not a type problem but an evidence problem. The review gate's applicability to binary-asset toolchains is withdrawn, ADR-0008's Phase-2 Unreal adapter loses its premise, and the five toolchain-keyed kinds (ADR-0073) are re-cut |
| **S-GIX: the common-dir/work-tree scenario cannot be made to work reliably even by shelling out** | **S-GIX** | One store per repository at `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`/redkiln/ shared by every worktree is not implementable as specified, and §5's store location and the parallel-worktree model in §7 are both rewritten |
| **P8-B: mid-graph resume outside the TTL costs more than a stage gate, against a real within-experiment baseline** | **P8-B**, **SM-073** | ADR-0087's provisional placement is withdrawn and gates return to stage boundaries. Note the current n: 3 runs, **0.45× / 0.55× / 0.78×** of ~18.6k against a >1.5× kill, all three **inside** F10's ~1 h TTL, with no within-experiment baseline [measured, F38] |
| **S-SUBTLE-CB: detection collapses when the change arrives framed as already approved** | **S-SUBTLE-CB**, **SM-093** | The review gate cannot be run on a change carrying a prior verdict — which is exactly the resume-after-gate path. §6's gate composition, and the order in which the two gates run, are rewritten. The external prior is a **16–93%** detection drop [preprint, arXiv 2603.18740] |
| **S-TTL1: the `ephemeral_*` fields do not exist at the pinned CLI version** | **S-TTL1** | The TTL is unverifiable at this pin. The split cache rule survives (it rests on F16 and S-WT, not on the TTL), but every cache-cost figure in §7 becomes a point estimate at an unknown TTL and the gate-cost model loses its time axis |
| **The catalog lint cannot be made monotone in internal quality** | **P7-CEIL**, **SM-035** | The ordinal rigor enum does not do the job asked of it, and the closed-enum risk the Dagster RFC documents has materialised: a small enum that could not compose. ADR-0081 is reopened and rigor becomes composable primitives |

One falsifier is deliberately absent, and its absence is the point. **No result from any spike above can make this design "final" on its own**: P8 must return a result, S-BIN and S-H1c must run, and until then no document, section or commit message may use the word. That prohibition is not a stylistic preference — it is the direct consequence of F38 having no result on its own question, with its third criterion **circular by construction**.

---

### 10.x Review of this document

This document was put through **two adversarial critic passes on 2026-09-08**, run against the same evidence brief, the same thirteen area files and the same `spikes/` records it is built from. One pass looked only for **what is missing** — material that exists in the corpus and silently failed to arrive here. The other looked only for **what overclaims** — numbers that drifted, saturated metrics reported as wins, powerless criteria reported as verdicts, bad denominators, and corrected claims that crept back under a new word. Both passes are recorded here because a document that holds every spike to a written limitation owes the same account of itself.

**What the overclaim pass found, and what was corrected.** Everything it raised is fixed; a wrong number has no upside.

| Class | The defect | The correction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Drifted figure | AC-071's unblocking bar was written **≥ 5 draws per item** in §4.1, §4.9 and §10.2 exit (6). AC-071's own executable precondition reads `rec.draws_per_item >= 10`. **Load-bearing:** arm L already ran 20 × 10 and the kill fired 4/4, so the design's bar could have been cleared by a re-run at half the draws that produced the kill | Corrected to **≥ 10** at all three sites, with the release condition now stated once, in AC-071's five clauses, in §0.1 |
| Wrong predicate | §5.2 clause 1 and three other sites stated the 19-of-153 figure under **two different predicates** — "neither GWT nor a verification note" and "no GWT **or** no verification note" | Harmonised to AC-137's own disjunctive wording, with the per-AC sub-counts (45 / 46) carried, at all four sites |
| Conflated denominator | §5.2 clause 6 read the three reclassified kinds "against spec sections at 98% overall (which averages *over* these three)". Spec sections is its own S-MIG slot and contains none of them | Restated against **the census overall**, as §8.7 already had it |
| Number outside its source | §8.4's "13 real / 34 false [measured, F32, **10 activities**]" — the corpus size is in `spikes/F32-DRAFT.md`, not in the brief's F32 row | Cited from the draft directly, and labelled as such |
| Laundered tag | §4.5's `~95`-activity ceiling shared a `[measured, F35/F34]` tag with three figures that are measured | Split: **~95 is ADR-0027's own approximate split of 267 raw rows, [documented], a planning estimate** |
| A price twice denied | §7.8 priced the `mandatory` arm at **$9.86** while §1.2 and §10.4 both state that no mandatory-tier price is quoted | **The price is struck.** The row now records why: dropped at n=1, and DECISIONS #7/#33 record it as pricing an unreachable configuration |
| Saturation laundering | F26's 20/20 was used as confirmation in the table headed “Settled”; §1.2 called the same result a prediction “**falsified twice**”; F31's 52/52 and 0/23 were quoted six times with no ceiling/floor label; §0.1 and §1.1 read a tie at ceiling as “not worse” and “nothing measurable was lost” | Every use now carries its label. **Four cells at ceiling confirm nothing**; the harder-tier prediction **could not be tested**, it was not falsified (principle 1 forbids exactly that move); and the non-inferiority language is replaced by “**no difference was detectable, and the instrument could not have detected one**” |
| Powerless criterion as verdict | “Changed the root cause on **1 item in 10**” appeared under §4.7's heading “What *is* citable”, and twice more. It is one cell (`issue-81`, 0/3 vs 1/3) moved by one draw of three — the powerless criterion's output. §6.3.6 said P8 “**falsified**” the mid-graph pricing assumption on three underpowered draws with no baseline | The 2.64× and the 33/33 stay citable — a cost and a constant predicate. **The 1-in-10 is removed from the citable set** and named as the criterion's output. §6.3.6 now reads “**untested, not falsified**”, and states that nothing there establishes mid-graph placement is safe |
| Bad denominator | §6.4.2's flat rule “CLEAN is never used as a denominator anywhere” was contradicted inside its own paragraph and in §6.4.4's telemetry table; the upper-bound label §1.2 requires “at the point of use” was absent at six sites; SM-086's baseline was stated as **0/23** in the sentence defining the denominator as adjudicated blocks only — and F31 adjudicated **14/14** | The rule is restated as it is actually kept: **CLEAN is never a denominator for an accuracy or false-positive-rate claim**, and the fractions are block counts. Upper-bound labels added at all six sites. SM-086 now records that **no compliant baseline exists** — the adjudicated reading is 0 of 14 |
| Blocked, quietly decided | §6.5.4 relaxed the per-AC mutation norm for the whole `binary` AC class, on the one class the document says has no evidence of any kind; §7.1 said `cache_profile` has two legal values and shipped a third in the next sentence; §7.8 set and priced a lane default three lines from §7.10's row calling it BLOCKED | The binary-class evidence standard is now **BLOCKED on S-BIN, not provisional-but-specified**. `cache_profile` has two values and **no shippable third**. §7.8 names **ADR-0015** as the tier authority and states explicitly that it is **not** the ADR-0080 shorthand |
| Single draw as a rate | F28's flat gate cost was stated as duration-invariance at six sites and carried its n at one; §0.1 and principle 11 quoted **29,619–29,680** as a range — two single draws from two different spikes, one of which has no finding number; §8.7 declared a ≥95% line falsified while declining to compute any interval | “One draw per duration point” now travels with every F28 use. The two static-prompt draws are quoted **separately, with their spikes and n=1**. The Wilson interval on 134/153 is computed and stated — **81.4–91.9%, which excludes 95%** — because falsifying a line is an interval claim |
| Crept-back claim | “**Account-wide** subsidy” survived the withdrawal of “org-scoped” in three places, while §7.2 of this same document says there is **no account-wide shared head**; §1.1 cited the CONTESTED AC-387 cell as authority for a settled “~31%” worktree price; F21's superseded 0.077 was forbidden by a rule that quoted it | “Account-wide” becomes “**shared-prefix**” at all three sites. §1.1 no longer prices worktree cold-start; the S-WT prompt attribution is marked **CONTESTED** at all three earlier uses. The F21 prohibition now reads “may not be quoted **as a value**” |
| Label carried too late | Cloudflare's $0.98 was asserted flat in §7.8 and flagged unreconciled 730 lines later | The unreconciled label is carried **at the point of use** |

Two of the overclaim pass's own readings were checked and **not** adopted as written. It read the 19-of-153 predicate as needing the *conjunctive* form restored; AC-137's text is the disjunctive one, so the correction runs the other way and the three “neither…nor” sites were changed instead. And it flagged “[measured, F32, 10 activities]” as a fabricated corpus size; the figure is real and is in `F32-DRAFT.md`, so it is cited from the draft rather than dropped. Both are corrections either way — the defect was the source of the number, not the number.

**What the completeness pass found.** Eight items had existed in the corpus and disappeared from this document entirely. Every one is now registered with a hedge, a spike and a named consumer, per ADR-0012 — none is closed by prose:

| Silent disappearance | Where it now lives |
| --- | --- |
| **8 of 23 corpus items sit outside S0.1** — a third of the corpus unmeasured by the gate FPR, while F11 is quoted as the warrant for the verification gate in four sections | §1.2 register row; a scope clause on **every** F11 citation; **S0.1-SCOPE** in §10.4/§10.5 |
| **E0 per-item stability** — no reviewer has been run twice on one item, so every 23/23 is one sample of a stochastic process | §1.2 row; **E0-VAR** in §10.4/§10.5, with E-REST as its programme home |
| **Equivalent-seed triage** (METHOD-E3 §10) never applied — the seed set the non-inferiority result rests on is untriaged | §1.2 row; **E3-SEED**, $0, §10.5 |
| **The in-session side of the cache asymmetry** — the programme's own observation of subagents on a **1 h, not 5 m** cache, opposite in sign to F3 | §7.3's contradiction box, now two-sided; the charter-E1 row of §1.2 |
| **The instrument lineage break** — 2.1.259 → 2.1.263 → 2.1.265 mid-P7, catching exactly `recommended`'s r3 draw; **the arms are not instrument-matched** | §1.2 row; the P7 rows of §7.8; §0.1's quality-shorthand row; **the pin's value (2.1.263) is now stated** at AC-423 and §9.2 |
| **The P-series remainders** — four supplementary packs unaccepted, the v0 pack untagged, the `optional`-override mode not a real reading | §4.6's pack-count discipline; two §4.9 rows; **P6-PACKS** in §10.4/§10.5 |
| **`forge-fix.js`'s absent-verdict-reads-as-approval defect**, and whether the Workflow runtime shares it — one dispatch to settle | **D-FIXCONSUMER** in §10.4/§10.5; and the three completed Charter §14 deliverables that carry it (the Run 3 scorecard, the RFC amendment, findings-for-product) are now rows in §11 |
| **The Windows handle a killed `vitest` leaves that no `Stop-Process` releases** — counter-evidence to reading AC-224 as “nothing survives” | §3.8.2 beside AC-224 and SM-068; §7.7's parallelism table; **S-CANCEL-WIN** |

Four spikes had a consumer but no register row, and one open item had **no spike at all** in violation of ADR-0012. All are registered: **S-WT-REC** (the S-WT run record — §7.9 calls it the most consequential defect in the area and §7.10 falsely claimed it appeared in §10), **P1-BOUND** (the boundary-distance diagnostic §4.1 claimed was “carried in the spike register” and was not), **S-CASCADE** (cascade recall loss, which §6.6 recorded as “No spike registered”), and the `system/api_retry` payload capture, now folded into **S-RETRY** with AC-026/AC-232 named. Nine further section-level open items that promised a §10 row are enumerated in §10.5's closing paragraph as **first measurements from the running product** rather than spikes — which is why they carry no cost. R-LINEAR and R-DOCS now name criteria (AC-507/AC-508, AC-510) rather than components.

Five uncovered-criteria gaps mattered enough to fix in place. The **harness area** (AC-200–AC-239) had no design section at all and was discharged in one sentence — it now has §3.6.1, which names every subject and surfaces two reconciliations it forces (AC-218 against §8.1's AC-428; the `--bare`/API-key rule living in three areas, which is the same collision §8.8 records). `ac-git-reconcile.md` §1.7's **merge queue and worktree lifecycle** (AC-189–AC-198) was uncovered in full and now has a subsection under §7.7, where the policy had been stated with one criterion between four rules. §8.4 now states that **two areas own a doctor rule table** and that only the cli-doctor half is reconciled. §5.4 and §7.4 now cite the tier-2 half of `ac-events-cost.md`. And §4.1 now pins AC-069/AC-070/AC-073/AC-074/AC-079 — including the decision-memory replay mechanism the operator-load hedge actually rests on.

Four unactioned research items are now acted on rather than dropped: Kiro-style `fileMatch` conditional inclusion is **workspace-scoped and cannot model cross-initiative atoms** (§8.6, §3.5's memo key now hashes the *resolved* atom set); Atlassian's *Agents in Jira* ships a native approval step, so the differentiation claim is **narrowed to the tree-hash binding inside an AI-coding-specific tool** (§6.3.2); the **Dagster RFC's closed-policy risk** is now cited where the ordinal enum is decided, not only where it is falsified (§4.7, §4.8); and the withdrawn “four or more workers” threshold and “current production consensus” claims are **recorded as withdrawn** in §2.2 rather than having vanished. §6.3.2 now names the Azure Durable Functions row it could not read, §3.7 names LangGraph as unread rather than excluded, §1.4 states that the AC-###-names-its-instrument convention and the BLOCKED-AC phrasing are **original process design**, and §11's literature preamble states the positive gap: no comparable published system exists to check this framing against.

**What was deliberately left, and why.**

- **Twenty SCHEMA-series ADRs (ADR-0032, 0034, 0035, 0039, 0044, 0048–0052, 0054, 0055, 0060, 0065, 0066, 0068–0071) are still not reflected individually.** They fix the activity-graph schema's *grammar*, whose home is `references/2026-09-07-activity-graph-schema.md`. A design document that restated a predicate grammar it does not own would create a second source of truth for a spec that already has one, and the two would diverge on the first amendment. **What was wrong was that they were invisible, not that they were absent** — so §4.5 now names all twenty in five groups with their substance in one line each, and the range citation `ADR-0031–ADR-0073` is no longer the only place a reader can find them. ADR-0072 was the exception and **is** now reflected, as the shipped-activity version-and-changelog rule, because it is a catalog-lifecycle decision rather than grammar. ADR-0058 and ADR-0041 are now cited at the places their substance is used (§4.6/§4.8 and §4.5's typed-exits row respectively, the latter with the ADR-0064 narrowing named).
- **The P3 tail of uncited criteria — roughly forty ids across eight areas — is left uncited.** §3.6's rule stands: the area files own the criteria and this document cites rather than restates them. Citing every id would convert this document into an index of the AC set without adding a decision. The cases that were fixed were fixed because a *section stated a rule with no instrument* (the merge queue, the harness contract, step 6 of the selection function) or because *two areas own the same rule* (the doctor tables, the `--bare` assertion) — both of which are defects in this document. A criterion that simply has no section citing it is not.
- **§8.8's AC-511 id conflict is still unresolved**, and §3.6.1 now adds a third home for the same rule rather than settling it. Ids are fixed and are never renumbered (ADR-0085), the sources do not settle which criterion holds the id, and inventing an answer here would be worse than carrying the conflict. The ports-dist and harness area files must be re-read before either is changed.
- **The DECISIONS-2026-09-09 “reverse or confirm first” table is still open**, and #15's `human_authored` remap still changes what the advisory fraction measures. **D-RECON** carries it; nothing in §§1–9 rests on an unreversed entry.
- **No new measurement was taken.** Every correction above is a re-reading of records that already existed. Where a figure was wrong, it was wrong in this document and right in its source; where a figure is missing, it is still missing, and it is now in §10 rather than in nobody's hands.

**What the two passes together say about this document's method.** The overclaim pass found twelve classes of defect and **not one of them was a fabricated number** — every figure traced to a real record. What failed was the *labelling*: a ceiling read as a confirmation, a powerless criterion read as an effect, a wrong denominator carried past the sentence that forbade it, a corrected claim surviving under a synonym. That is the same failure mode this document's own principle 1 exists to catch, and it caught none of them unaided. The completeness pass found the mirror: material that was correct where it was written and simply never arrived, most of it from `spikes/HANDOFF.md` — the one source in §11 that is a **handover note rather than a finding**, and therefore the one with no F-number to make its absence conspicuous. Both failure modes are cheap to introduce and expensive to notice, which is the argument for running a pass like this again before any successor document is written, not after.

---

## 11. Sources

Built from what §§1–10 actually cite. Nothing is listed that no section used, and nothing a section used is omitted. Where a source is named in the document but could not be resolved to a fetchable record, the row says so rather than dropping it — an unverified provenance is a finding, not a tidying job (§10.4, **R-MUT**). Platform and library rows carry the fetch date because a version list has an expiry: **ADR-0084 forbids citing its own version list as a current-versions source after 2026-09-08**, and this section inherits that expiry.

### Literature and prior art

**A positive gap, stated before the table.** The research pass looked for a named research subfield covering **LLM-driven process or workflow tailoring** and found none [documented, research pass §5 unresolved]. That is worth recording as a result rather than an absence: **no comparable published system exists to check this framing against**, so the sources below anchor individual mechanisms — instability, judge bias, rank reversal, multi-agent scaling — and none of them anchors the shape of the thing as a whole. Where this document's own conventions are original rather than borrowed, §1.4 says so.

| Source | What it contributes | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **arXiv 2603.04417**, *Same Input, Different Scores* (Feb 2026) — GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, Gemini-2.5-Flash, Claude-Haiku-4.5, Claude-Sonnet-4.5 | "Despite expectations of stability at temperature=0, substantial variability persisted across models"; temperature=0 had limited or inconsistent effect for the Anthropic models | §0.1, §1.3, §2 (principle 3), §4.1 |
| **arXiv 2604.22411**, *Introducing Background Temperature* (Apr 2026) | **Claude-Sonnet-4 measured at T_bg = 0**, perfectly deterministic in their harness — which places P1's instability on the **schema/prompt** side, not the decoding side | §0.1, §1.3, §4.1 |
| **arXiv 2511.01176** (code-clone classification) | Instability **correlates with proximity to the decision boundary** — the one cheap diagnostic available against P1's existing draws, and the reason **a temperature spike is explicitly not the answer** | §4.1, §4.9 |
| **arXiv 2606.13685** (LLM-judge stability) | Coding items at **23.6%** instability in the high-flip stratum; a single trial matches a 50-trial majority only **86.6%** of the time — external consonance for removing the verdict channel | §1.1 |
| **arXiv 2603.18740** — controlled experiment over **250 CVE vulnerability–patch pairs** | Framing a change as already reviewed and approved reduces vulnerability detection by **16–93%**, **strongly asymmetric toward false negatives**. The mirror risk this programme has never measured; the warrant for ADR-0088's unrun arm | §1.2, §2 (principle 12 caution), §6.4.5, §10.4, §10.6 |
| **arXiv 2609.04167**, **SWE-Gate** — review constraints derived from real PR comments, 303 repair instances across 75 Python repos | **221 of 644 (34%)** functionally-*passing* agent repairs fail those constraints. A second, fresher anchor for "green ≠ pinned" — a **different quantity** from anything in this corpus, and never a rate this design inherits | §2 (principle 12), §6.2, §6.4.4 |
| **arXiv 2603.00539**, Haolin Jin & Huaming Chen — *Are LLMs Reliable Code Reviewers?* **One paper, not two** | Rationale-heavy prompting drives false rejection (GPT-4o FNR **26–36% → 58–88%**); the fix-guided verification filter (§7.2 / Table 4) recovers 54.8%→16.3% HumanEval, 69.0%→28.9% MBPP, 51.0%→24.0% QuixBugs. **The provenance correction matters**: counting it as two entries overstated the independent replication behind ADR-0074 | §6.4.5 |
| **arXiv 2512.08296** (Google Research/DeepMind — **not "Google/MIT"**; quoted figures are the original 180-config / 4-benchmark version, not the Apr 2026 v3's 260 configs / 6 benchmarks) | Sequential multi-agent decomposition **−39% to −70%**, parallel **+80.9%**, error amplification **17.2×** independent vs **4.4×** centralised | §2 (principle 9), §7.7 |
| **arXiv 2606.13003** | Automatic multi-agent systems "consistently underperform CoT-SC despite being up to **10×** more expensive" across six frameworks | §2 (principle 9) |
| **ScienceDirect S0895717711007850** [peer] | **Rank reversal occurs for virtually every weighted MCDM method**, TOPSIS and SAW included — the general result, so "just use TOPSIS" is pre-answered. Why rigor is an ordinal deontic enum and is never summed | §0.1, §2 (principle 14), §4.7, §4.8 |
| **Mavin et al., EARS**, IEEE RE'09 [peer] | EARS single-trigger phrasing as closer to machine-checkable than Gherkin — a **phrasing preference with no enforcement and no measurement on this corpus**; the AC *type* stays permissive because the census requires it | §5.2 (clause 8), §8.7 |
| **SHOP2** ordered-method discipline (HTN planning prior art, named in the catalog proposal) | Lowest `method_rank` wins and **a tie is a validation error**, never a runtime choice | §4.4, §4.8 |
| **Cloudflare**, *Orchestrating AI Code Review at scale* (blog.cloudflare.com/ai-code-review/) — 131,246 review runs across **48,095 merge requests** over 30 days | Overall average **$1.19**; **pooled** median **$0.98**; full tier **mean $1.68 / median $1.47**; "break glass" **288 of 48,095 = 0.6%** — the only public false-block comparator this pass found, used as an order of magnitude and never as a threshold. **[Editorial note: the evidence brief labels the $0.98 figure as the *read-only tier* median; §7.8's footnote, from the 2026-09-08 re-fetch, labels it a median *pooled across all tiers*. Both readings are carried; the discrepancy is in the label, not the figure, and it is unreconciled here.]** | §4.9, §6.3.4, §6.4.4, §7.8 |
| **Mergify**, *State of Merge Queues 2026* [vendor] — 200,000+ merges across 477 teams | AI-assisted PRs broke main **1.9%** vs **4.4%** for non-AI **when routed through a merge queue** — the reduction appears to come from the queue, not from the agent being safer | §7.7 |
| **augmentcode.com** [blog] | Worktree-per-task as the 2026 default for parallel coding agents, and that a worktree buys **file-level isolation only** | §7.7 |
| **Agentless vs CodeR** benchmark comparison; **Anthropic Code Review** at $15–25 per review | External cost orientation only: **$0.70/instance at 32.00%** against **$3.34 at 28.33%** — cheaper *and* better, a warning against pricing rigor by spend alone | §7.8 |
| **git-branchless issue #1334** | A `reference-transaction` hook cannot be made atomic and forces full-log replays — why **no git hooks feed the store**. ADR-0018's own note records that #1334 "is about hooks, not worktrees" | §5.7, §5.10 |
| **Backlog.md** (issue **#558**; and Backlog.md + Archon as the two standalone binaries that got adopted) | Store ownership checked rather than assumed; the durable-artifact-plus-skill/MCP-layer shape behind the plugin surface — **borrowed evidence, PROVISIONAL** | §2 (principle 19), §5.7, §5.10 |
| **Nx** (targets vs executors) · **Tekton** (Pipelines vs Tasks) · **Cloud Native Buildpacks** (ordered detect-and-contribute) · **GitHub reusable workflows vs composite actions** | Convergent prior art for the two-axis pack split: one layer owns structure, the other binds and contributes at named extension points | §4.2 |
| **Dagster RFC** on closed enums | The named risk if the rigor ladder cannot be made monotone: a small enum that could not compose | §10.6 |
| **Symphony** — named as the anti-pattern | Tracker-as-state-machine, rejected: projections are derived and never authoritative | §8.9 |
| **matklad.github.io** [blog, measured on Cargo's own suite by its author] · **swatinem.de** · **nickb.dev** · **debasishg.github.io** · **OneUptime** (2026-01-25) · **Pi Stack** (2026-06-22) | Consolidated integration binaries (build time **3×**, artifact size **5×** — direction and mechanism only, **not** a predicted figure for this workspace); "pin for a binary you control"; feature-unification leakage under resolver v2; dyn async in practice; the `thiserror`/`anyhow` split — adopted as a **house convention on two practitioner blogs and no first-party source**, and not presented as a standard | §3.8.1, §9.3, §9.4 |
| *State of Mutation Testing at Google*, ICSE-SEIP 2018 — **named as an unverified provenance candidate only** | The possible origin of "median one live mutant per changelist" and "70% of high-priority bugs". Both arXiv PDF mirrors returned undecodable content, so **neither figure is quoted anywhere in this document**; the verification gate rests on **F11's own measurement**. Discharged as **R-MUT** | §10.4 |
